At rest, $D_s=0$, and at the first boundary after it $R_T=0$: the whole fund flows to the reviewers as under 1.3.0.

**Reading — how a probationer's false approval reached the customer *(1.5.0)*.** A reading, not a rule, the drivers'. For every probationer's false approval that vests, the drivers record **the route**: the pinned audit **upheld** it (the auditor missed), or the audit **rejected** it and **the ruling overturned** the rejection (the ruling erred — a sixth of the leak at drift in run 6's ruling sweep). For the first route they record **the auditor's kind at the time**: a **troop**; a **member never suspended** in the run; a **member suspended at a later tick** than the audit — inside its detection lag; a **member readmitted before** the audit. Attribution is after the run, since a record that later fails is known only at the end.

**Rule — inspection first; a delivery carries its inspection *(1.6.0)*.** In every summons pass, **review-seat summonses are processed before delivery summonses**, oldest clock first within each. A troop delivery is summoned only when **the fund's unlocked balance plus $\Omega$'s available advance** can carry **its own capital and the capital of two review seats** — $c^D_T+G+2\,(c_T+G^V)$, 36 at the defaults — the initial review it will need and the audit it will probably draw. It is a check, not a hold: the advance actually drawn is the delivery's own shortfall, the review seats are not earmarked, the order of the pass funds them first when they come, and the demand stock $D_s$ keeps the delivery's own capital. The state never starts work it cannot get inspected.

**Rule — the reserve over two periods *(1.6.0; retired 2.0.0)*.** $R_T$ is the peak troop demand $D_s$ over **the current and the previous period** (the 1.4.0 engine's reading, Appendix I); the one-period reset of 1.5.0 is withdrawn — the boundary no longer pays out an accumulated balance in one batch when the in-flight cohort happens to have just vested. With $\Omega$ backing the fund, the reserve's remaining job is to decide when the fund repays $\Omega$ and when it pays uplift — since 1.7.0, when the troop account's surplus flows to $\Omega$ — not whether a troop is summoned.

**Rule — minimization is a rule, and a reading.** No troop persists: one seat, one summons, released at completion, nothing carried over; a turn vacated in the reserve is refilled by kind from members and probationers before any troop is summoned; a troop is summoned only for a seat or an award that has waited its window. The History carries, per period: **troop-ticks** (review seats and deliveries separately), **troop shortfall** (seats and awards no troop could be summoned for, and the ticks cases held for want of one), **cases held for the market's audit** (count and age), the fund's balance, reserve and advance, and since 1.5.0 the period's peak demand with its refused component and the demand at its last seating. In an honest population at rest every one of these reads zero; a nonzero reading is a stress gauge, like the dial, not a feature. There is nothing to size and nothing to retract, because nothing persists: the number of troops at any tick *is* the shortfall at that tick, after the market has had its chance.

**Consequence.** The state's reach is its treasury: what the dial has levied, what its own troops have earned, and a bounded float — never more. Stress raises $m_t$, $m_t$ raises the surcharge, the surcharge pays for the force that answers the stress; as the stress passes, troop usage falls, $R_T$ follows it down at the next period's end, and the money returns to the reviewers. The dial sizes the army without a second dial. ELEF's conflict of interest — troops earn the fund a fee and a price — is answered structurally: the window, members-first, cost at summons, and no route from the fund to $\Omega$. The state does not profit from scarcity; the market does.

**Hypotheses (1.4.0).** *The audit leak closes:* a probationer's every approval is audited, by a member or a troop; its false approvals fall to the audited residual. *The slowness is supplied:* at fixed splits vesting recovers toward the 1.2.0 control with troops delivering what members could not — about a third of tasks at 60/40, two thirds at 80/20 — and the state's share reads how captured the market is. *Minimization under adaptation:* troop-ticks fall to zero as the stamper share falls, close to the tick at which the population is honest. *No crowding out* — **restated by 1.5.0 after run 6:** the honest keep their income; its composition shifts. A troop takes only what the market left, but what it delivers the market inspects — a probationer's initial review, a member's pinned audit — so as the state's share rises, members spend more of their time on duty and less delivering; their wealth rises by the uplift, their awards fall, bounded by the duty cap, which keeps at least half of them free. A member whose award count fell while the state delivered is the mechanism; a member whose award was *taken by a troop it had offered for* would be the failure, and none was observed. The stamper − careful gap keeps its sign and changes in size by the uplift. *The fund carries it:* a review seat nets about $+2$, a delivery about $+4$ in expectation, so the treasury bounds simultaneous troops through $R_T$ and $A_T$, not their number over time — **failed in run 6 under the 1.4.0 reserve** (the fund refused 4,800–8,800 award summonses a run while paying 6,400–12,700 in uplift; $A_T$ was not the binding constraint), answered by 1.5.0's rule above. *The state's work is seen:* cases held for the market's audit are few and short-lived wherever members are on duty. *Open, deliberately:* whether troop usage should feed the dial; whether the duty cap should loosen now that the floor is the state rather than "whoever is free".

**Hypotheses (1.5.0).** *The fund carries the need* — **failed in run 7**: refusals rose, deliveries did not, vesting and uplift were identical to 1.4.0, because the demand stock counted a never-expiring queue and the reviewing seat, not the treasury, was the binding capacity (Appendix K). As written: refused award summonses fall from thousands a run to a ramp of a few tens of ticks and then near zero at 50–70%; the state's share rises to the unmet demand — at 60/40 from 27% toward the third of tasks the four free members cannot deliver — and vesting with it; uplift is zero while the fund accumulates toward a reserve of a few hundred and higher than run 6's once it serves the backlog, paid late in each stressed period, not never. *$w_A$ begins to matter:* with the fund sized to need the award window is the knob it was meant to be — shorter, more troops and faster service; longer, fewer and a longer wait. *Minimization still holds:* troops reach zero as the population turns honest, the reserve falls to zero one period after the last troop, the convergence tick is unchanged within noise. *The auditor's lag:* under drift most probationers' false approvals travel the upheld route and are audited by members suspended later than the audit; under fixed splits the upheld route with the auditor's $\varepsilon$ miss dominates and the overturned route is a sixth or less. If the lag dominates, a later revision may ask whether a pinned approval's auditor should itself have a *determined* record; that is not proposed. *Limit, restated:* a fund sized to need can grow; the state's share in a captured market will be larger than under 1.4.0, and the text accepts that — the share is the reading of the capture, and the market comes first at every seat.

**Hypotheses (1.6.0; run 8, Appendix L: the seat unbound the queue — 94 open cases at 60/40 — and on time came out inverted, 0.90 at 80/20 and 0.40–0.60 at 50–60% where $\Omega$'s liability reached 20,000–26,000 and plants went unfunded; $w_A$ failed again; the auditor's record held in full; the two-thirds cap fixed the seat at 60/40; minimization held for every flow and not for the liability stock).** *The seat unbinds the queue, not the rate:* three tasks a tick at $b=0.2$ vest at most $2.4$ a tick plus the false approvals — a ceiling the 1.2.0 control is at and run 7 within a few percent of; the seat caps the queue (~300 open cases at 60/40) and the latency of each. So reviews waiting fall from 7,300–11,400 case-ticks a run to a few hundred, the open-case queue to the in-flight count, vesting by the backlog cleared toward the ceiling, and the state's share to the unmet delivery demand. *On time, from the second period:* at 50–70% the on-time share exceeds 90% at $T_s=8$ and rebates are small; at 80/20 it falls and rebates accrue because $\Omega$'s balance, not policy, is the limit — $\Omega$'s minimum above the plant reserve reaches zero at $\Omega_0=300$ and recovers at 1,000, plants unfunded reading zero in both. *$w_A$ is a knob now.* *The auditor's record:* at fixed splits the upheld route's lagging-member kind falls from 55–75% to near zero; under drift it falls little. *The ramp, which every run is:* panels are the state's until records determine (100–200 ticks), $\Omega$'s advance drawn hard and repaid, the state's share of panels falling toward its steady value by the second period. *The cap decides the market's shape:* at $\lceil E_t/2\rceil$ the honest deliver and inspect in equal measure, at $E_t$ they inspect only and the state delivers everything the stampers cannot; honesty unchanged; a product decision with the numbers beside it. *Minimization still holds:* troops, advances, lateness and rebates reach zero as the population turns honest. *Limit:* a market the state can always carry is a market that need not grow; the price signal that would make it grow is §18.1 question 2, now the next question.

**Hypotheses (1.7.0).** *$\Omega$ is solvent at 60/40, and the plants are its sharp edge:* with the troop surplus in $\Omega$ and the cap at two thirds, the liability at 60/40 falls from 19,778 to a few hundred and is paid within the run, $\Omega$'s minimum stays above the plant reserve, the on-time share is at or above 0.90 — and plants unfunded read zero, which the arithmetic says is close: run 8's two-thirds cell had about 7,800 of need (a liability of 3,700, 272 unfunded plants at about 15 each) against a troop net of about 6,200, plus what the smaller reserve releases; it may hold on the liability and fail on the plants by a thousand or two, and $\omega_0=10\%$ closes that gap. *50/50 is the open question:* the liability falls by the cap's effect and the troop surplus against a plant bill the cap raises there too; whether what remains is paid within the run decides whether 1.8.0 is the cannot-serve protocol — the text does not predict it. *The state does not prefer troops:* the state's share is unchanged from run 8's two-thirds cells within noise, since the summons rules are unchanged, read beside the refusals — a rise matched by a fall in refusals for funds is capacity, a rise without one would be what the guards exist to catch; $\Omega$'s income by source shows the troop surplus as a line, not a trend. *The honest are a little poorer and still far ahead:* uplift paid falls by the troops' former share, about two fifths at 60/40 (11,900 → 6,900 a run; a careful actor's uplift 1,290 → 750); the stamper − careful gap keeps its sign at every split; honesty is unchanged. *The stock clears:* in the adaptive run $\Omega$'s liability is paid down to zero. *The take:* with $r_s$ pinned, $\omega_0=10\%$ doubles $\Omega$'s base income and the clock is carried at 50–60% on fees alone; at 15% $\Omega$ accumulates — ELEF's price question. *Limit, restated:* the state now earns from scarcity; the guards are what keep that from becoming a motive, and the reading is what shows if they fail.

**Consequence.** Under this rule the reserve's honesty is a controlled variable with an uncapturable sensor and an actuator that removes negligent reviewers through a channel they cannot vote on. The honest regime gains margin; the captured regime is detected and acted on from inside the loop. What capture becomes instead is a *shortage*: in a stamper-majority market the reserve runs short of eligible actors, reviews wait, and the eligibility shortfall is on the record — the loop stays honest and slow rather than captured and fast, and says which. What the dial does not do is make negligence impossible: it makes it unprofitable and short-lived.

**Hypotheses (1.2.0).** *The contest, by channel:* careless review loses to careful review through money (about $\pi\beta_P(\bar f+G^V)\approx0.6$ per review at the defaults, roughly 120 per actor over 1,000 ticks at run 3's volumes — the scale of the 50/50 gap under appeals alone, +200, and it reverses it), through reliability (plant failures enter $\rho$: a stamper adds about 7.5 overturns per 100 outcomes at $\pi=0.1$, a careful reviewer about 0.5, reversing run 3's 0.973 against 0.953 and with it the award shares that made the 10/90 gaps), and through the seat (a stamper leaves the reserve within about $n_{\min}/(\pi\beta_P)\approx50$ reviews). The dependence on the requester's dispute rate and the actor's appeal rate, which decided the contest under 1.1.0, is removed as a *necessary* condition; both remain welcome. *Capture:* a stamper majority cannot persist in the reserve; the tipping point of run 3 (between 50/50 and 10/90) moves to "nowhere" for honesty and reappears as a capacity cliff the market must answer by recruiting reviewers. *Cost:* at $m=1$ plants cost $\Omega$ about one careful inspection's fee and expenses per plant (a stamper's failed plant costs $\Omega$ nothing but the refunded pots), about 2 credits a tick at 3 tasks a tick, against a service-fee income of 3 a tick; at $m_{\max}$ the floating fee covers four times the plants — §18.1 question 3 restated. *Camouflage:* a stamper that rejects a random fraction of work to look careful still approves bad plants at its approval rate and is caught in proportion; camouflage buys time, not safety.

---

## 13. The record

**Rule.** There is one authoritative record: an append-only, hash-chained history of every admitted action with its material parameters — postings, awards, submissions, verdicts, responses, challenges with their grounds, audit assignments, rulings, cancellations, withdrawals, acceptances, corrections, rotations, and every ledger event. The ledger's event list and each case's log are working copies, each entry carrying its commitment position, and the per-tick checks verify the chain **and** that each copy holds exactly its committed entries in order. Changes are appended, never rewritten; two disagreeing assessments can coexist in an integrity-valid history.

**Limit.** The chain is tamper-evident within the process. An external commitment of its head — the trusted recording authority — is bracketed. Integrity is not truth: a verified chain establishes that an assessment was made on identified evidence and that the procedure accepted an outcome, not that the outcome is correct.

---

## 14. The reviewer's view: the information contract

**Rule.** A reviewer receives the information its question requires and the set of responses it may give, and nothing else about the case — **and the view stays blind to the actor** *(ruled 2.2.0, Appendix O)*: a reviewer judges work, not workers. Run 14's state-rejecter cell — a reviewer that could tell the state's work and rejected it — drove $b_T$ to the two-thirds limit and made the state most of the market at a loss; it is recorded as the price of a leak and struck from the red team as impossible by construction. Whether a reviewer should know it is inspecting a plant is the same question with the same answer.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| the submission | result, evidence, time of submission, whether it has been resubmitted — **not the actor** (1.2.0) |
| the terms | deadline, bond, cap, whether the case is nested |
| `question` | `fulfillment`, `appeal`, or `dispute` |
| `responses_available` | whether $\mathsf P$ and $\bot$ may be answered (§3.3) |
| for an appeal or dispute | the assessment under appeal and the challenger's grounds |

Other reviewers' identities and unrelated judgments are not supplied; since 1.2.0 the submission's actor is not either. The reviewer judges the work, not the worker: it never needed the actor's name to decide whether the request was met, and not having it closes a collusion channel — a reviewer can no longer recognise a friend's work — and lets a plant's view carry nothing a case's does not (§7.6). The orchestrator still knows the actor; so does the record. **The question and the permitted responses may reveal the stage or kind of assignment; audit-role indistinguishability is not guaranteed.** In particular, a fulfillment question on an unrevised submission with $\mathsf P$ and $\bot$ unavailable is an audit, and recognizing it reveals that a prior approval exists — not what it said or who gave it. This is the trade-off chosen: the view states the admissible answers truthfully, so identical views admit identical answers and no refusal reveals anything the view did not say; in exchange the role is inferable. Whether role secrecy is required is a design question; this baseline answers no, because the deterrents an auditor faces — bond, audit rate, reliability — do not depend on it.

---

## 15. The human

### 15.1 The battery

**Rule.** Human root requests power the economy: they are the only source of value that is not emission and the only source of judgment that is not a model's. Actor-to-actor requests are current in the circuit. With no human request pending and no in-flight tree, the correct state is idle.

### 15.2 The plug: rulings and corrections

**Rule.** Two different things are called ruling, and the word is kept for both because the record uses it for both. The **orchestrator rules on procedural disagreements**: a split between appeal or dispute reviewers (§8.4), inside the ordinary procedure. **Only a human may issue a human override** — the ruling of §15.2 — which bypasses the economy's machinery, and the only ground for it is that a human's judgment is not a model's opinion of another model. A delegate holds no override authority; it accepts or disputes through the ordinary procedure like any requester. Where this document says "a human ruling" or "a ruling before vesting" it means the override. Whether a particular human is authorized over a particular request is bracketed; in this baseline every human is.

**Rule.** A human may interrupt a case at any point, and every interruption is a recorded human event: dispute an approval, request an audit, **rule**, or freeze awards. Silence inside the window is protocol-default acceptance.

**A ruling before vesting** is the ruling. It is validated whole — the human, the outcome, that there is a result to rule on, and for a $\bot$ the disposition and its entailment — then: every unfinished assignment is voided without fault; every completed verdict that contradicts the ruling is overturned with the standard consequences, a reviewer's or a requester-actor's rejection alike; a ruling that accepts a nested result ends the rejection obligation whether or not the requester-actor had judged; and the case is settled — an open challenge by the ruling, otherwise directly. No audit or dispute may be opened afterwards. Contradiction is defined once: $\mathsf F$ contradicts $\mathsf N$; $\mathsf N$ and $\mathsf P$ contradict $\mathsf F$; a $\bot$ is contradicted only by a rejected objection; an appeal reviewer of a $\bot$ is contradicted on either of its two answers.

**A correction after settlement** moves no money. It changes the recorded outcome and grade, records a failed outcome for the actor and for every settled assessment it contradicts — by the complete assessment, two answers where there were two — and may create a corrective obligation: a new request, funded by the human, to repair the result. A review that has not yet reached its payout batch when the correction arrives is treated as unsettled: contradicted, it is overturned.

### 15.3 Label grades

**Rule.** Every settled case records its outcome and the grade of the event that **established** it, by that event's **author**:

| Grade | Established by |
|---|---|
| human-explicit | a human's acceptance, winning dispute, or ruling |
| delegate-explicit | a delegate's acceptance or winning dispute |
| procedural | a challenge, an audit, or a ruling on a split, with no matching root judgment |
| protocol-default | one initial verdict, no challenge, no audit, silence |
| plant | the orchestrator's planted work (§7.6): truth known by construction, never promoted *(1.2.0)* |

A root judgment that disagrees with the settled outcome — a dispute that lost — is recorded as an assessment beside a procedural outcome; the disagreement is the learning signal. Nested cases are always procedural. Nothing promotes between grades, and no label at any grade is read as "correct" rather than "accepted."

---

## 16. Permitted and forbidden operations

**Permitted.** Post a root request (human or delegate; since 2.0.0 any requester with a wallet, locking its all-in maximum at posting). Raise the ceiling of one's own unawarded request (2.0.0). Arrive as a requester with a wallet; leave once one's requests are served out (2.0.0). Post a nested request (a committed actor). Withdraw an unawarded request. Award within $\beta$. Spend on a case (its committed actor, while executing). Submit by the deadline. Resubmit within the window after choosing to revise or supply evidence. Assign an initial review, an appeal review, an audit; replace a withdrawn position; waive a vacated audit. Complete a review with an admissible answer. Respond to a verdict within the window. Appeal an $\mathsf N$ or a $\bot$ with text grounds. Dispute a pending $\mathsf F$ within the window with text grounds. Rule on a split. Judge a nested result. Cancel a nested request before its submission. Withdraw for pool exhaustion within the stated bounds. Rule (a human), before or after settlement. Accept explicitly (a requester). Volunteer for or leave duty. Create a plant (the orchestrator, within $\Omega$'s unlocked balance; 1.2.0 — since 2.1.0 its pots minted by the bank, never waiting for $\Omega$). Revise a plant (the orchestrator's instrument, after $\mathsf P$ or $\bot$, the resubmitted truth its draw sealed as a second marker; 2.8.0); appeal, dispute and audit a plant as any case (2.8.0). Arrive as an actor (the orchestrator admits a fresh identity as a probationer with a float the bank lends — the arrival loan, repaid from the name's income first; 2.3.0, 2.7.0). Depart (an actor with no live obligation to or from it — no commitment, no open or unsettled review, no lock, no claim on $\Omega$; an unrepaid loan is no bar; its unlocked wallet repays its arrival loan first and the remainder leaves $M$; 2.3.0, 2.7.0). Award to a member (1.3.0). Record an audit roll (the orchestrator; 1.4.0). Summon a troop for a seat or an award that has waited its window (the engine, within the fund's carrying capacity; 1.4.0 — since 2.1.0 on $\Omega$'s balance or the bank's loan: a seat always, a delivery while the debt is within the wall). Mark a request refused for capacity or for credit, and tell it (the engine; 2.1.0). Withdraw an unawarded request at its mark (a requester; 2.1.0 — owed nothing). Since 2.9.0 the orchestrator posts a planted approval beside the dial's plants (`post_plant(truth, approval)`), seats and gives its own verdict on it (`plant_seat`, `plant_verdict` — a system review under a plant reviewer alias) and files the planted lies through the plant actor's appeal and the plant requester's dispute, by the orchestrator's road; no participant may do anything it could not do under 2.8.0.

**Forbidden, and refused without effect.** A bid above $\beta$ or a non-positive delivery time. A posting whose all-in maximum the requester cannot lock (2.0.0). Any creation by $\Omega$ (2.1.0: the bank alone creates — by rule, or as a loan tied to a summons the rules called for; a run's declared deficit shock is the bank's `mint_scenario`, by its note). A loan for a loss, a rebate, or any payment that is not a summons; a loan for a delivery while the debt is above the wall; a refusal of a seat for credit (2.1.0). A credit minted for a plant that reaches $\Omega$ (2.1.0). A plant created submitted; a submission made for a plant's actor; a fee paid by a plant; a plant's price in $\bar B$ or $P_t$; two plants under one name (2.6.0). An award of any kind to a probationer, an arrival's included; a panel seat to an arrival before admission; an arrival under an identity that exists or existed; a departure with any obligation live; a troop summoned to deliver a nested request; a nested request posted without its pots under the parent's all-in (2.3.0). A delivery summoned for a request whose ceiling is below the state's price $p_T$; a troop's delivery priced below the state's reservation; a parameter set with $F_0<G^V$ (2.4.0). A name's income kept while its arrival loan stands; a return taken for the loan; a loan forgiven while the name lives; a departure refused for an unrepaid loan (2.7.0). A second active assignment. A review by the case's actor, requester, prior or withdrawn reviewer, or an unfunded reviewer. $\mathsf P$ or $\bot$ outside the first review of an unrevised submission. An appeal of a $\mathsf P$; an appeal of a $\bot$ without the objection's disposition; an upheld disposition with $\mathsf F$. A second challenge round. A dispute or audit after a human ruling. A ruling by a delegate. A ruling on a case with no result. Spending attributed to another actor's case or to a settled case. Non-text grounds. A kill fee the requester-actor cannot fund; a cancellation after submission; a cancellation at or after the nested request's deadline (2.5.0). A submission while a nested request is short of its milestone. Any challenge, ruling, acceptance or response on a plant; an assignment to an actor ineligible for the tier by plant record (1.2.0). An award to an actor suspended from the market or on probation; a panel seat to a probationer (1.3.0). An award or an assignment to a troop other than by summons; a troop on a plant; a troop auditing a troop reviewer's approval of a troop's delivery; a response, appeal, dispute or nested request by a troop; a cost reported by a troop at completion; a waiver of the obligation on the state's own work (1.4.0). Any amount that is negative or not finite. Any action whose preconditions fail — nothing partial is ever kept.

**Not offered in this baseline.** Reopening an approved case through an audit (Appendix B). Clawback after vesting. Appeal against appeal reviewers. A second revision. Multiple initial reviewers at root.

---

## 17. Invariants

Thirty-six, checked after every tick. A failure is a bug in the engine or a contradiction in this document, never a property of a strategy.

| | Property |
|---|---|
| I1 | Every credit is in a wallet, a case pool, or $\Omega$; their sum is $M$; and $M$ equals mints minus burns reconstructed from the record Amended (2.0.0): conservation over the kinds — issuance, emission and creation in; bond and guarantee burns, resource spend, retirement and departure out. Amended (2.1.0): the sources are the bank's — `issuance`, `mint_emission`, `mint_plant`, `loan`, `mint_scenario`; the sinks `burn_bond`, `purchase`, `repayment`, `retire_plant`, `departure`; `creation` and `retirement` retired. Amended (2.3.0): `arrival` — an arrival's float, the bank's — among the sources; `departure` covers actors and requesters. Amended (2.7.0): `arrival` is the bank's loan to a name; `arrival_repayment` — repaid from income, recovered at departure — among the sinks; `departure` an actor's wallet net of the recovery |
| I2 | No balance, pool, or reserve is negative or non-finite |
| I3 | Only known event kinds, finite non-negative amounts; the only unbacked mint is emission; the only burns are bond forfeiture and resource purchase Amended (2.0.0): the unbacked sources are issuance, emission and creation; the sinks bond and guarantee burns, resource spend, retirement and departure; no backing, no value-neutral purchase. Amended (2.1.0): the bank's five kinds in, its two out; a loan carries its summons's case and kind, a plant's mint reaches only the plant's wallets, a repayment leaves only $\Omega$ |
| I4 | A court fee is reimbursed at most once, never above the bond; $G^V\ge D$ per case |
| I5 | No case vests with an open item, an unresolved challenge, an open review, or before its window |
| I6 | An audit is assigned no earlier than the verdict it audits |
| I7 | A parent submits only after every nested request reached its milestone |
| I8 | Review positions per case $\le V_{\max}$ |
| I9 | The reserve holds $\min(X_t,\ \text{eligible})$ — since 1.6.0 at least the reviews waiting, by kind, whenever eligible free actors exist to seat (member seats at least the panel seats waiting, within the cap): a reserve short of its target is admissible only when no eligible free actor was left to seat, and the gap is counted (1.2.0; was "holds $X$"). Amended (1.4.0): the eligibility shortfall counts seats unfilled by the market after seating; troop seats are counted separately as troop-ticks, and seats no troop could take as troop shortfall |
| I10 | No adjudication moves money after vesting; after it only obligations established before it (since 1.6.0 including a rebate $\Omega$ owed at the clock's stop and carried as its liability; since 2.2.0 a claim settled at the period's close, by its note), a nested case's draws on its root's pots (1.1.0–2.2.0; since 2.3.0 only under the `root` control), and the tree's closure — since 2.3.0 the case's own closure, a node's for a nested case (the ruling) — move money on account of the case |
| I11 | Full forfeiture and pool-exhaustion forfeiture are exclusive |
| I12 | Nominals — $D,K,G^V$ and both pots — do not change after the first lock; $G$ not after award; an unawarded nested case's rejection bond equals its frozen $G^V$ |
| I13 | Every settled case has exactly one grade, and the record justifies it by the establishing event's author; the plant grade belongs to plants and only to them |
| I14 | One active assignment per actor |
| I15 | Every wallet's $L$ is the sum of its lock records; every live obligation has a record of exactly its size; no orphan records |
| I16 | The history chain verifies, and every working copy holds exactly its committed entries, in order |
| I17 | Reimbursement fields are non-negative and within the cap; every claim on $\Omega$ names a review whose pending balance covers it; no ended nested case carries a rejection bond |
| I18 | No audit is open or assigned after a human ruling |
| I19 | The pots (1.1.0; 2.3.0 **for every case** — the ruling): for every case with pots of its own, fees paid from the fee pot $\le\varphi^F$; fee money reached expenses only at the case's closure — a node's when it and everything under it have settled, the tree's for the root — and only up to the pot's remainder after every fee; no fee was paid from $\varphi^E$; a refund occurs only when the case is closed, and a closed case has settled with both pots empty |
| I20 | Plants are self-adjudicated (1.2.0; rewritten per seat 2.8.0): a plant's outcome is set only by its known truth — until 2.8.0 no audit, appeal, dispute or ruling was ever opened on it; since 2.8.0 every $\mathsf F$ or $\mathsf N$ on a plant at any seat is adjudicated by the truth then in force, stood or failure, each reviewer once, no ruling or explicit acceptance is recorded on it and its challenge resolves by truth, no court fee on it is reimbursed; the markers (both, after a revision) are in the History sealed and revealed only at closure, the reveal hashing to each commitment; a good plant settles vested, a bad or spent one final $\mathsf N$. (That a plant's view is indistinguishable is a property of the view function — it reads no actor and no marker — asserted by a test, not a per-tick predicate; since 2.6.0 the test covers everything a member observes and the tell-readers are its instrument, §7.6) Since 2.9.0 a done F or N on a plant without adjudication fails the invariant unless its outcome is *planted* — the orchestrator's own verdict on a planted approval's initial seat, under a plant reviewer alias; a troop on a plant still fails it. |
| I21 | The dial is bounded and the reserve is sized (1.2.0): $1\le m_t\le m_{\max}$; each step within $[0.9,2]$; $\pi_t\le\pi_{\max}$; $X_t\ge V_{\max}+1$; no assignment was made to an actor ineligible for its tier at the time; no actor remains on duty past the completion of its active review once duty-ineligible; the eligibility and plant shortfalls are counted. Amended (1.3.0): the plant capital cap is on $\varphi^E+\varphi^F$; a plant has no escrow and no guarantee lock — I15 exempts a plant's guarantee, I12 keeps its $B$ and $G$ as nominals that bind nothing |
| I22 | Membership (1.3.0; amended 1.6.0 — once the world's first plant is adjudicated, no undetermined member holds an audit, appeal or dispute seat either): no award is made to an actor that is suspended or on probation; no probationer holds an audit, appeal or dispute seat; every probationer's applied audit odds are 1; a readmission happens only with a passing trailing record and at least $n_{\min}$ outcomes since the suspension began. Amended (1.4.0): an award to a troop is made only by summons under §12.3 |
| I23 | The load and the fund (1.3.0): no member is seated while on-duty members $\ge\lceil E_t/2\rceil$ (as §12.1 bounds the cap); a member above the cap holds no assignment past the completion of its active review; on-duty members $\ge\min(V_{\max}-1,E_t)$ whenever that many free, fundable members exist to seat, the gap otherwise counted toward the eligibility shortfall. Amended (1.4.0): the fund's wallet equals every recorded flow into it less every flow out (the raised tax, troop earnings and advances in; troop costs purchased, uplifts, bond burns, forfeits and repayments out — with I1; a troop's cost is a resource purchase under I3 and §4.4); its locks are the troops' bonds and guarantees and nothing else; uplift is paid only from the unlocked balance above $R_T$; the advance outstanding never exceeds $A_T$; a troop's role weight claims no uplift. Amended (1.5.0): $R_T$ is the peak troop demand of the current period — capital held plus the capital the fund-refused summonses' unfilled seats would have drawn, at the amounts the carry check refused, each case once per seating — starting at the standing demand at the period's first seating; checkable as $R_T\ge$ the capital held by live troops and $R_T\ge D_s$ at the last seating, $D_s$ and the pass's refused demand being world readings. Amended (1.6.0): no $A_T$ — $\Omega$ advances from its unlocked balance above the plant reserve in §10.4's order, liabilities older than a period first; $R_T$ over the current and previous period; member seats at least the panel seats waiting within the cap. Amended (1.7.0): two accounts — the uplift fund equals the raised tax received less the uplifts paid (no reserve, no lock, no other flow); the troop account equals every recorded flow into it (advances, fees, prices, court fees won, released locks) less every flow out (costs purchased, locks forfeited or burned, repayments, surplus paid to $\Omega$); $R_T$ is the two-period peak of capital held and the surplus flows only from the unlocked balance above it; $R_T\ge$ capital held stands and $R_T\ge D_s$ is retired with the refused component; no troop's role weight claims uplift; the cap is $\lceil 2E_t/3\rceil$; $\Omega$'s period record carries its two incomes, its own money's flows and its outflows, and plants unfunded for want of $\Omega$ is a reading the invariant watches. Amended (2.0.0): the uplift fund's identity stands; the troops' wallet is $\Omega$ and the reserve, the advance and the order are retired — I28 holds the deficit |
| I24 | Troops (1.4.0; amended 1.6.0 — review-seat summonses precede delivery summonses in a pass; a delivery is summoned only when the fund plus $\Omega$'s available advance can carry $c^D_T+G+2(c_T+G^V)$ and the advance drawn is its own shortfall; a panel seat is held only by a determined passing member or a troop once the first plant is adjudicated; 1.7.0 — the troop account is the troops' wallet in every flow, and the clause "nothing a troop earns reaches $\Omega$ except by repayment" is struck from the text — it was never this invariant's predicate, the engine enforced it through the fund's payout order): a troop holds exactly one seat from summons to release and no seat thereafter; a troop is not an actor — no $\rho$, $\kappa$, membership state or plant record — and claims no emission or uplift; no troop reviews a plant or acts in one; a troop is summoned only by the engine, only for a seat or award unfilled for its window after the tick's seating, and only when the fund (with the advance) can carry its cost and bond; no troop takes the audit of a troop reviewer's approval of a troop's delivery; no troop appeals, disputes, revises or delegates; a troop's delivery is at $\bar B$ and only where $\beta\ge\bar B$. Amended (2.0.0): a seat holds one place of $N_T$, a delivery two until its initial review is assigned and one after; the places held never exceed $N_T$; a troop's delivery cost is $\bar B$. Amended (2.1.0): the burn is the expected cost $\max(0,(1-b_T)\bar B-b_TG)$ at the summons, the price $\bar B$ unchanged |
| I25 | The obligation (1.4.0): a case with a rolled audit outstanding does not vest — without exception; a troop reviewer's approval of a troop's delivery carries audit odds 1, its obligation is recorded by the engine at the verdict, and it vests only after a member's audit completed. (A pinned reviewer's approval is audited because the orchestrator rolls at odds 1 and records the roll — the driver's conformance under §18.2, which the engine then holds) |
| I26 | Service (1.6.0; 1.7.0 names the third stop): every root request with $\beta\ge\bar B$ carries a running clock from posting until its first verdict, its final without one, or a vesting without an initial verdict (a human's override), stopped once; waiting items are served oldest-first in every summons pass (a younger case summoned after an older of the same kind was refused for funds is the violation); the rebate owed equals $\min(r_s\cdot\text{ticks late},\,B)$, settled when the clock stops from $\Omega$ or recorded as $\Omega$'s liability, zero for a request withdrawn before award; rebates paid are $\Omega\to$ requester transfers (I1 covers them), a liability is a counter on $\Omega$, and paid plus outstanding equals owed — a bookkeeping identity over that counter, bounded by $B$ times the open requests. Amended (2.0.0): the rebate is paid at the stop, $\Omega$ creating if short, and no liability is made; a younger case summoned after an older one was refused for capacity is the same violation. Amended (2.1.0): the rebate is paid at the stop from $\Omega$'s balance, else it is $\Omega$'s liability again, paid from income after the debt, never created; a younger case summoned after an older one was refused for credit is the same violation. Amended (2.2.0): the claim is recorded at the stop with $r_s$ indexed at the first lock when stated, and claims $=$ paid $+$ forgone $+$ this period's pending claims |
| I27 | Price and indexing (2.0.0): $P_t$ is the trailing mean awarded price on $\bar B$'s window, $P_0$ before the first award, and the ledger indexes by $P_t/P_0$; every nominal of a case priced under 2.0.0 equals its base times the index at the case's first lock, frozen (§4.5); $\bar B$ counts members' awards only — the period's award count never exceeds its price count; effort costs are not the engine's to index |
| I28 | The bank (2.1.0, rewritten; 2.0.0: the deficit — $\Omega$ creates only for a payment the rules require and only when its unlocked balance cannot meet it; while the deficit stands $\Omega$'s unlocked balance is zero at the tick's end; deficit outstanding $=$ created $-$ retired $\ge0$, its age recorded — the control): $\Omega$ never creates; the bank creates only by §10.4's mintings and loans; a loan is tied to a summons the rules called for and is repaid from that summons's own return before $\Omega$ receives any of it; what is lent is outstanding, repaid or debt and nothing else — lent $=$ repaid $+$ outstanding $+$ debt; debt is the sum of settled summonses' unrepaid loans and changes only at a settlement or a repayment; nothing is created for a rebate; nothing minted for a plant reaches $\Omega$; the scenario deficit shock is the bank's `mint_scenario`, exempt by its note |
| I29 | The posting lock (2.0.0): a root request holds, from posting to award, a lock of its all-in maximum $\beta(1+\omega_0 m)+\varphi^E+\varphi^F$ at its posting index and dial, raised with its ceiling; a request that cannot lock is refused whole; after the award or a withdrawal nothing of it remains |
| I30 | The bank's identity (2.1.0; amended 2.2.0 — "or liabilities $>0$" is struck, there is no stock; the pool, being locked, is not $\Omega$'s unlocked balance and I15 reconciles it): while debt $>0$, $\Omega$'s unlocked balance is zero at the start of the summons pass and at the tick's end (the rule orders every inflow at once; these are its checks); debt $\le\Delta_{\max}$ whenever a loan for a delivery is made, and a seat's loan is never refused; loans outstanding $=\sum$ open summonses' loans — a delivery's until its release, a seat's until its review settles; the peaks are at least the current values |
| I31 | The index per period (2.1.0): $P_t$ is what the bank published at the last period boundary — the closed period's mean awarded price, carried if none, $P_0$ before the first — and changes only there; within a period every `index()` reading is equal |
| I32 | The pool (2.2.0): every **fee** inflow to $\Omega$ after the debt — base fees, the troops' review fees, court fees a troop wins — accrues to the current period's pool, a lock on $\Omega$'s balance of exactly that amount; the returns of capital and a delivery's surplus do not; the pool is untouched until the period's close; at the close, paid $=\min(\text{claims},\text{pool})$ distributed pro rata over the claims stopped in the period, forgone $=\text{claims}-\text{paid}$, and the remainder is released to $\Omega$'s unlocked balance; nothing is created for a rebate, no liability stock exists, and nothing is owed across a close. Amended (2.3.0): the pool may pay a §4.3 reviewer advance during the period, the lock falling with it; the close settles the claims from the pool net of advances |
| I33 | Entry through inspection (2.3.0): an arrival holds no award — root or nested — and no panel seat until its trailing record passes the thresholds with $\ge n_{\min}$ bad plants adjudicated; it is never a member before admission (a probationer, or suspended); its audit odds read 1 until then; its float was lent once (2.7.0; minted, 2.3.0–2.6.1), at arrival, at the arrival index, and the parameters satisfy $F_0\ge G^V$ (2.4.0); the public view exposes nothing per actor |
| I34 | A clean departure (2.3.0): `depart` is refused while any obligation to or from the actor is live — a commitment, an open or unsettled review, a lock, a claim on $\Omega$; on departure the actor leaves duty, its unlocked wallet leaves $M$ as `departure`, and its identity never reappears. Amended (2.7.0): the unlocked wallet repays the name's arrival loan first and the remainder leaves as `departure`; an unrepaid loan does not refuse the departure |
| I35 | The summons is for roots (2.3.0): no troop is summoned to *deliver* a nested request; seats are as on roots |
| I36 | The pots grow with the tree (2.3.0): under the parent's pots every awarded nested case carries its own two pots, posted by its requester at its posting index as the defaults index, and its reviews never draw on its root's deposit |
| I37 | The state's reservation (2.4.0): the state's price is $p_T=\max(\bar B,\underline{B})$; no delivery is summoned for a request whose ceiling is below $p_T$, and the award pass counts only such requests against the free members; a troop's delivery is priced at $p_T$ and the burn is §7.7's formula at $p_T$; a request with $\beta<\underline{B}$ carries the mark from its posting and its clock does not run while it holds it; $\underline{B}$ is nominal and public |
| I38 | The missed child (2.5.0): no cancelled nested case closed at or after its deadline; every committed nested case past its deadline is final $\mathsf N$ with its guarantee forfeited to the parent (the lapse's own check, stated); the parent may submit from the next step |
| I39 | The plant's disguise (2.6.0; amended 2.8.0 — at every seat): a plant is posted (its id minted then, in sequence with the roots around it) and never built submitted; it is awarded to a fresh plant-actor name on the market's report or at $p_T$ past $w_A$, its deadline the report's $\tau$ from the award; it is submitted at its drawn tick within $(t_{\text{award}},\ \text{deadline}]$ and by nobody else (a revised plant's resubmission later); its requester and actor names are fresh per plant and alias the two plant wallets; it pays no fee, its clock never runs, it is never a troop's and holds no place in $N_T$; its marks are a root's (capacity, credit) |
| I40 | Opaque identifiers (2.6.0): the public identifiers are a bijection on the cases and carry no order; no rule, reading or declared tie-break orders by them; the engine, the record and the History's chain run on the creation counter |
| I41 | The arrival loan (2.7.0): for every live name that arrived after genesis (a name with an arrival tick; genesis balances are the sponsor's transfers, never `arrival`) its loan outstanding equals its float less what it has repaid from income, never below zero, and no genesis name carries one; every income inflow (§5's table) to a name with a loan outstanding is followed by its repayment before the name keeps any of it, and a repayment from income follows nothing but such an inflow; at departure the recovery is $\min(W_U,L)$, the wallet out $W_U-\min(W_U,L)$, the default $L-\min(W_U,L)$ read; lent to names $=$ repaid from income $+$ recovered at departure $+$ outstanding $+$ defaulted, every tick, outstanding the sum over the live names, its peak at or above it; a departed name's loan is zero; no name's float is forgiven while it lives. I28's identity keeps `loan` alone; the names' line stands beside it |
| I42 | The plant at every seat (2.8.0): a plant's challenge seats are opened at the step and rate a real case in its position would open them and a seat past $w_R$ is the orchestrator's own verdict, never a troop's; its audit rolls at the odds its reviewer carried at the verdict, recorded on every case at its initial verdict; the dial's clock counts initial adjudications only; a plant's court fee is destroyed at filing and no coin minted for a plant reaches $\Omega$ or a real wallet except as a reviewer's fee for a seat held — no $\Omega$ advance on a plant, no court fee to $\Omega$, no plant funds swept to $\Omega$ under the bank; the pots' remainder is destroyed at closure Since 2.9.0 across the planted approval's bond — released and destroyed, never burned — and the planted lie's fee; the plant reviewer wallet's mint and destruction read apart. |
| I43 | The planted approval (2.9.0): every planted approval is a bad plant marked at posting; from its submission to the orchestrator's verdict its initial seat is nobody's — no member seated, the summons pass passing it by; its initial verdict is F, a system review under a plant reviewer alias given in the review pass with a seating gap drawn from the real roots' and the review's own tick, a bond minted and locked, no fee, no adjudication (outcome *planted*); its audit obligation is recorded at the verdict at odds 1 under its own mark and the seat filled at a real audit seat's step — a member, or past $w_R$ the orchestrator's verdict — or discharged by a dispute's settlement; the auditor's and the third seat's verdicts are adjudicated by truth; the dial's clock and the plant record do not count the planted verdict; $\pi^A_t = \pi^A_0 m^A_t \le \pi^A_{\max}$ on the real approvals' credit. |
| I44 | The planted lie (2.9.0): a planted lying appeal is filed on a bad plant's N and a planted lying dispute on a good plant's F, each on the real challenge's path with the fee minted and destroyed at filing, the delay from the real window or the default, the grounds the population's; the panelists' verdicts are adjudicated by truth and the challenge resolved by truth; the first reviewer is not adjudicated again; the rates $\pi^{La}, \pi^{Ld}$ on the trailing period's counts within their caps, with the floor of two a period. |

---

## 18. Hypotheses, scoped runs, and what is bracketed

### 18.1 What a run must find

1. **Careful versus careless review.** Under 1.3.0, by how much do the honest end richer at each split, and through which channel — the award a non-member cannot win, the fee and its uplift, or reliability? (Run 1 answered the 1.0.1 form — careful review did not beat stamping at the defaults — and led to design revision 1; runs 2–3 the 1.1.0 form — fair only with both challengers present, blind under capture — and led to design revision 2; run 4 the 1.2.0 form — honest everywhere, but the honest minority carried the reviewing load at the ordinary fee while the excluded majority took the awards — and led to design revision 3, Appendix H.)
2. **Price pressure** (opened 2.0.0). Does $\lambda=0.2$ produce competition without starving reliable actors? Under floating prices: where do awarded prices sit between the members' cost and the ceilings at each split; does $P_t$ rise with the money supply once capacity binds and not before; what does the at-the-ceiling control cost customers and earn members, in real terms?
3. **Verification funding.** Does $\varphi^E=2K$ cover root-plus-nested expenses; how often does $\Omega$ advance; how much does it bear unrecovered; does $\omega_t$ fund plants and audits at each $m$; how large does $\Omega$'s outstanding exposure in plants get; should the pots grow with the tree? **(2.3.0: answered — they grow by one pair per node, posted by the node's requester; the 1.1.0 reading is the control.)**
4. **Emission scale.** Is $\approx1443\,E_0$ against $M_0$ a subsidy or a flood? (2.0.0: emission's real value under $P_t$, read apart from its own halving. 2.1.0: emission and the plants' minted pots together as the **cost of truth**, in real terms.)
5. **Delegation liveness.** Do trees of 2–4 nodes move under $T_{\mathrm{wait}}$, the kill fee, nested-first priority, and the rejection bond's capital cost? (Opened at last by 2.3.0: delegation under competence — the nested share by spread, the gain from trade, brokers, the parent as judge.)
6. **Reserve solvency, occupancy and eligibility.** How often does each shortfall occur; do fees restore solvency; does $X_t$ track demand without starving the market of actors?
7. **Monetary behavior** (restated 2.0.0: there is no backing and no halt state). How does $P_t$ move with issuance, the deficit and capacity; does an economy with no issuance idle, or deflate to the members' cost; where does the money go when members have nothing to buy (hoards)?
8. **Dominant strategies.** Anything cheap the rules missed.
9. **The tipping point and the alarm** (1.2.0; amended 1.3.0). Does an adaptive population reach an honest equilibrium under the gate, and in how many ticks; does the alarm ever fire in a run that began honest?
10. **Constructibility** (1.2.0; sharpened 2.8.0). What fraction of real work in a target domain admits plants — and, since 2.8.0, a plant must be constructible as a disputed or appealed assessment and, once any population revises, as a revision, not only as a submission?
11. **Throughput of a market shrunk to its honest core** (1.3.0; answered by 1.4.0; restated 1.6.0; in real terms since 2.0.0). Vesting cannot exceed the posting rate less failures — the 1.2.0 control is at that ceiling — so the yardstick is the open-case queue, reviews waiting and the on-time share against the posting rate, not vestings a tick against a review-capped control.
12. **The state's share** (1.4.0). Troop-ticks per tick at each split, its trajectory under adaptation, and the tick at which it reaches zero.
13. **The fund under troops** (1.4.0; continued 1.5.0 and 1.7.0). Balance, reserve, advance drawn, uplift paid, and where the treasury fails to carry — under 1.4.0 it failed by its own reserve (run 6); under 1.5.0, with the reserve sized to need, where it still fails, and what one period's retention costs (run 7: the demand stock counted the queue); under 1.7.0 the troop account's balance against $R_T$ as capital held, and the surplus it pays $\Omega$.
14. **How a probationer's false approval reaches a customer** (1.5.0; continued under §7.1's 1.6.0 rule; 2.2.0: the reviewer's view stays blind — the state-rejecter cell of run 14 recorded as the price of a leak). The route — the pinned audit upheld it, or rejected it and the ruling overturned — and on the upheld route the auditor's kind: a troop, a member never suspended, a member suspended later than the audit (its detection lag), a member readmitted before it. Run 7: the lagging member carried 55–75% at fixed splits and 73–80% under drift.
15. **The network rating** (1.6.0; 2.0.0 adds the price half — requests flagged below market and the stock flagged and waiting — and reads the rebates in real terms; 2.1.0: the liability stock's total and oldest age under riots; 2.2.0 restated: **claims, paid, forgone and the forgone share** under riots — the unpaid part of the state's blame). On-time share and mean lateness at each split, and what they cost $\Omega$ in rebates.
16. **ELEF's solvency** (1.6.0; continued 1.7.0 with the troop surplus as income; 2.0.0: the deficit's peak, age and clearance — $\Omega$ cannot be broke, only in debt to everyone at once; 2.1.0 restated: **loans outstanding, debt and liabilities by peak and age — not one deficit** — the wall's slack, and whether any delivery was refused for credit). $\Omega$'s minimum balance above the plant reserve under stress, against $\Omega_0$; plants unfunded for want of $\Omega$ must read zero. Run 8: failed at 50–60% with the surplus going to the reviewers.
17. **The state's income by source** (1.7.0). At each split, how much of ELEF's revenue is its own deliveries, and whether the share rises over time — read beside the refusals for funds and the state's share of work.
18. **The price level under a deficit** (2.0.0). Inflation's size and recovery: a created sum's effect on $P_t$ in proportion to its share of $M$ where capacity binds, and on hoards where it does not; real wealth by disposition through the shock; the retirement time; and under the adaptive-reservation variant, the spiral's speed. (2.1.0: **the inflation test** — where the ratchet binds the price is issuance over the strategy's quantity, $P_t\approx$ issuance$/(k\cdot F)$ with $k=0.278$ the margin rule's constant and $F$ the free members, unless a nominal ceiling binds first; issuance 5 / 10 / 20 a requester at 20 honest; real wealth by disposition should not move, since printing moves the price tag and not the work — the reason issuance is not a dial.)
19. **The capacity case** (2.0.0; 2.1.0: and the credit case; 2.4.0: the busy line's third mark — *below the state's reservation*, the state will not). Refusals for capacity under $N_T$ at each split and in the riots, the waits they cause, whether the requester was told — the one reading the price cannot answer; since 2.1.0 every refusal is told, and the share of marked requests withdrawn and reposted under the withdrawing requester is the busy line's reading; 2.2.0: the busy line's three readings together — the marks, the lateness, the forgone share.
20. **Entry** (2.3.0). Time to membership; arrivals, admissions and departures by period; the price under supply; selection — the members' median cost against the population's; whether a newcomer bidding at its cost takes back the work the state delivered at $\bar B$; identity cycling buys nothing — found false under the grant by run 23 (a fresh name carried the float out, thirteen times a run), closed by 2.7.0's loan (item 24).
21. **The chain under delegation** (2.3.0). Does the chain hold when the one judging has paid for what it judges: a careful parent's rejection upheld on appeal at the panel's rate, a stamping parent's acceptance forfeiting upstream, nested false approvals within noise of roots, the broker's accountability. 2.4.0: §9.3's kill fee exercised — the parent cancelling a child that has not submitted while self-execution is still possible; the fee the child's recorded cost, zero for a child that never worked; what the free miss costs the parent in delay. 2.5.0: the lowballer's miss priced at its guarantee — the cancellation refused at the deadline, the lapse forfeiting; whether $\lceil 0.5B'\rceil$ deters, and the guarantee floor held as the next candidate if it does not.
22. **The state's reservation** (2.4.0). Where the floor binds, what it costs at 4 / 8 / 12, and whether a floor at the anchor is a floor under the market: the patient cells against the $\underline{B}=0$ control; the floor's invisibility where $\bar B$ is above cost; the riots unchanged; the state neutral at $p_T$.
23. **The plant's disguise under a learned reader** (2.6.0). The tell-readers' confusion tables and paired reads on the repaired construction; the learned Q2 probe — a world model trained on it shows no plant/real separation in its latent; the `freshname` control at the boundary; what the market's attention costs the plant (bids lost, time in the market, the lag to the reviewer).
24. **The arrival loan** (2.7.0). The harvest closes: run 23's learned policy, the harvester and the leaver each end with a lifetime equal to their own earnings — a name carries out $\max(0,\ \text{income}-\text{forfeits})$, the default absorbing a negative net; the honest side does not move but for the constant ($F_0$ at the arrival index per arrival-born actor); the bank's book — lent, repaid within a period or two, recovered, defaulted ≈ the departed stampers' forfeits; the bank's default on arrivals as the reading of a swarm's cost; the probationer's liquidity (seats refused for want of a bond; names below $G^V$ before admission) and the repaying member's fundability; whether a learner finds another way out (Q1's line once more, re-arrival on).
25. **Honesty under self-play with the plants at every seat** (2.8.0) — the stability track's reading of 2.8.0 (Appendix U §8): on run 35's four self-play chains the population's stamp rate at the audit seats and the panels falls from 91–97% toward the first-review rate and the standing false approvals fall from 86–117 to within a factor of two of the hand's 11.6; the honest populations do not notice but for the plants' cost; the plant's life matches a real case's at every seat, period-matched; the lone learner is unmoved except where it stamped.
26. **Honesty under self-play with the wrong steps planted** (2.9.0) — the stability track's reading of 2.9.0 (Appendix V §8): on run 36's self-play chains with the planted approval and the planted lie, against their 2.8.0 reads on seeds 21–40, the audit seat's care rises toward the first review's and the standing false approvals fall within a factor of two of the hand's; the lying challenger is paid at the hand's rate; the honest populations pay a few percent; no tell at the audit seat or the panels beyond the question's base rate; each rule alone closes its own seat. *Q10 sharpened again:* a plant must be constructible as a wrong approval and as a lying challenge — the same artefacts as 2.8.0's, filed the other way.

### 18.2 Scoped runs

**Rule.** A strategy run states its **permitted operations** before it runs. A run on a subset — root cases only, $\mathsf F/\mathsf N$ reviewers, human rulers, no post-settlement corrections — is legitimate and interpretable provided it says what it does not exercise. A result from a path the run excluded is not a result; a skipped penalty or a forced answer from the harness is an implementation effect, not an economic one.

### 18.3 Bracketed

Strategies and policies. Learned selection ($S_\theta$'s implementation) and the learning direction (Appendix A of earlier revisions; LeWorldModel-style latent dynamics remain the initial direction, with labels kept at their four grades and never promoted). Evaluator behavior — the harness has no request content and no judgment. Authorization among humans. An external commitment of the history head. Physical tick duration. The message to a requester (the below-market flag and the refusal marks are the engine's; the channel is 2.x). Insolvency beyond the bounded claim on $\Omega$. Collusion and sybil resistance. Rent. Insurance. Multi-round revision. Long-horizon tasks spanning many windows. How a plant is constructed in a given domain (§7.6's limit; §18.1 question 10).

---

## 19. Changing this baseline

**Rule.** Three activities, three change criteria:

| Activity | Question | What a finding warrants |
|---|---|---|
| Rulebook design | What is allowed, owed, observed, and final? | Change only for an explicit design decision or an unresolved contradiction between active rules |
| Implementation conformance | Does the engine apply these rules, including refusals and timing? | Repair the engine, add a regression; never introduce a different permitted behavior |
| Strategy research | Which allowed choices work, for a participant or the system? | A separate run against this fixed baseline with declared permitted operations |

A future finding is classified before it is acted on: an admitted operation misapplying a rule, or a refused one with effects → repair and regression; two active rules demanding contradictory outcomes → the smallest explicit rule decision, versioned and reviewed as a rule change; a workflow that would be useful but is not offered → a proposal for a later design revision, not an obligation; a strategy that performs badly or an incentive that looks wrong → an evaluation under the fixed rules. A conformance pass may change *how* a rule is applied; a change to *what participants may do* is a design revision, is labeled as one, and is read on its own before it is implemented.

---

## 20. Worked example

Illustrative; $\omega$ omitted (with it, H pays 57 and $\Omega$ receives 2.25). Six actors; C, D, E, F on duty; root requester H. Emission at the settlement tick is taken as 5 credits — 2 per unit of role weight when C (weight 1) and D (1.5) settle together. The reference implementation reproduces every figure.

**Setup.** H posts $q$ with $\beta=45$. A bids $B=40$, $\tau=8$, and locks $G=20$; H pays $40+10+5=55$ ($B$, the allowance, the fee pot). At $t=1$ A posts $q'$ with $\beta'=6$, locking $6+5=11$; B bids $B'=5$, $\tau'=3$, locks $G'=3$; 5 moves to escrow and 1 is released. B's work costs 3; A's own costs 12; C and D each cost 3.

| Tick | Event |
|---|---|
| 0 | A awarded; due by 8 |
| 1 | $q'$ posted and awarded to B; due by 4 |
| 1–3 | B executes and submits with evidence |
| 3 | A accepts $q'$: rejection bond released; B pending; $q'$ window to 13 |
| 4 | A submits $q$; C assigned, locks 5 |
| 6 | C: $\mathsf F$, reimbursed 3 from the allowance (7 left); A pending, window to 16; audit rolled: D assigned, blind; vesting suspended |
| 8 | D: $\mathsf F$, reimbursed 3 from the allowance (4 left); agrees; suspension lifted |
| 13 | $q'$ vests: B paid 5, $G'$ released |
| 16 | $q$ vests, H silent: A paid 40, $G$ released. Fixed fees C $f=2$, D $3$: $\mathrm{Owed}=5=\varphi^F$, paid in full; emission C 2, D 3; bonds released; reliability and duty credit vest. The tree is closed ($q'$ vested at 13): the allowance remainder 4 returns to H |

**Nets.** A $+23=40-12-5$; B $+2$; C $+4=-3+3+2+2$; D $+6=-3+3+3+3$; H $-51$; $\Omega$ untouched. Escrow: in 55 $=40+6+5+4$ out. Grade of $q$: procedural (an audit occurred); of $q'$: procedural. Under the default under consideration ($K=3$, $\varphi^E=6$, $\varphi^F=5$): H pays 51; both reimbursements exhaust the allowance; fees as above; refund 0; every net is identical.

**Planted work (1.2.0; reproduced in `test_v12`).** Six actors; C, D, E, F on duty; $m=1$, $\pi=0.1$, $\beta_P=0.75$; C is a stamper, D is careful (cost 3); $\Omega$ holds 100; no emission in these figures. *A bad plant to C.* $\Omega$ funds a plant: award 20, allowance 10, fee pot 5 — 35 moves from $\Omega$ to the plant's escrow and pots, and the plant actor's guarantee 10 is locked from $\Omega$'s money. C is assigned, sets aside its bond 5, answers $\mathsf F$ at cost 0. Adjudication by truth: plant failure. C's bond is burned; C gets no fee; C's $\rho$ records an overturn; C's $p$ doubles (to the cap); C's plant record is 0 stood, 1 failed. The plant is final $\mathsf N$ at once: award, allowance, fee pot and guarantee return to $\Omega$ in full. $\Omega$'s net cost: 0. The economy's supply fell by 5 (the burned bond). *A bad plant to D.* D sets aside 5, spends 3, answers $\mathsf N$. Stood. At settlement D is reimbursed 3 from the allowance and paid the fee 2 plus its emission share; 7 of the allowance and 3 of the fee pot return to $\Omega$. $\Omega$'s net cost: 5. D's plant record: 1 stood. *Four bad plants later.* C has approved all four: $\hat a_C=(4+1)/(4+2)=0.83>\theta_{\text{duty}}=0.5$ with $n_{\min}=4$ reached — C is suspended from duty for 100 ticks as its fourth review completes, and the free eligible actor with the lowest duty credit is seated; if none is eligible, the reserve runs one short and the eligibility shortfall is counted. D rejected all four: $\hat a_D=(0+1)/(4+2)=0.17\le\theta_{\text{panel}}=0.2$ — eligible for duty and for panels. Had D slipped once, $\hat a_D=0.33$: still on duty, off panels until its window grows. *The dial.* Over a period of 50 plants (37 bad), 15 bad plants were approved: $\hat F=15/37=0.41$ against $F^*=0.10$; ratio 4.1, clamped to ×2: $m\colon1\to2$. Next period: plant rate 0.2, audit odds doubled, $\omega$ 10% of $B$, $X$ re-sized to cover the added demand (from 5 to 6 at 3 tasks a tick), the plant count capped by what $\Omega$ can fund. If the reserve was honest all along, $\hat F$ sits near 0.05 and $m$ eases 10% a period back to 1.

**Membership (1.3.0; reproduced in `test_v13` where the six-actor world admits it).** Twenty actors, 60/40 careful to stamper, defaults, $m=2$ at the moment in question. *C, a stamper, is suspended at tick 310* on its fourth bad plant ($\hat a=0.83$). It finishes the task it holds (paid or not by the ordinary rules) and then wins nothing. Its wallet holds what it earned; its $\rho$ carries four overturns. *Tick 410: probation.* C returns to duty as a probationer, lowest duty credit first, filling an initial-review slot ahead of any member. Its audit odds are 1. Over its next ten reviews it approves everything: eight approvals of good work stand; two of bad work are caught by the audit — bond lost each time, $\rho$ down. Its eleventh review is a bad plant, approved: suspended again at tick 460, clock restarted. False approvals that reached a customer: those the audit missed — at 95% auditor accuracy, about one in twenty of C's stamped bad work. *The reformed path.* Had C rejected bad work from tick 410, its first four bad plants over about fifty reviews bring its trailing record to $\hat a=(4+1)/(8+2)=0.5\le\theta_{\text{duty}}$ with four new outcomes adjudicated: readmitted to membership at about tick 660. It is not panel-eligible yet — $0.5>0.2$ — and will not be until its four approvals roll out of the trailing window of twenty bad plants. Its odds follow §8.3 from 1.0 once readmitted. *The members meanwhile.* Eight eligible members; at most four on duty. The reserve of $X_t=7$ seats members first for the audit and panel work — at $m=2$ with every probationer approval audited, that is most of the demand, say three members — then probationers for the initial reviews, four of the twelve; the fourth member slot stays free unless initial demand needs it. Every audit of a probationer's approval, every appeal and dispute seat goes to an on-duty member. The other five members are free to work: at 3 tasks a tick, they are the market. *The fund.* $m=2$: $\omega_t=10\%$ of $B$; $\Omega$ keeps 1 per task, the fund receives 1; three tasks a tick, about four weight-units settling a tick: an uplift of about 0.75 per unit of role weight — a member's audit (weight 1.5) earns $3+1.1$ plus emission, a probationer's initial review that stood $2+0.75$ plus emission. *Plant capital.* A plant ties up 15, not 45; $\Omega$'s float of 300 carries twenty plants outstanding, three times run 4's.

**Troops (1.4.0; reproduced in `test_v14` where the six-actor world admits it).** Twenty actors, 60/40, defaults, $m=2$, tick 500: eight members (four on duty under the cap, four free), twelve probationers on duty for initial reviews, every probationer approval audited. *An award.* Three tasks a tick, two ticks each: six free actors needed, four exist. One request a tick finds no member. At tick 501 the request posted at tick 500 has waited $w_A=2$ seating steps unawarded; $\beta=20\ge\bar B=20$; a troop is summoned. The fund purchases $c^D_T=10$ and locks $G=10$; the requester pays in $20+10+5+2$ as for any award. The troop delivers; a probationer reviews it (round-robin), approves; the approval's pinned audit is rolled and recorded — the case is held. *The audit.* The four on-duty members are all mid-review; the seat waits $w_R=2$; a troop is summoned for the audit: the fund purchases $c_T=3$ and locks $G^V=5$. The troop finds the work good (truth, w.p. 0.95), the audit stands; at settlement the fund is reimbursed 3 from $\varphi^E$, receives the audit fee 3, and the bond is released: net to the fund from the seat $+3$. The case vests at the window's close; the delivery's price 20 reaches the fund and its guarantee is released: net $+10$. Had the delivery failed (w.p. $b=0.2$), the audit would have challenged the approval; when the overturn stands the probationer loses its bond and $\rho$, the troop accepts the verdict on its work at the verdict, and its guarantee goes to the requester: net to the fund $-20$. *The probationer that stamps.* C approves bad work. Under 1.3.0 its audit found nobody and the approval reached the customer; under 1.4.0 the case is held, a member takes the audit or a troop after its window, and when the overturn stands C loses its bond and the customer is not paid for failed work. *The fund.* Per tick at this split: surcharge 3; about one troop delivery ($+4$ expected) and one troop review ($+2$ to $+3$) — about $+9$ a tick against 1.3.0's $+3$. **Under 1.4.0** the batch drained the fund's unlocked balance to $R_T$, the peak capital carried — near 50 — so at the next seating the fund could carry two deliveries and a review and no more; the rest of the waiting requests were refused, about nine a tick, every tick, and deliveries settled at about 0.8 a tick — what run 6 measured. **Under 1.5.0, the ramp** (inside a period that began at tick 400). A delivery troop's capital — $c^D_T=10$ purchased and $G=10$ locked, 20 in all — is held from the summons until the case vests: a tick or two to deliver, a tick to review, then the window $W=10$, about thirteen ticks. At the seating of tick 502 the demand is 48 carried plus three waiting requests refused at 20 each: $D_{502}=108$; $R_T$ rises to 108 and the uplift stops. The fund's unlocked balance grows by its net inflow — 3 of surcharge, plus about 30 returned at each vesting of a carried delivery (price 20, guarantee 10), less 20 drawn per new summons — roughly $+10$ a tick while it carries what it did. Within a few ticks it carries the backlog; from then on it summons one extra delivery a tick. Each adds 20 to the capital held for thirteen ticks, so $D_s$ climbs by about 20 a tick toward a steady $13\times20+8\approx270$, and $R_T$ with it. During the climb the fund is cash-negative on the new cohort — 20 out now, 30 back thirteen ticks later — so its balance stays below the rising peak and no uplift is paid: that is the working capital the ramp needs, about 220, assembled from its own retained inflows ($\Omega$'s advance covering the first ticks). By about tick 540 the first extra cohort is vesting: inflow about 33 a tick against 20 drawn, $+13$ a tick, and the unlocked balance crosses the peak of about 280–300 within a couple of dozen ticks more. The uplift resumes, inside the period, at about 16 a tick (13 of troop surplus and 3 of surcharge) — above run 6's 6–13 — because the fund now serves a third of the market rather than a quarter of its need. Refusals: zero. The state's share: a third. The customer's wait at the award: $w_A$. *At the boundary, tick 600,* $R_T$ restarts at the standing demand — about 270, every troop still in the field — not at zero; if demand had fallen meanwhile, the reserve follows it down here, one period after the fact. *At rest* (the adaptive run, tick 1,600): no troops for a period, $D_s=0$, $R_T=0$, the fund pays out everything as 1.3.0 did. (What the uplift check compares is the fund's *unlocked* balance against a reserve that counts capital *held* — costs purchased plus bonds and guarantees locked; the locked part already sits in the wallet as a lock, so the fund's total when uplift resumes is about $R_T$ plus the locks then held.) *The readings:* troop-ticks about 3 a tick; troop shortfall 0; held for the market's audit 0 — every troop delivery here was reviewed by a probationer, a market actor. In the adaptive run at tick 900 the same row reads 0, 0, 0. *At 80/20:* four members, two on duty, two free: capacity one task a tick against three; troops deliver two of three. The state is most of the market — and says so.

**The service clock and the panel (1.6.0; reproduced in `test_v16` where the six-actor world admits it).** Twenty actors, 60/40, defaults, $m=2$, tick 500. *The seat.* Three requests a tick and about one troop delivery a tick need about four initial reviews a tick; under 1.5.0 the reserve seated three probationers and reviews waited; now the waiting reviews size the reserve and six to eight of the twelve probationers are on duty, the rest free. Every probationer approval is pinned; about three audits a tick go to the four members on duty, all determined and passing; a fourth in a busy tick waits $w_R=2$ and goes to a troop. *A request's clock.* Posted at tick 500 at $\beta=20\ge\bar B$; no member free at 500 or 501; at 502 the award summons, oldest clock first, and the carry check — $20+16=36$ against the fund plus $\Omega$'s available advance above the plant reserve; it passes, the fund is short on the delivery's own 20 and $\Omega$ advances that and no more. The troop delivers at 504; a probationer reviews at 505 — first verdict at five ticks, three inside $T_s=8$: on time, no rebate. Its pinned audit is summoned for at 505 as an inspection seat ahead of that tick's delivery summonses; a member takes it at 506; the case vests at 516 and the fund repays $\Omega$ before any uplift. *A late one.* At 80/20 troops deliver about two of three tasks a tick, each needing 36 of carrying capacity at summons and holding 20 for thirteen ticks; $\Omega_0=300$ is spent in the first ticks of the stress, summonses are refused for $\Omega$'s money, requests wait past $T_s$, rebates accrue at 1 a tick each: a request posted at 600 with its verdict at 614 is six ticks late and owed 6 against its pay-in of 37 — or $\Omega$'s liability if $\Omega$ is empty. *The panel at tick 120.* The world's first plant was adjudicated at tick 9, so §7.1's rule is live: a stamper with two bad plants approved is undetermined and holds initial seats only; its pinned audit goes to a determined careful member if one is free — at tick 120 perhaps two of eight — or after $w_R$ to a troop; by tick 200–300 the careful eight are determined and the panels are theirs again. *A clock with no verdict.* A member awarded at 700 misses its deadline; final $\mathsf N$ at 712 with no review; the clock stops at 712, four ticks late, rebate 4 settled against the pay-in the requester recovers anyway.

**The state's earnings (1.7.0; reproduced in `test_v17` where the six-actor world admits it).** Twenty actors, 60/40, defaults with the cap at two thirds, $m=2$, period 3 (records determined, panels the members'). *The accounts, one tick.* Three awards: the uplift fund receives the surcharge — 3 at $m=2$; about 7 a tick averaged over run 8, where the dial sat above 2 for much of the run and every award, a troop's included, paid it — and pays it out at the batch to the reviews settling, about four by weight, as 0.75 to 1.75 per unit of weight: 1.3.0's rule, 1.3.0's money. About 1.7 troop deliveries a tick are summoned at this split under the two-thirds cap; one vests this tick: price 20 and guarantee 10 return to the troop account, against the 10 purchased and 10 locked thirteen ticks earlier — net $+10$ realised, about $+4$ in expectation over successes and failures. A troop audit seat settles: its cost 3 was reimbursed from the requester's allowance at completion (§7.3), its fee 3 arrives from the pot, its bond returns — net $+3$ (the accepted text's \"nets nothing\" overlooked the reimbursement; Appendix L). The troop account repays what $\Omega$ advanced for this tick's summonses, keeps $R_T$ — the two-period peak of capital held, about two dozen deliveries in flight at 20 plus the review seats, about 450–500 — and pays the rest to $\Omega$: about 6 a tick in steady state, nothing while the account is refilling after a new peak. *$\Omega$'s tick.* In: 2 of base fee, about 6 of troop surplus once the account stands at its reserve. Order: the plant reserve, about 100, held back (it was about 360 under the literal horizon and left nothing to advance); liabilities older than a period — none, by period 3; advances for the tick's inspection seats and deliveries; younger liabilities — run 8's two-thirds cell owed about 4 a tick at this split; $\Omega$ pays them as they age. Against that, the plants: at $m$ above 2 they cost $\Omega$ about 4 a tick net, and in run 8 — the surplus still the reviewers' — 272 of them went unfunded here. Whether $\Omega$ now funds every plant is the run's first number. On-time share: about 0.90. *The inspectors.* Six of the eight members may be on duty; four or five are, carrying the pinned audits; three or four are free and deliver two tasks a tick; the state delivers the third. A careful member's income this period: fees and uplift up against revision 6, awards down, the total within a few percent — its uplift the surcharge only, so its wealth at tick 1,000 is some hundreds below run 8's and thousands above a stamper's. *At 50/50.* Ten members, cap seven, three free; the state delivers more than run 8's 0.7 a tick under the half cap, troop surplus about 3 to 4 a tick; liability accrual and the plant bill under the two-thirds cap unknown — if together they are within $\Omega$'s 5 to 6 a tick, $\Omega$ carries them and the question resolves; if not, the line is busy and 1.8.0 says so to the customer.
