**The price of work (2.0.0; reproduced in `test_v20` where the six-actor world admits it).** Twenty actors, 60/40, defaults, tick 500, records determined: eight members, cap six on duty, two to three free; twelve probationers; six requesters, wallets near 150–200, issued 10 a tick, ceilings a third of the unlocked wallet — 40 at the cap when rich, less when the float is out; $N_T=40$, the quiet field about 23 of it; $P_t\approx9$. *A quiet tick.* Three requests posted; each locks its all-in — $\beta+15\times0.45+\omega(\beta)$ at the posting tick's $m$, about 45 at $\beta=40$ — from a wallet of 170. Two free members bid: one awarded last tick bids $8\times1.3=10.4$; one idle four ticks bids $8\times1.05=8.4$. On the oldest request the two differ in $\rho$ by 0.01 and in price by $0.2\times(10.4-8.4)/40=0.01$ — the cheaper wins at 8.4, and its requester's lock releases $40-8.4$ and $\omega_0 m\,(40-8.4)$. The second goes at 10.4. The third waits $w_A=2$ and goes to a troop at $\bar B\approx9$ (its ceiling is above it); the summons needs two places in the field — the delivery's and its inspection seat's — and has them; $\Omega$ burns 9 and locks $G=5$; if its unlocked balance is short, it creates the shortfall and the deficit rises by it, to be retired on $\Omega$'s next inflow. The members and probationers who review these cases are paid at $9/20$ of the 1.x fee — the same work in fewer credits; the careful reviewer's care cost is its own 2–3 as before, so its real margin on a review is thinner than at $P_0$ (§4.6's named effect). $P_t$ stays near 9: cost 8, margins of 5–30%, capacity just slack. *A customer below market.* A requester whose wallet has run down to 30 can lock all-in only for $\beta\approx6$; it posts, is flagged below market (9), its clock stopped, and waits. Three ticks of issuance later it raises the ceiling to 10, locking the difference; a member idle six ticks bids 8.4 and takes it. *The hard spike, with $N_T=40$.* A crowd of thirty requesters arrives at tick 400 with wallets of 200 and issuance of 10, and posts about fifteen a tick for 100 ticks. The members' bids rise to the ceilings as idleness vanishes; the state summons until forty places are filled — about twenty-six deliveries and their seats — and then refuses for capacity: those requests wait, their clocks run, and each is recorded. $\bar B$ climbs from 9 toward the ceilings (40), $P_t$ with it; $\Omega$ creates the in-flight capital of the field (about $26\times(c^D_T+G)$ at the risen prices — some 1,500) and retires it as the cohort vests; at cost the state's surplus is nil and its failed deliveries cost it $1.5\bar B$ each. The crowd leaves at tick 500 with its open requests served out, its wallets departing $M$; ceilings recover, bids fall, $P_t$ returns toward 9 over a period; the refusals for capacity are the riot's one reading the price could not answer. With $N_T$ unbounded the same crowd fields 200 troops, creates 4,000–7,000 in its first window — a third of $M$ — retires it inside two periods, and leaves no queue; $P_t$ rises less, because nothing was scarce but money. *A deficit shock.* At tick 600 the driver's scenario operation (recorded as a scenario, not as a rebate) creates 2,000 and pays it to the six requesters, against $M\approx9{,}000$. Their wallets rise by a third; their ceilings and posting rate with them; capacity binds at 40, bids rise, $P_t$ toward 12. A member's 500 credits buy 42 tasks' worth instead of 55; its indexed fee still buys the same work; its nominal $c_D$ of 8 is now a third below the going price, so its margin widens. $\Omega$ retires on every inflow; $P_t$ returns toward 9 over a period and a half; the 500 buys 55 again. *(What the engine's first smoke showed before run 12, for the record: at 60/40 six of the eight members are on duty and two deliver, so the two free members' margins ratchet to the ceiling and the state follows them at $\bar B$ — $P_t$ ran to about 35 in 400 ticks with the deficit at zero and the field slack; the worked example's 9 assumes members plentiful, and run 12 reads which it is.)*

**The bank (2.1.0; reproduced in `test_v21` where the six-actor world admits it).** Twenty actors, 60/40, defaults, tick 500, records determined; eight members, two to three free; six requesters issued 10 a tick by the bank; $N_T=40$; $\bar B\approx30$ (run 12's level — the ratchet's, not the rules'); $P_t$ published at tick 500 as the fourth period's mean, 31; $b_T=0.2$ from the last period's 67 state deliveries (run 12's 670 a run at this split), 13 of them final $\mathsf N$. *A summons.* A request past $w_A$ with $\beta\ge30$ goes to a troop at $\bar B=30$. The burn is $c^D_T=0.8\times30-0.2\times15=21$, not 30; $\Omega$ locks $G=15$. $\Omega$'s unlocked balance is 4 (it funds summonses before the bank does), so the bank lends the shortfall, 32, tied to the summons; loans outstanding rise by 32. Its inspection seat, summoned when the delivery is submitted if no member is free, is lent for likewise — $c_T$ and $G^V$ — and repaid at the review's settlement, before or after the delivery's own close. Thirteen ticks later the delivery vests: it returns 30 and releases 15; 32 repays the loan, 13 reaches $\Omega$ — of which, if the state has debt, every credit goes to the debt the moment it arrives. Had the delivery ended in a final $\mathsf N$: nothing returns, $G$ goes to the requester, and the 32 lent becomes debt. Over the period's 67 deliveries: about 54 successes at $+9$ and 13 failures at $-36$ — 486 against 468, neutral within the rounding. The period's base fees (about 570) and the troops' review fees are $\Omega$'s to keep, after any debt from the variance (about 150 — three failures more or fewer than the rate, at 45 a swing — well within a period's fees) and after rebates owed. *A period boundary.* At tick 600 the bank publishes $P_t=$ the fifth period's mean, 32; every case locked from tick 600 to 699 is indexed at $32/20$ and keeps it; a case locked at tick 599 keeps $31/20$. $\bar B$ went on moving every tick. *A plant.* The dial calls for a plant at tick 610; the bank mints its two pots, $(10+5)\times32/20=24$; the plant is posted (its award and guarantee notional, as since 1.3.0), awarded, reviewed and closed; at closure its wallets hold about 11 of refunds (run 10's 6 of 10 and 0.8 of 5, at the index), which the sweep returns to the bank and destroys. The cost of truth that plant: about 13, beside emission's $E_{610}\approx6.6$ a tick. $\Omega$'s balance was never asked, and nothing minted for the plant reached it. *The flood.* Twelve requesters sustained; the field full; 3,500 refusals a run, every one told. The default requesters hold: their clocks run, the state owes rebates at the stops, $\Omega$ pays what it can at each stop and the rest accrues — a liability stock that peaks at a few thousand and ages a period or two, paid down from the flood's own fees after the debt. $M$ grows by issuance alone. The withdrawing variant withdraws at the mark and reposts; its share withdrawn is the reading. The loans line carries the field's capital, 1,000–1,500, repaid as it vests; the debt line carries the failures' losses, repaid from the flood's fees inside a period; the wall — a flood period's fees, in the thousands — is never reached. *The wall, if it were reached.* Suppose the state's deliveries began failing at a half while $b_T$ still read a fifth: for a period each delivery is $0.5\times9-0.5\times36=-13.5$ in expectation, about 900 on 67 — above a period's fees. The bank stops lending for deliveries; they are funded from $\Omega$'s balance, which is zero while the debt stands; requests past $w_A$ wait, marked *refused for credit*, told; the state has retracted. Review seats are still lent for: the members' deliveries are inspected as before, and the market runs on without the state. At the period's close $b_T$ reads a half, the burn falls to $0.5\times30-0.5\times15=7.5$, each success now leaves 22.5, and the debt is repaid within the next period's deliveries and fees; lending resumes. Had the failures run above two thirds, no burn would make the state neutral and the wall would hold until the state's fee income alone had repaid the debt. The customers waited, in public, for a state that had lost more than it earns. *(In the six-actor world of `test_v21`: $\bar B=10$, $G=5$, the burn 7, the loan 11 over $\Omega$'s 1.0 of fees, 4.5 left to $\Omega$ after the vesting; a failed delivery's 11 of debt and the next award's fee repaying 0.5 of it inside the award; a wall of 0.5 after a four-tick period with one award, the next delivery refused for credit and a seat lent for regardless.)*

**The rebate from income (2.2.0; reproduced in `test_v22` where the six-actor world admits it).** *A quiet period at 60/40* (run 14's cell). The period's fees: base ≈ 275, the troops' review fees ≈ 190 — a pool of 465 after a debt of 0, locked on $\Omega$ as it arrives; the troop deliveries' prices and guarantees coming back during the period land unlocked and fund the next summonses, as before. Eight requests stopped late inside the period, claims 1–4 each, 9 in all. At the close each is paid in full; 456 is released to $\Omega$'s unlocked balance; forgone 0. Nothing a requester or a member can see has changed from 2.1.0, except that the rebate came at the close rather than at the stop — and that in the first period, with only the float of 300 unlocked, a few summonses are the bank's loans until the first close. *The flood of twelve, $N_T=40$* (run 14: claims accrued 32,503 over six flooded periods). In a flooded period some 300 requests stop late with claims near the cap — about 5,400; the state's fees for the period are about 900, its debt repayments about 50; the pool is 850. At the close each late requester receives $850/5{,}400\approx16\%$ of its claim; 4,550 is forgone, recorded on each request and in the period's readings as a forgone share of 0.84; $\Omega$ retains 0. Over the flood's six periods the customers are paid about 5,100 of the 32,500 the clock says they were owed, and the ledger shows the other 27,400 as forgone, period by period. *The crowd of thirty, 100 ticks* (run 14: claims accrued 14,190). Two periods of claims near 7,000 each against pools near 1,200: the forgone share about 0.83 while the crowd is in; the period after, claims fall to a few hundred as the queue drains and are paid in full. *A state at the wall* (the three-quarters failure; run 14: debt repaid 6,831 a run). The debt takes about 680 of a period's fees of about 900; the pool is 220 against claims near 900 — a forgone share of 0.75 at a lateness where the honest flood's would be lower. The customers of a losing state get less back: the bank's money first. *(In the six-actor world of `test_v22`: two awards' fees of 0.5 locked as the pool; a state delivery lent 12 in full, since the pool funds no summons; its claim of 1 recorded at the stop and paid from the pool of 1.5 at the close, the remainder 0.5 repaying debt; two claims of 3 against a pool of 1 paid 0.5 each, 5 forgone, a share of 5/6; two claims of 9 against a pool of 1, a share of 17/18.)*

**The arrival loan (2.7.0; reproduced in `test_v27` in the six-actor world).** *An honest arrival* at $P_t=33$ is lent $50\times33/20=83$, posts its bond of 8 from it, earns about $+1.2$ a review and sends each fee home to the bank — about seventy reviews, most of its probation — its wallet near 83 less the one bond it forfeits on a plant it misread; admitted with a few credits outstanding, its first award repays them and the rest is its own; at the run's end its wallet is its earnings, under 2.6.1 its earnings plus 83. *A cycler* arrives with 83, earns 6 in fees (repaid, $L=77$), forfeits two bonds, 16, and is suspended at its first bad plant; it departs at once: the bank recovers its wallet of 67, the default is 10 — the bonds burned less the fees repaid — and it carries out nothing; thirteen cycles carry out thirteen times nothing where each carried out 67 under the grant. *A leaver* with 1,100 and a loan long repaid takes 1,100 out, nothing recovered; had it left in its first period with 40 outstanding and 95 held, the bank would recover 40 and 55 would leave — its earnings to that tick. *The classification, event by event:* a reviewer's fee repays; its cost reimbursed passes untouched (spent by `spend`, a burn, and replaced); a court fee coming back passes untouched; a child's guarantee forfeited to a parent and a kill fee repay; every release passes.

**The plants reach every seat (2.8.0; reproduced in `test_v28` in the six-actor world).** *A bad plant approved, then audited.* Reviewer C, a stamper at odds 0.5, approves a bad plant blind: a plant failure at C's verdict (bond burned, record −1, $p_C$ doubled to 1), as 1.2.0 had it — and the plant is **pending with its window**, not final $\mathsf N$ at once; the audit rolls at the 0.5 C carried at its verdict, not the doubled 1; auditor D stamps $\mathsf F$ blind — a plant failure for D, the audit seat priced — and the audit agrees, so the case holds to the window's close and is finalized $\mathsf N$ by truth; had D rejected, D stands and a third reviewer E is seated as on a real root, adjudicated by truth, the case final $\mathsf N$ by truth with no ruling and C adjudicated once. *A good plant rejected, then appealed.* Careful D misreads a good plant: a plant failure for D; the plant actor appeals, its alias topped up by the bank with $D=2$ and the fee destroyed at filing — nothing to $\Omega$, no bond to pay it back; panelists E ($\mathsf F$, stood) and F ($\mathsf N$ blind, a plant failure); the case vests by truth; the pots pay E's fee and are destroyed at closure. *A bad plant answered $\mathsf P$.* With no real revision in the instrument's window the plant actor accepts and the plant is spent, the review paid; where the window holds real revisions the plant actor revises with a drawn truth sealed as the second marker, the same reviewer resumes and is adjudicated against it. *The pots.* $\varphi^E=10$ and $\varphi^F=5$ pay the initial seat and one challenge seat; at the third seat the fee pot's shortfall of 3 is minted — nothing before. *A seat nobody is free to take.* With one reviewer on duty and excluded from its own case, the plant's audit seat waits $w_R=2$ seating steps and is taken by the orchestrator's own verdict (the truth at $\varepsilon_T=0$), then the third seat the same; no troop, no fee; the case final $\mathsf N$ by truth; $\Omega$ unchanged throughout.

**The plant takes the wrong step (2.9.0; reproduced in `test_v29` in the six-actor world).** *A planted approval.* A bad plant posted as a planted approval lives to its submission; its initial seat is nobody's (the seating offers it to no member; the pass passes it by); at the drawn gap the orchestrator seats its review under `plant_rev:…` with a bond of $G^V$ minted and locked, and the next tick records F — the case pending with its window, its audit obligation at odds 1, the period's approvals and the audit demand +1, the dial's plants, bad and bad-approved untouched; the public `audit_odds_at_verdict` shows the value drawn from the real approvals' odds. Member D confirms blind: a plant failure (bond burned, record −1, odds doubled); the case is final N at the window's close, the alias's bond released and retired with the pots, nothing reaching Ω. Had D rejected, the third seat opens and resolves by truth; had the plant requester disputed first, the panel judges it and the standing obligation is discharged at the settlement. *A planted lie.* A bad plant answered N by careful C: while the period's quota stands the plant actor appeals (D minted to the alias and destroyed); one panelist approves blind — a plant failure — one rejects — stood; the case resolves N by truth, C not adjudicated again. *The real case.* A real root rolled for audit, then disputed and lost, vests at its window's close with the obligation discharged (before 2.9.0 §4 it stayed pending).

**The supply side (2.3.0; reproduced in `test_v23` where the six-actor world admits it).** Twenty actors at genesis, 60/40, costs drawn at $\sigma=0.43$ — the eight members' costs 5, 6, 7, 8, 8, 9, 11, 14; the stampers' similar. Tick 300, $P_t$ 33, $\bar B$ 30, three members free, posts 1.9 a tick, the state at 38%. An entry pool of twenty candidates with costs from 3 to 19. *An arrival.* A candidate with cost 5 reads the view: $\bar B-c_D=25$ an award, a free member wins about 0.27 awards a tick, time to membership about 250 ticks — expected earnings well above $r=0.8$. It arrives; the bank mints its float, $50\times33/20=83$; it is a probationer: seated for initial reviews, every approval audited, plants at the ordinary rate. It earns review fees and uplift, about $+1.2$ a review, and wins no award. At tick 560, four bad plants adjudicated and its record at 0.5, it is admitted; it bids $5\times1.0=5$ on the oldest request against the incumbents' ratchet bids of 29 and the state's $\bar B$ of 30, and wins at 5. $\bar B$ begins to fall. Over the next two periods six more candidates with costs 3–8 arrive and are admitted; by tick 900 the members number fifteen, $P_t$ is 14, the state's share 9%, and the honest real earnings per member about 0.9 a tick — just above $r$; the remaining candidates, costs 10–19, read earnings below $r$ and do not come. *A departure.* The incumbent with cost 14 has won nothing since tick 700: the price is 14 and its bid floor is 14. Its trailing period's earnings are 0.2 a tick, below $r$. It holds no commitment, no review, no lock; it departs; its wallet of 1,100 leaves $M$ as `departure`; its record is archived. Had it been mid-review, the departure would have been refused until the review settled. *A delegation.* The incumbent with cost 11 wins a request at 20 (its bid at $m=0.8$). Its own margin is 9. It posts a nested request at $\beta'=9$, locking the nested all-in $9+15\times1.65+0.05\,m\,9\approx35$ and $G^V$; the arrival with cost 3 — admitted at tick 620, a member now — bids $3\times1.3=3.9$ and wins; the pots and the fee are drawn from the lock and the rest released. (A probationer could not have bid.) The child delivers; the parent judges — carefully — and approves; the child's work is audited at the member's ordinary odds from the child's own pots, stands, and vests to the child. The parent submits its own delivery, built on the child's, and vests at 20. The parent's take: $20-3.9-$ the nested fee $\omega_0\cdot3.9-$ the nested pots' net cost, about 1.5 (posted by the parent, refunded less what the child's reviewers drew) $-$ its care in judging, about 2 — some 12, against 9 had it done the work itself. The root requester's deposit paid for none of it. **Had the parent been a stamper** and the child's work bad, the parent approves, builds on it, and forfeits its own guarantee upstream with no recourse (§9.2). **Had the parent rejected good work**, the child appeals against the parent's rejection bond and the panel overturns at its ordinary rate. *A cycler.* A stamper suspended at tick 400 departs at tick 420 with a wallet of 190 (gone from $M$) and arrives anew at tick 421 with a float of 83 and no record. It is a probationer exactly where it stood, 107 credits poorer; its first bad plant restarts its probation as before. *(In the six-actor world of `test_v23`: the float 50 at the index, 62.5 after a boundary at 25; a nested request's lock $6+15+0.3=21.3$ and the bond 5; the child's audit paid from the child's allowance, 7 of 10 refunded to the parent at the node's closure and the root's 10 untouched; an arrival admitted on its second bad plant with $n_{\min}=2$ and left a probationer by two good ones.)*

**Variants** — each reproduced in the test suite: rejection and appeal (A $+23$, C $-5$: bond pays A's fee and burns 3, costs reimbursed, no fee; E and F each owed 3, $\mathrm{Owed}=6>5$, paid 2.5 each plus emission 2.5); appeal of a $\bot$ with the objection rejected (same figures) and upheld (A bears the fee, final $\mathsf N$, A $-12-2-20$); pool exhaustion (A withdraws at 6, forfeits 5, nets $-17$; both pots return to H at closure; with $\tau=4$ the deadline falls first and A $-20$); cancellation (kill fee $\min(2,5)=2$ from escrow, 3 returned); nested rejection appealed and overturned (A's rejection bond pays B's fee; B $+2$ as if accepted); shortfall and haircut (allowance exhausted, $\Omega$ advances 5; the fee pot pays $\mathrm{Owed}=8$ scaled by $5/8$ and has nothing left at closure, so the advance stays unrecovered, borne by $\Omega$; emission in full); timeout and replacement (3 positions on the path, 4 distinct reviewers); a dispute at the window's last tick (admitted; vest at 14 not 12); a correction after vesting (record and reliability change, no credit moves).

---

The 1.6.0 text's horizon was the ticks remaining in the period, which at $\Omega_0=300$ left $\Omega$ nothing to advance (about 360 at 60/40); the implementer read a plant's life, since a plant's pots return to $\Omega$ at its closure, and 1.7.0 ruled it so; the literal horizon is kept as a declared cell (`--plant-reserve period`) | §10.4 |

| reserve target (1.6.0) | $\max(X_t,\ \text{reviews waiting}+\text{the period's initial-review rate})$ at every rotation, by kind | §12.1 |

| troop account (1.7.0; retired 2.0.0 — the troops' wallet is $\Omega$) | $\Omega$'s advances and every troop's earnings in; costs purchased, locks, a guarantee forfeited at a final $\mathsf N$, a bond burned on an overturn, repayments out; the unlocked balance above $R_T$ to $\Omega$ at each settlement batch; the flag `troop_account` on, `--rules 1.6.0` the control (the merged fund, the half cap) | §12.3 |

| troop price (1.4.0; 2.0.0: $P_t$ when there is none, members' awards only) | $\bar B$, the trailing mean awarded price; $\beta$ if none; summoned only when $\beta\ge\bar B$ ($=\beta$ in the drivers so far) | §12.3 |

| not indexed (2.0.0) | the agents' effort costs ($c_D$ 8 per task, the care cost 2–3 a review — nominal, the drivers' declared reservation prices; an adaptive variant follows $P_t$ with a one-period lag, swept); emission; balances held | §4.6, §4.2 |
| requesters (2.0.0; the drivers) | 6; initial wallet 200; issuance 10 a tick each (60 a tick — clears near $B\approx9$ at 60/40 under $N_T=40$: supply about 4.2 a tick, a task's all-in net of refunds about $1.6B$; swept 15, which clears near 13 and leaves every quiet cell mildly capacity-bound); ceiling $\min(40,\ \tfrac13 W_{\text{unlocked}})$, one post a tick while $K_{\text{open}}=5$ and the all-in fit; a flagged request raised to the market price when the wallet allows | §2.1, §4.1, §5.1 |
| members' bid (2.0.0; the drivers) | $c_D(1+m)$, $m$ from 0.3, $+0.3$ on an award, $-0.25$ per three idle ticks (continuously), floored at 0; every offer at the ceiling is the control; $\lambda$ 0.2, swept 0.5 | §5.1, §5.2 |
| $c^D_T$ (2.0.0; 2.1.0 the expected cost) | $\max(0,(1-b_T)\,p_T-b_T G)$ at the state's price $p_T$ (2.4.0; $\bar B$ before it), neutral in expectation up to $b_T=2/3$; controls $p_T$ (2.0.0's at cost) and $0.5\,P_t$ (the 1.x margin) | §7.7 |
| $\underline{B}$ — the state's reservation (2.4.0) | **8**, nominal, public — the anchor cost (the median effort cost the drivers declare, 2.0.0 §2.3); the state's price $p_T=\max(\bar B,\underline{B})$, the summons only where $\beta\ge p_T$; swept 4 / 8 / 12; **0 is the control** (2.3.0's state) | §5.1, §12.3 |
| $b_T$ (2.1.0) | the final-$\mathsf N$ share of the state's deliveries over the last closed period if it closed ten or more, else trailing periods back until ten, else the prior 0.2 (swept 0.1 / 0.3; the window one or two periods) | §7.7 |
| the bank $\mathfrak B$ (2.1.0) | one account, not an actor; mints `issuance`, `mint_emission`, `mint_plant`, `mint_scenario`; lends `loan`; destroys `repayment`, `retire_plant` | §10.4 |
| the loan (2.1.0) | the shortfall of a summons over $\Omega$'s unlocked balance, tied to the summons, repaid from its own return (a delivery's at vesting or final N, a seat's at its review's settlement) | §10.4 |
| $\Delta_{\max}$, the wall (2.1.0) | one closed period of $\Omega$'s fee income — base fees and the troops' review fees; unbounded in the first period; deliveries only, seats always lent for; control unbounded (`debt_bound_periods` 1 / 0; swept 2) | §10.4 |
| the rebate (2.1.0; 2.2.0) | the claim $r_s=1$ a tick late past $T_s=8$ (the drivers: $\omega_0 B$), indexed, capped at $B$, recorded at the stop, withdrawn before award owed nothing — the measure; paid at the period's close from the period's fee pool pro rata, the rest forgone and read; never created; no stock (2.1.0: at the stop from $\Omega$'s balance, else a liability) | §3.1, §10.4 |
| the pool (2.2.0) | $\Omega$'s fee income of the period after the debt — base fees, the troops' review fees, court fees a troop wins; a lock on $\Omega$, untouched until the close | §10.4 |
| readings (2.2.0) | claims, paid, forgone, the forgone share, $\Omega$ retained — per period; the busy line's third reading | §12.3, §18.1 |
| `arrive` (2.3.0) | a fresh identity, no record, a float $F_0=50$ at $P_0$ indexed, lent by the bank (`arrival`; the name's arrival loan since 2.7.0 — minted as a grant 2.3.0–2.6.1); a probationer (1.3.0 §2.2 without the suspension). **The float exists so that an arrival can post the reviewer's bond: $F_0\ge G^V$, a constraint on the parameters, nominal, the index cancelling** (2.4.0; with $F_0=0$ no arrival is ever seated, so none is admitted — run 17) | §2.1, §11.2 |
| admission (2.3.0) | the trailing record passes $\theta_{\text{duty}}$ with $\ge n_{\min}$ **bad** plants adjudicated; panels at $\theta_{\text{panel}}$; no award of any kind before; readmission of the suspended unchanged | §11.2 |
| `depart` (2.3.0) | admissible with no live obligation to or from the actor (commitment, open or unsettled review, lock, claim on $\Omega$; an unrepaid loan is none); off duty as it leaves; since 2.7.0 the unlocked wallet repays the arrival loan first (`arrival_repayment`) and the remainder leaves as `departure`, the shortfall the bank's default, read; the record archived, never reused | §2.1, §5, §16 |
| the arrival loan (2.7.0) | $L=F_0$ at the arrival index, nominal; repaid from the name's income first at each inflow (§5's table: fees, uplift, emission, the price whole, a kill fee, a forfeited child's guarantee — never a release, a reimbursement or a court fee coming back); recovered at departure up to the loan; `Params.arrival_loan` on, off is 2.6.1's grant (the control) | §4, §5 |
| the genesis float | the sponsor's gift — 50 per genesis actor, paid into $M_0$ once, never lent (2.7.0 §2.2) | Appendix A, initialization |
| $c_D$ (2.3.0; the drivers') | log-normal, median 8, $\sigma=0.43$, clipped $[2,40]$, drawn once per actor; swept $\sigma$ 0.2 / 0.43 / 0.8; private; cost only | §5.1 |
| the entry pool (2.3.0; the drivers') | 20 candidates; reservation $r=0.8$ real a tick; at most one arrival a tick; depart after one period below $r$; swept $r$ 0.5 / 1.2, the cap 3, the float 0 | §2.1 |
| nested pots (2.3.0) | posted by the parent at the nested posting index with the all-in; refunded to it at the node's closure; control: the 1.1.0 reading (`nested_pots root`) | §4.3, §9.1 |
| the summons (2.3.0) | deliveries for root requests only; review seats on nested cases as on roots; no nested clock | §9.1, §12.3 |
| the reviewer advance (2.3.0) | may draw on the rebate pool during the period, the pool at the close net of it | §10.4 |
| refusals (2.1.0) | for capacity, for credit: the request marked with the kind and the tick, told, readable; the requester holds (default) or withdraws and reposts (the variant) | §12.3 |
| $N_T$ (2.0.0) | 40, deliveries and review seats together; a delivery holds two places until its inspection is seated; 0 = unbounded, the control | §7.7, §12.3 |
| creation / retirement (2.0.0; retired 2.1.0 — the control, `Params.bank` off) | last resort for a required payment; every inflow burned while the deficit stands; the engine's `Params.issuance` defaults to 0 (the driver declares the requesters' issuance; the reviewer tests see none) | §10.4 |
| plants (2.1.0) | the two pots minted at posting (`mint_plant`, at the posting index); whatever the plant's wallets hold at closure destroyed (`retire_plant`); the cost of truth $=$ minted $-$ destroyed; never capped by $\Omega$'s cash | §7.6 |
| the plant's seats (2.8.0) | `Params.plants_every_seat` on (off is 2.7.0): the audit, the third seat, the appeal, the dispute, the resubmission — at the real population's rates and delays, the construction's (no dial); adjudication by truth at every seat, the initial reviewer once; the resubmitted truth the instrument's draw, a second marker, spent while the real-revision window is empty; the markers revealed at closure; the audit roll at the odds the reviewer carried at its verdict (every case); the seat past $w_R$ the orchestrator's own verdict at $1-\varepsilon_T$, no fee, counted apart; the pots topped up to the seat's need (`mint_plant` at assignment), the court fee minted to the challenger's alias and destroyed at filing (`retire_plant`); the dial's clock on initial adjudications | §7.6, §8.3 |
| the planted approval (2.9.0) | `Params.planted_approval` on (off is 2.8.0): $\pi^A_0$ 0.02 planted approvals per real approval audited by a member (the ruling after run 38; before: per real pending approval), $\pi^A_{\max}$ 0.40, $m^A_{\max}$ 20 (the defaults correction after run 38 under §19 — $m_{\max}$ 4 left $\pi^A$ at 0.08), the multiplier stepping on at least ten pooled adjudications (`seat_dial_min_adj`); the audit seat a member's or nobody's, an unseated planted approval closed by truth at the window's end; the audit odds 1, recorded at the verdict (not a roll); the seating gap drawn from the trailing real roots', the review's own tick |
| the planted lie (2.9.0) | `Params.planted_lie` on (off is 2.8.0): $\pi^{La}_0 = \pi^{Ld}_0$ 0.25 per real appeal / dispute (a coin per real challenge filed in the pass since the eighth conformance item; the trailing closed period's count times the rate the expected count, the record), $\pi^{L}_{\max}$ 2.0, $m^{L}_{\max}$ 8 (the defaults correction after run 38), the multipliers stepping on at least ten pooled adjudications, a floor of two a period of each kind filed at ticks drawn from the trailing real challenges' positions (uniform where empty), the grounds the population's string |
| issuance (2.1.0) | 10 a tick a requester, the bank's; swept 5 / 10 / 20 at 20 honest — the inflation test | §4.1, §18.1 |
| a riot (2.0.0; the drivers) | a crowd: $n$ requesters arrive for the event with the default wallet and issuance and leave after, their wallets departing $M$ — thirty for 100 ticks is run 11's hard spike, twelve sustained its flood; a demand event. The deficit shock — 1,000 / 2,000 / 5,000 created at tick 600 and paid to the standing requesters — is the money event | §4.4, §18.1 |

**Initialization (2.0.0; 2.1.0).** No backing: $M_0$ is the genesis balances, all issuance — a one-time cold-start float of 50 credits per actor, $\Omega_0=300$ for the orchestrator, and each requester's initial wallet (200 in the drivers; about 2,500 in all at twenty actors and six requesters); $P_0=20$; the deficit 0 (2.0.0); the bank's position the genesis issuance, loans and debt 0 (2.1.0). *(1.0.1–1.7.1: $v_0=1$; $\mathrm{Res}_0=10{,}000$ real units paid in by a sponsor, $M_0=10{,}000$ credits, the floats and budgets drawn from it; $10^7$ in the drivers.)* The engine's `Params.floating=False` is the 1.7.1 engine, kept for the tests and the controls; `Params.bank=False` is the 2.0.0 engine (`drivers.stamping --rules 2.0.0`).

## Appendix B. Superseded ideas and why

| Idea | Held in | Dropped because |
|---|---|---|
| Fixed resource prices | v0.1 | For the initial model only; prices float by §4.4 |
| The orchestrator as funding source, "funding received" | v0.1 | Nothing pushed price down and no budget existed; money now enters with requests |
| Fulfillment-first selection with no price term | v0.1 | Bids rose unbounded; $\lambda$ added |
| One-sided reviewer liability (wrong $\mathsf N$ costs $D$, wrong $\mathsf F$ costs nothing) | v0.1 | Approving everything was dominant; bond, audit and dispute made it symmetric |
| $\bot$ paid regardless | v0.1 | A free exit; now a substantiated claim the actor may contest |
| One scalar rating with $+\epsilon$ per completed review | v0.1 | Rewarded participation, not correctness; split into $\rho$ and $\kappa$, both vesting on survival |
| Actor stuck until settlement | v0.1 | A 10-tick window would idle it; capacity releases at the verdict |
| Fixed reviewer fee $f=2$ | v0.1 | Could not hold with fixed emission; derived |
| $\varphi$ as 10% of $B$ | v0.2 | Not a funding source for reimbursement; now a deposit sized to verification cost |
| Carry-forward emission | v0.2–v0.3 | The first reviews after a quiet start won a lottery |
| Bond smaller than liability | v0.2 | A reviewer could owe 7 with 5 locked |
| "Stamping is negative in expectation" | v0.2 | Did not follow; now a hypothesis with the inequality stated |
| $\kappa$ as an emission weight | v0.2 | Confused a count with a weight |
| Discretionary printing | never adopted | Inflation erodes fixed penalties first; emission is a rule |
| "$\mathrm{Res}\to0\Rightarrow p_r\to\infty$" | v0.3 | Purchases are value-neutral; the halt is through wallets |
| Nominals indexed at use | v0.3 | A later court fee could exceed the bond already locked; frozen at first lock |
| Unbonded nested rejection | v0.3 | An overturned rejection had no reimbursement source; the posting lock is the bond |
| $V_{\max}$ as a lifetime count of reviewers | v0.3 | Unbounded under replacement; it counts positions |
| Grade by participation; by requester's role | v0.3, v0.8 | A losing dispute is not a human label; the author of the establishing event decides |
| "Self-execution at any time" | v0.3 | Ignored an awarded subcontract; the kill fee, and no cancellation after submission |
| Clawback after vesting | considered | Rejected for corrective obligations; vesting is final |
| A delegate with ruling authority | an unstated reading | A delegate is a model; the override exists because a human is not |
| **Audit reopening** — an auditor's $\mathsf P$/$\bot$ sending an approved case back to the actor | v0.9 | A design change made in a conformance pass; collided with timestamps, capacity, replacement, nested liability and merged disputes at once. An audit judges a finished verdict. Withdrawn in v0.10; the interface problem it addressed is solved by the view stating its admissible answers (§14) |
| Role-indistinguishable audits | claimed through v0.9 | Not true once the view states admissible answers; stated as the trade-off it is (§14) |
| Panels chosen by $\rho$, or a $\rho$ floor for duty | proposed after run 3 | Under capture $\rho$ is itself produced by the captured panels: in run 3's stamper-majority reserve the careful minority's $\rho$ ended *below* the stampers'. A remedy that leans on $\rho$ entrenches capture; the plant record is adjudicated by truth instead (§11.2) |
| A single plant reliability $\rho^P$ over good and bad plants, floor 0.5 | 1.2.0-rc.1 | Good plants are free passes for an approver: a reviewer approving half the bad plants scored $\rho^P\approx0.63$, eligible for ever while contributing five times the target negligence. Eligibility is defined on the quantity the clock measures |
| Plants to newcomers at twice the rate | 1.2.0-rc.1 | Cannot coexist with round-robin assignment — the orchestrator cannot aim a plant — and would itself be a signal; $p_{\text{start}}=1$ does the work |
| The plant record cleared on return from suspension | 1.2.0-rc.2 | Would have readmitted every stamper with a clean slate for the ~50 reviews a record takes to re-form, leaving panels stamper-majority at about 70% strength indefinitely; the record persists (§11.2) |
| The actor's identity in the reviewer's view | v0.1–1.1.0 | A plant actor named there would be known to every reviewer after the first revealed plant; the reviewer judges the work, not the worker (§14) |
| Suspension from duty alone | 1.2.0–1.2.1 | Run 4: exclusion from duty freed the negligent to take the awards while the honest carried the reviewing load at the ordinary fee — a reward; suspension is from the market (§5.2, §11.2) |
| A duty levy — a suspended actor keeps winning awards against a cash levy paid to the reviewers | proposed for 1.3.0 | Softer and preserves throughput, but prices negligence as an option, keeps the negligent inside the market as competitors of the honest, and converges more slowly under an adapting population; may return as a later softening |
| The audit taper from the start for newcomers (odds of 1 until five approvals in a row have stood) | 1.2.0 | Easy to pass by stamping: a third of stampers passed it at once; odds are pinned until the bad-plant record is determined (§8.3) |
| Readmission on the outcomes since suspension alone | 1.3.0-rc.1 | Two records for one actor; the §6 example then made a reformed stamper panel-eligible at once (0.17 ≤ 0.2) when its trailing record was 0.5; one record, one meaning (§11.2) |
| Plant capital counting the award and the guarantee | 1.2.0 | $\Omega$ paying itself; 30 of the 45 tied up per plant bound nothing real, and the clock saturated on bookkeeping (§7.6) |
| A member seat held for probationers' audits; probation paused when no member is free to audit; a provisional approval on its own | proposed for 1.4.0 | Spends the scarce thing on standby; stalls first looks for a shortage not the probationer's; converts the shortage into waiting rather than supplying what is short — the provisional approval survives as the obligation of §8.3 (§7.7) |
| Pricing a troop's delivery at the customer's ceiling | proposed for 1.4.0 | Rewards the state for scarcity; the troop delivers at the trailing market price (§12.3) |
| "Unverified by the market" as a reading that lets a troop-reviewed troop delivery vest unaudited | 1.4.0-rc.1 | Contradicted the obligation it sat beside and I25; the audit of the state's own work is an obligation like any other, a member's only, and the reading is cases held for it (§12.3) |
| A troop's acceptance of a verdict recorded at the response deadline | 1.4.0-rc.2 | The window exists for a party that might appeal; a troop has nothing to decide, and waiting it out delayed settlement and the troop's release against §12.3's minimization — recorded at the verdict (the external reviewer's note at acceptance) |
| A backed credit: $v_t=\mathrm{Res}_t/M_t$, the value-neutral purchase, the halt state | v0.2–1.7.1 | The backing was fiction — $10^7$ against a market in the thousands; $v_t$ moved 0.05% in a thousand ticks, and nothing in the loop could make a credit worth more or less. 2.0.0: a credit is worth the work it buys, $P_0/P_t$ (§4.4) |
| The 1.5–1.7 solvency machinery: $A_T$, $R_T$, the plant reserve, $\Omega$'s order of payment, the troop account, the liability stock | 1.4.0–1.7.1 | They rationed a cash rule imposed on $\Omega$ as a stand-in for servers that cost real dollars; with the backing gone, the state creates for a required payment and retires from every inflow, on the public record (§10.4) |
| Emergency emission against a backing; the server budget as the state's bound inside the ledger | proposed for 2.0.0 | There is no backing; a server budget inside the ledger contaminates the economy with a cost that is not the market's — the bound is $N_T$, a parameter outside it (§7.7) |
| Effort costs indexed to the price level | 2.0.0-rc.1 | A slack market would deflate without limit — bids at cost, cost following the price; the agents' costs stay nominal and anchor the price (§4.6) |
| The state's delivery at a margin ($c^D_T=0.5\,\bar B$) | 1.4.0–1.7.1 | With the funds check moot and volume unbounded a riot would be the state's windfall; the state delivers at cost (§7.7); the margin is the control |
| Delegation in the runs of 2.0.0 | 2.0.0-rc.1 | With one cost for every actor there is no gain from trade, and the parent judges its child; deferred to 2.1.0 with heterogeneous costs |
| The arrival float as a grant | 2.3.0–2.6.1 | Run 23: a name made to be lost carried the float out and a fresh name was given a fresh one — thirteen times a run, 655 real for one slot; a loan of the bank's since 2.7.0, repaid from income first and recovered at departure (Appendix T) |
| Departure refused while the float is unrepaid; a smaller float; no float; the float at admission; the loan forgiven at admission; an entry bond; pricing identity | 2.7.0-rc.1 | A dead name on the roll; harvested at its size; no arrival seated (run 17); the same as no float; a grant at the admission rate; deters the honest poor; not the rules' to do — the rules can stop paying for a name, which the loan does |
| The busy-line protocol as a rule | proposed for 1.8.0 | The price is the busy-line signal; what remains is the below-market flag (§5.1) and the capacity case (§12.3), recorded |

## Appendix C. Notation

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $q$, $\operatorname{root}(q)$, $\beta_q$ | a request, its root, its maximum delivery price |
| $B,\tau,G$ | delivery price, delivery time, guarantee |
| $\varphi_q=\varphi^E_q+\varphi^F_q,\ \omega_q$ | verification deposit — expense allowance and fee pot — and service fee |
| $f,\ f_r=w_r f,\ \mathrm{Owed}_q,\ s_q$ | ordinary-review fee, fee by role, the fees owed to a case's surviving reviews, the fee-pot scale |
| $D,K,G^V$ | court fee, reimbursement cap, reviewer bond |
| $W,T_{\mathrm{resp}},T_{\mathrm{rev}},T_{\mathrm{evid}},T_{\mathrm{review}},T_{\mathrm{wait}}$ | the windows |
| $E_t,w_r,e_v$ | emission, role weight, a review's emission share |
| $\mathrm{Res}_t,M_t,v_t,c_r,p_r(t)$ | the backing (retired 2.0.0, held at 0), supply, the credit's value ($P_0/P_t$ since 2.0.0), real resource cost, credit price |
| $W_i,U_i,L_i$ | total, unlocked, locked balance |
| $\Omega$ | the operating account |
| $\rho_i,\kappa_i,p_j$ | reliability, duty credit, audit rate |
| $S_\theta,\lambda$ | the fulfillment predictor, the price weight |
| $X_0,X_t,V_{\max},\mathcal V_t,\mathcal R_t$ | initial and sized reserve, positions per path, duty set, review-eligible set |
| $\pi_t,\beta_P,\hat F_t,F^*$ | plant rate, bad-plant share, the reserve's bad-plant approval rate and its target (1.2.0) |
| $m_t,\gamma,m_{\max}$ | effort multiplier, its gain and cap (1.2.0) |
| $\hat a_i,\hat r_i,n_{\min},n_P,\theta_{\text{panel}},\theta_{\text{duty}},T_{\text{susp}}$ | an actor's smoothed bad-plant approval and good-plant rejection rates, the record's determination count and window, the two eligibility thresholds, the suspension length (1.2.0) |
| member, suspended, probationer; $E_t$ | an actor's standing in the market; the number of members eligible for duty (1.3.0) |
| duty fund, uplift | the dial's raised tax, $(m_t-1)\omega_0 B$ per award, and its payout to settling reviews by role weight (1.3.0); the troops' wallet under 1.4.0–1.6.0; the uplift fund alone again since 1.7.0 |
| troop account; $\Omega$'s income by source | the troops' wallet since 1.7.0 — advances and troop earnings in; costs, locks, forfeits, burns, repayments and the surplus above $R_T$ to $\Omega$ out; $\Omega$'s two incomes (base fee, troop surplus), its own money's flows (advances made, repaid) and its outflows (rebates, liabilities serviced, plant capital), per period (1.7.0) |
| troop; $\varepsilon_T$; $w_R,w_A$; $c_T,c^D_T$; $\bar B$; $R_T$; $A_T$ | the orchestrator's own reviewer or deliverer; its declared error rate; the seat and award windows; its review and delivery costs; the trailing market price; the fund's troop reserve — since 1.5.0 the current period's peak troop demand; $\Omega$'s advance cap (1.4.0) |
| $D_s$ | the troop demand at seating $s$: capital held by live troops plus the cost of the summonses refused for funds, its two components held and refused (1.5.0) |
| service clock; $T_s$; $r_s$; on-time share; lateness; rebate; $\Omega$'s liability | a root request's ticks from posting to its first verdict while $\beta\ge\bar B$; the service target; the rebate a tick late; the readings of the network rating; what $\Omega$ owes a requester and could not pay at the stop (1.6.0) |
| determined record; plant reserve; reserve target | $\ge n_{\min}$ bad plants adjudicated (1.3.0 §2.2, required of a panel-seat holder since 1.6.0); the capital $\Omega$ holds back for the honesty clock; the reviews waiting that size the reserve (1.6.0) |
| troop-ticks; troop shortfall; held for the market's audit | the readings of §12.3 (1.4.0) |
| $P_t$, $P_0$; real wealth | the price level — the trailing mean awarded price on $\bar B$'s window — and its base 20; $W\cdot P_0/P_t$ (2.0.0) |
| $c_D$; reservation price; adaptive reservation | a member's nominal effort cost per task, burned at submission; the agents' own costs as the anchor; the variant that follows $P_t$ with a lag (2.0.0) |
| issuance, creation; retirement, departure; deficit outstanding; $\Omega$'s net position | the sources and sinks of 2.0.0; created less retired; $\Omega_U$ less the deficit (2.0.0; creation and retirement retired 2.1.0) |
| $\mathfrak B$ the bank; `loan`, `repayment`; `mint_plant`, `retire_plant`; `mint_scenario` | the second account that alone creates and destroys; its loan to the state against a summons and its repayment; a plant's minted pots and their destruction; the declared shock (2.1.0) |
| loans outstanding; debt; liabilities; $\Delta_{\max}$ the wall; $\Omega$'s net position | capital in flight (open summonses' loans); settled summonses' unrepaid loans; rebates owed; one period of $\Omega$'s fee income; $\Omega_U-\text{debt}-\text{liabilities}$ (2.1.0) |
| $b_T$; the expected cost; the cost of truth | the trailing fail rate of the state's deliveries; $\max(0,(1-b_T)\,p_T-b_TG)$, the burn (at $\bar B$ before 2.4.0); the plants' minted pots less what returned, with emission (2.1.0) |
| refused for credit; the mark | a delivery the bank would not lend for above the wall; the request's record of a refusal — its kind and tick, readable (2.1.0) |
| claim; the pool; forgone; forgone share; $\Omega$ retained | the rebate as the measure of lateness, recorded at the stop; the period's fee income after the debt, a lock on $\Omega$; the part of a claim the pool did not cover; forgone over claims; what the claims left of the pool (2.2.0; *liabilities* and the *liability stock* retired) |
| `arrive`, `depart`; `arrival`; $F_0$; an arrival; admission; time to membership | the two public operations; the float's kind; a probationer with no record and a bank-minted float; its passage to membership on $n_{\min}$ bad plants; the ticks from arrival to admission (2.3.0) |
| the arrival loan $L$; `arrival_repayment`; the default on arrivals; loans to names outstanding; the wallet out $W_U-\min(W_U,L)$; income and a return | the float as the bank's loan on a name; the sink — repaid from income, recovered at departure; the bank's loss on a name that left short, a reading; the live names' unrepaid floats, the bank's second line; what a departing name takes out; what repays the loan and what passes untouched, fixed against the engine's events (2.7.0) |
| competence; the spread $\sigma$; the entry pool; the reservation $r$ | an actor's own effort cost per unit of work, drawn once, private; the log-normal's width; the candidates reading the view; real earnings a tick below which one stays out or leaves (2.3.0) |
| a broker; the gain from trade; a node's closure; the parent's pots | a parent delegating most of its awards (a reading); $B-B'-$ overhead; when a case and everything under it have settled; the nested case's own two pots (2.3.0) |
| the posting lock; $\beta_{\max}$, $K_{\text{open}}$; below market; raise the ceiling | the all-in maximum a root locks at posting; the drivers' ceiling policy; the flag and the requester's operation (2.0.0) |
| *a missed delivery*; the deadline tick | §9.1's terminal state — final $\mathsf N$ without a submission, the guarantee forfeited; since 2.5.0 the deadline tick is the child's: it may submit on it, the parent may not cancel at or after it (2.5.0) |
| *the plant's seats*; *a plant failure at any seat*; *the resubmitted truth*; *the second marker*; *the state's seat on a plant* | since 2.8.0 the audit, the third seat, the appeal, the dispute and the resubmission a plant reaches (§7.6); 1.2.0's plant failure at whichever seat the verdict was given; the truth the instrument draws for a revised plant from the trailing real revisions' outcomes; its sealed commitment at the resubmission; the orchestrator's own verdict on a plant's seat past $w_R$ |
| *the planted approval*; *the plant reviewer alias*; *the system reviewer*; *the planted lie*; *the wrong step*; *the dial per seat* ($m^A, m^{La}, m^{Ld}$); *the planted-approval credit*; the plant outcome *planted* | since 2.9.0: a bad plant marked at posting whose initial seat is nobody's and whose F is the orchestrator's own — a system review (a troop or a plant reviewer alias: no actor, no record, no emission, no uplift; the alias paid no fee and not adjudicated) under `plant_rev:`, an alias of the plant reviewer wallet — audited with certainty at odds 1; a plant's challenge against its truth (the plant actor's appeal of a bad plant's N, the plant requester's dispute of a good plant's F), adjudicated by truth; the steps a case takes only through someone's dishonesty, taken by the plant at the orchestrator's rate; one multiplier per planted wrong step, each moving its rate alone; the credit of $\pi^A$ per real pending approval |
| *the plant's life*; *time in the market*; *the seen set*; *tell-readers*; *opaque identifier* | since 2.6.0 a plant is posted, available, awarded on the market's report and submitted at a drawn tick (§7.6); its ticks from posting to award; what a free member saw available and when; the hand-written readers that are the indistinguishability test's instrument; a case's public identifier, a hash carrying no order (§7.6, I40) |
| $\underline{B}$; $p_T$; *below the state's reservation* | the state's reservation — its floor, nominal and public, read beside $\bar B$ the market's level; the state's price $\max(\bar B,\underline{B})$; the mark on a request posted or raised below the floor, with its tick (2.4.0). $R_T$ stays the fund's troop reserve of 1.5.0–1.7.0 (retired 2.0.0) |
| $N_T$; places; refused for capacity; told | the most troops ELEF can field at once; one for a seat, two for a delivery until its inspection is seated; the capacity case and its record (2.0.0; since 2.1.0 every refusal marks the request and is told) |
| a crowd; the deficit shock | a riot as requesters arriving with wallets (a demand event); a created sum paid to the requesters (a money event) — the drivers' declared scenarios (2.0.0) |
| $\mathsf F,\mathsf N,\mathsf P,\bot$ | the four verdicts |
| $Z_q$ | the accepted settlement outcome |

## Appendix D. Provenance

Discussion anchors [C0]–[C12] (October 15, 2026) settled the purpose and boundary, fixed-then-floating prices, learned selection and the guarantee, verification and appeal, the reserve and rotation, money entering with requests and emission funding verification, symmetric reviewer liability, reliability versus duty credit, and requesters and delegation. Eight external reviews [R1]–[R8] of v0.2 through v0.9 found, in order: the funding gap, bond-versus-liability and the stamping overclaim; the payout-after-shortfall and indexing gaps; atomicity, occupancy, replacement, cost units and the Ω claims; the interaction defects of those repairs; the boundary defects of those; the follow-through defects; the correction-logic and interface defects; and the consequences of reopening. A ninth review [R9] of v0.10 found no new execution defect in its bounded checks, corrected the "blindness stands" claim to the trade-off stated in §14, and recommended this consolidation and the change discipline of §19. A tenth review [R10] of v1.0 accepted it as the frozen baseline, recommended keeping it frozen, and found four places where the consolidated wording said more or less than the decisions it consolidates — the two senses of "ruling", when a response releases capacity, what moves credit value and whether halting is a consequence or a hypothesis, and the two kinds of shortfall at settlement; each is corrected in place and recorded in Appendix E. The reference implementation, `agentic_sim/`, encodes every rule here with injected decisions, checks the nineteen invariants (eighteen through 1.0.1) after every tick, and carries every reviewer's test file verbatim — 156 passing (four added with v1.0.1 for the one permitted operation no suite had exercised, withdrawing an unawarded request), with 24 expected failures each stating why, strict and parameter-exact in both entry points. The `_line` map "The actor and its economic life" (revision 4) records the Section 1 discussion and is superseded by the map of this baseline. **1.1.0.** The first scoped run of the frozen baseline [S1] (October 16, 2026; §18.1 question 1, ten runs) found that careful review did not beat stamping at the defaults, through the fee: 1.0.1's remainder rule charged care against the reviewer's own fee. Design revision 1 — the verification deposit is two pots — was proposed the same day as a release candidate, read on its own through two implementation reviews [I1], [I2] (October 17; the per-case "unowed fee money" defect in trees, the I10 and record-mode consequences, the reviewer-income consequence, the appeals qualification of §7.3, the test list, the rerun's power; then the net-cost figure, who the fee pot's remainder pays, and the provenance), accepted as rc.3 on October 17, and consolidated here. The engine implements it; the suite is 181 passing with 25 expected failures (the twenty-fifth: the fifth review's unfunded-dispute test, which pinned the 1.0.1 deposit through the human's budget; its principle is re-asserted in `test_v11` at the 1.1.0 pay-in); the golden History heads were re-pinned once, at the implementing commit. **1.2.0.** Runs 2 and 3 of the 1.1.0 baseline [S2] (October 17, 2026; 1,295 runs, `agentic_sim/results/studies/1.1.0-stamping/` and `1.1.0-combined/`) established that each direction of reviewer error has a natural challenger and the loop is fair only when both are present (break-even dispute rate ≈ 0.07; careful review pays above ≈ 92% accuracy), that a stamper-majority reserve captures the loop and nothing inside it recovers it ($\rho$ itself corrupted), and that bond size cannot carry deterrence. Design revision 2 — the loop's own clock — was written from the design discussion of the same day [C-rev2] (planted work as "a real dial", under the assumption that humans do not intervene) and the October 14 proposal map's question on known-answer audits [P-oct14], read on its own through three implementation reviews [I3]–[I5] (the actor in the reviewer's view; I9 against I21 when eligible actors run short; $\rho^P$ mixing good and bad plants; newcomer plant-doubling against round-robin; the solvency figure; $\Omega$'s working capital; the audit cap; the channels of the hypothesis; the record persisting through suspension; one newcomer audit rule; eligibility at assignment; the period on adjudications), accepted on October 17, and consolidated here. The engine implements it; the suite is 197 passing with 26 expected failures (the twenty-sixth: the third review's test 18, which pinned $\Omega$'s float at 20); the golden History heads were re-pinned once, at the implementing commit, on a scenario that now plants work. **1.3.0.** Run 4 of the 1.2.0 baseline [S4] (October 17, 2026; 300 runs, `agentic_sim/results/studies/1.2.0-clock/`) established that the clock keeps the loop honest with nobody watching, at a capacity price, and that suspension from duty was a reward: at 60/40 the eight careful actors carried 500–800 reviews each at a fee of 2 while the twelve suspended stampers, freed from duty, took the awards at 20 and ended richer in 19 of 20 seeds. Design revision 3 — inspection is membership — was written from the design discussion of the same day [C-rev3] ("if you get thrown out of inspection you are banned from receiving a new task; to get tasks back you have to prove you are doing good inspection for a while"), read on its own through two implementation reviews [I7]–[I8] (readmission stated two ways with §6 following the wrong one; the cap as a standing predicate against mid-review completion and the panel floor at small memberships; sizing on a population mean rather than applied odds; the refill order for turns; the invariant consequences of netted plant capital; the panel floor bounded by availability), accepted on October 17, and consolidated here. The engine implements it; the suite is 208 passing with 26 expected failures; the golden History heads were re-pinned once, at the implementing commit. **1.4.0.** Run 5 of the 1.3.0 baseline [S5] (October 17, 2026; 360 runs, `agentic_sim/results/studies/1.3.0-membership/`) established that the gate holds everywhere and that a captured fixed population is honest and short of hands: awards unfilled for want of a free member in 816–899 of 1,000 ticks at fixed splits, and at $m_{\max}=2$ two thirds of the false approvals were probationers' whose pinned audits found no member free — a leak older than probation, since the drivers had dropped a rolled audit that found no auditor since run 1. Design revision 4 — the orchestrator's troops — was written from the design discussion of the same day [C-rev4] ("the orchestrator is like the government; it has troops — not paid as reviewers, employees of ELEF; honest by construction; and ELEF should optimize to minimize the troops at any time and retract them as early as possible"), read on its own through two implementation reviews [I10]–[I11] (the "unverified" reading against the obligation and I25; the engine summons and the driver answers; the troop's cost as a resource purchase; the held case accepted and retried; why minor; three figures; the obligation as a conformance correction of the drivers; panels with troops; $A_T$; Hypothesis 4's size), accepted on October 17 with the external reviewer's one note (acceptance at the verdict), and consolidated here. The engine implements it; the suite is 218 passing with 26 expected failures; the golden History heads were re-pinned once, at the implementing commit, on a scenario that now rolls audits as obligations and summons troops. **1.5.0–1.7.0.** Run 6 [S6] (October 17, 2026; 940 runs) found the 1.4.0 reserve self-limiting — design revision 5 sized it to the need (two implementation reviews, [I12]–[I13]; suite 225 + 26); run 7 [S7] (482 runs) found the reviewing seat, not the treasury, capping the market, and the probation leak the plant record's lag on the auditor — design revision 6, auto-scale, five rules behind flags (two reviews, [I14]–[I15]; suite 236 + 26); run 8 [S8] (October 17–18; 840 runs) found the panel rule the honesty gain, $\Omega$'s backing the capacity gain, and the clock's liability a stock $\Omega$ could not pay down while the troops' earnings went to the reviewers — design revision 7, the state's earnings, written from the design discussion of October 18 [C-rev7] (Baruchi: "why should an inspector profit from something a troop did?"), read through two implementation reviews [I16]–[I17] (the $\omega_0$ sweep moving the rebate; the cap's cost in plants unfunded; $R_T$'s figure and purpose; the surcharge's size; a review seat's earnings — the last corrected again at implementation, Appendix L), accepted on October 18 and consolidated here. The engine implements it; the suite is 244 passing with 26 expected failures; the golden History heads were re-pinned once, at the implementing commit.

**2.0.0 (October 18, 2026).** Eleven runs at a fixed price, and run 11's hard spike as the case the price answers [S1–S11]; the design discussion [C-2.0] — Baruchi: "the servers needs to be decoupled from the economy. otherwise we contaminate it"; "maybe it is time we introduce floating prices"; "if I print more money then money is worth less, if money is worth less you get less on the work that you do"; "we are missing something to balance this money with and the actors have nothing to 'buy' with their credits"; and earlier, "the reserve is not real… the reserve is made up just like everything else" — on whose frame design revision 8 was written after the design reviewer withdrew two prior answers (emergency emission against a backing; the server budget inside the ledger); three implementation reviews [I17]–[I19] (`spec/design_revisions/dr8_2.0.0-rc.{1,2,3}-review.md`), all points adopted; two rulings at acceptance (Appendix M).


**2.8.0 (October 26, 2026).** Run 34 [S34] — the reviewers in self-play: one basin from two starts, care 0.48 / approve blind 0.51, the plant-blind seats stamped 94–97%, the standing false approvals nine to ten times the hand's, the audit layer's overturn rate below the hand's — "the auditors are the population; the plants alone keep the market alive"; the design reviewer's classification, a design revision, after two cells and a diagnostic. Run 35 [S35] — the finding to the decimal on the corrected instrument after the plant path's fourth, fifth and sixth conformance corrections and erratum 2.6.2; the dispute layer captured with the audit layer (69–75% against 97%); the half at 0.70 / 0.29; the plant's life matched at every stage, period-matched. Run 26 [S26] — Q1's reading of the "resubmissions", corrected: no case in runs 1–35 was ever revised; the seats were audits and panels. The design discussion [C-2.8]: the design reviewer — "today we only check the front door; 2.8.0 puts a check at every door"; Baruchi — "a plant of fake things for every seat? work, review, dispute?" — "reviewers, not workers: workers are already tested by review"; "ok, let's draft it." The implementation reviews [I34] (rc.1: implementable with four corrections where the text met the engine — the court fee and $\Omega$'s advance, the resubmitted truth re-sealed and the empty window, the state's seat on a plant past $w_R$, the roll at the odds of the verdict — six corrections of fact and nine readings, all adopted in rc.2) and [I35] (rc.2: ready for acceptance; the roll's parenthesis, the seat pass's per-seat test, the top-up as the shortfall and the court fee's source taken as four phrases at acceptance). Appendix U.

**2.9.0 (October 27, 2026).** Run 36 [S36] — 2.8.0's §8.2: the capture closes by more than half and not to the hand's; the audit seat still stamped 77–98% by every all-member learner. Run 37 [S37] — §8.3–§8.7: honesty unmoved by hand and the cost of truth +18–72%; the audit seat blind to the probe; the plant rate not the lever on the audit seat (82–98% stamped at π 0.10 and 0.40, dial live or off); the lying challengers paid at the appeal seat (23–31% at the case); the `log` tell reclassified a conformance item of the public view and landed with its class (notes §61–§63). The ruling after run 37: 2.9.0 proposed — the plant takes the wrong step; the three facts of §62. rc.1 (October 27) reviewed by the implementer [I36]: three corrections, four facts, ten readings; rc.2 took them whole and read clean [I37]; accepted the same day and implemented behind two flags (Appendix V).

**2.7.0 (October 21, 2026).** Run 23's re-arrival cell [S23] — the learned reviewer-worker's harvest of the arrival float by cycling names: +270 ± 89 on the brief's lifetime measure, −373 ± 86 on its own earnings, thirteen floats a run, 655 real minted for one slot; the hand-written harvester and leaver bounding the learned mix; the implementer's reading that the finding points at 2.3.0's float, not the taper; the design discussion [C-2.7] — the design reviewer: "the float becomes a loan from the bank, exactly the way the bank already lends to the state in 2.1.0"; the two implementation reviews (rc.1 → rc.2: the income table fixed against the engine's events, three corrections of fact, eight readings — `spec/design_revisions/dr15_2.7.0-rc.{1,2}-review.md`); accepted as written, October 21, 2026.

## Appendix E. Errata applied in v1.0.1

Four textual corrections from the tenth review [R10]. Each clarifies a decision already made; none changes a rule, a default, a permitted operation, or the engine. Classification under §19: consolidation wording, not design and not conformance — the engine was checked against each item and already behaves as the corrected text says.

| # | Where | v1.0 said | v1.0.1 says | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | §1, §15.2, closing | "Only a human rules," while §8.4 has the orchestrator rule on a split; §1 credited every root requester, delegates included, with non-model judgment | The orchestrator rules on procedural disagreements (splits); only a human may issue the override; a delegate's judgment is a model's and is graded as such (§15.3) | The two senses of "ruling" contradicted each other read literally; the label grades already kept the distinction |
| E2 | §2.2 | An actor is committed "until it has responded to its initial verdict; it is then pending — execution capacity released" | Approval or an admitted appeal releases capacity; choosing to revise or supply evidence keeps the actor committed through that work and its assessment; terminal closure releases the commitment | "Respond" includes revise and supply-evidence, which return the actor to work; the engine holds `RESUBMITTING` as executing and a standalone reader would otherwise free the actor on "revise" |
| E3 | §4.4, §18.1(7) | "Purchases never move the price; only emission does"; busywork "is self-terminating" stated as a consequence | Purchases are value-neutral; emission lowers credit value, bond burning raises it, backed issuance and transfers leave it unchanged. The halt state is a definition; that a rootless economy reaches it is the hypothesis of §18.1(7) | The sentence contradicted the table above it; the rules bound real spending but do not force it to exhaust the reserve |
| E4 | §4.3 step 3 | "What the pool cannot cover becomes a reviewer claim on $\Omega$" | An unpaid reviewer reimbursement becomes a named claim on $\Omega$; an advance $\Omega$ paid but could not recover is an unrecovered advance borne by $\Omega$ and creates no reviewer claim | The v1.0 sentence admitted a double-payment reading; the engine already writes the unrecovered advance down against $\Omega$ and files claims only for unpaid reimbursements |
