### Errata applied in 2.6.1 (October 21, 2026)

One textual correction, the design reviewer's ruling after run 24 (Appendix S). It clarifies a construction already in force; no rule, default or permitted operation of a member changes, and I39 is unchanged in substance. Classification under §19: consolidated wording that said less than the decision (the plant "waits as a real root waits"), corrected as errata.

| # | Where | 2.6.0 said | 2.6.1 says | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E5 | §7.6, "Rule — the plant's life" | "a ceiling drawn from the trailing distribution of real roots' ceilings (the last closed period's, or two)" | "a ceiling drawn from the trailing distribution of real roots' ceilings **as posted** (a rolling window of the last $k$ real roots', $k$ the trailing period's count) … **the plant requester then raises the ceiling as the population's requesters raise theirs** … so that the plant waits as a real root waits" | Run 24's probe re-read, after the instrument's posting was corrected, still read a case-level separation of 0.016–0.019 of AUC whose largest piece was the time a root was seen available before its award: a real root posted below the market is raised by its requester after a wait (35 % of awards), a plant's ceiling — drawn from the ceilings real roots were *awarded* at — never was, so the plant was taken at once more often and, when it was not, waited to $w_A$. "Drawn once" was the initial draw; the raise is the requester's step a real root takes, and the plant takes it too. |

### Errata applied in 2.6.2 (October 25, 2026)

One textual correction, the design reviewer's ruling after run 34's addendum (Appendix S), landed with the two conformance corrections of the same ruling. It clarifies a construction already in force — the plant's ceiling is drawn from the real roots' — by fixing the unit of the draw; no rule, default or permitted operation of a member changes, and I39 is unchanged in substance. Classification under §19: consolidated wording, an erratum.

| # | Where | 2.6.1 said | 2.6.2 says | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E6 | §7.6, "Rule — the plant's life" | "a ceiling drawn from the trailing distribution of real roots' ceilings **as posted** (a rolling window of the last $k$ real roots' …)" | "a ceiling drawn from the trailing distribution of real roots' ceilings as posted, **relative to the market price at their posting** — β/B̄, the drawn ratio applied to the $\bar B$ of the plant's own posting" — the raise unchanged (the plant requester raises to the current market price as the population's requesters do) | The plant's life by stage in the learned reviewer population (run 34's addendum, notes §50): the plants were raised 10 points more often than the real roots where the price trended up (+0.100 ± 0.010; +0.013 in run 24's world, −0.011 where it trended down) — a real root's ceiling is the market rule at the live $\bar B$, a plant's was a β drawn from a window that lagged it, so under a trend the plants posted apart from the market and were told by their raises (the ceiling alone separated plant from real at AUC 0.59–0.60). The unit of the draw is the ratio to the market; the window keeps it as posted. |

## Appendix F. Design revision 1 — the verification deposit is two pots (1.1.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr1_two_pots_1.1.0-rc.3.md`, kept verbatim; this appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S1].** Strategy run 1 (October 16, 2026): at the defaults, with half the reserve stamping, stampers ended richer in four of five seeds; 47% of truly failed work was paid in full, 88% with nine stampers in ten. The channel was the fee: a careful reviewer earned 7.9 per review and a stamper 9.5 — a gap equal to the careful reviewer's own expenses.

**Mechanism.** Under 1.0.1 §4.3 the deposit was one pot doing two jobs: reimbursing capped expenses, and paying *what remained* as the fee. Every credit reimbursed was a credit of fee lost. The rule charged for diligence at the margin, while 1.0.1 §7.3 still reasoned with the fixed fee of v0.1 R16–R17 ("capped expense reimbursement plus a fixed fee … for review work, not for the answer"), which v0.3 had replaced by the remainder when it repaired the second review's shortfall finding. The accounting closed; the incentive inverted.

**Classification.** A design revision motivated by a strategy finding and justified by the spec's own §7.3 assuming a fee its §4.3 did not pay — the case §19 permits when the revision is labelled, read on its own, and reviewed before implementation. It restores a decision already on the record. The selection tie band and the audit floor, which the run also implicated, were left as defaults, to be swept separately.

**What changed.** §4.1 pay-in; §4.3 whole (two pots; expenses from the allowance alone; fixed fees by role from the fee pot, scaled only when short; the cross-pot step and the refund at tree closure; $\Omega$ as the pooled expense fund); §7.3 (compensation and the restated hypothesis, with the appeals term); §10.3–10.4; I10's two exceptions, I12 (both pots are nominals), I17's note and the new I19; §18.1 questions 1 and 3; §20 re-settled; Appendices A and C. Defaults: $f=2$, $arphi^E=10$, $arphi^F=5$; under consideration $K=3$, $arphi^E=6$.

**Found in review and adopted.** rc.1 computed "unowed fee money" per case, which in a tree would pay a root's expenses with money its nested case's reviewers were still owed; rc.2 moved the cross-pot step to tree closure. rc.1 said "nothing else moves"; rc.2 recorded that reviewer income from deposits falls by about three quarters in the common case and that I10 and record mode gain the tree consequences. rc.1 stated the hypothesis unconditionally; rc.2 qualified it to the no-appeals regime and added the appeals term. rc.3 settled the requester's net-cost figure, that the fee pot's remainder pays the tree's claimants directly, and the provenance. The revision's own text calls the post-vesting invariant "I4"; in this baseline's numbering it is I10 (I4 is the court-fee invariant).

**Engine.** The ledger's case pool holds the allowance (the field keeps its 1.0.1 name, `deposit`) and the fee pot; settlement follows §4.3 in order and reads the root's pool for a nested case; the refund waits for tree closure; I19 is checked per tick; a root with open pots stays resident in record mode. Reviewer-authored tests were not edited: one is superseded (above); the rest pass unchanged.

**After acceptance.** Rerun strategy run 1 with only this rule changed — twenty seeds or more, at $X\in\{4,8\}$ and $f\in\{2,4\}$, the $X=4$, $f=2$ cell the like-for-like comparison — then the default sweep (band, audit floor, bond), the appeals run, and the requester-dispute run, in that order.

---

## Appendix G. Design revision 2 — the loop's own clock (1.2.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr2_the_loops_own_clock_1.2.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.3 with its one consolidation fix); the three implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S2].** Runs 2 and 3 (October 17, 2026; 1,295 runs under 1.1.0). The actor polices rejections (appeals, $a\approx0.98$); the requester polices approvals (disputes). With appeals alone a careful reviewer's 5% false rejections are punished almost every time while a stamper's negligence is caught about 3% of the time, and stampers end richer (+200 per actor at 50/50, $X=8$); with the requester disputing one bad result in four, careful ends richer by 489; break-even is a dispute rate of about 0.07, and careful review pays only above an accuracy of about 92% at that rate. At 10/90 every panel is stamper-majority: disputes fail (23 of 135 won), only the careful minority's errors are adjudicated, their $\rho$ ends below the stampers', selection hands the stampers the awards (159 to 66), and no dispute rate recovers it. At $G^V=20$ about 7% of duty slot-ticks are held by actors who cannot fund the bond and the reserve starves.

**Mechanism.** Every signal the loop produced was panel-produced, so under capture the loop was blind to its own state; the design assumption that humans do not intervene removes the one external oracle the baseline leaned on. The orchestrator therefore owns some truth itself: the truth of the work it writes. Planted work (§7.6) gives a measurement no panel can paint; the plant record (§11.2) turns it into eligibility a captured majority cannot protect; the dial (§12.2) turns it into effort that rises with the threat and eases without it, and into the reserve's size and the security tax.

**Classification.** A design revision motivated by strategy findings (runs 2–3), labelled and read on its own. It adds a mechanism; it does not alter any settlement, verdict or challenge rule of 1.1.0. It changes what participants may do (the orchestrator creates plants; an actor can be suspended from duty; no challenge is admitted on a plant), what they may see (§14), and makes two fixed quantities endogenous ($X$, $\omega$).

**What changed.** §1 (the design assumption); §2.1, §2.4; §4.1 ($\omega_t$); §7.1, §7.5 (labelled), §7.6 (new); §8.3 defaults; §11.2 (new); §12 (whole: §12.1 the reserve holding $\min(X_t,\text{eligible})$ and the eligibility shortfall, §12.2 the dial, the alarm, working capital); §14 (the actor leaves the view); §15.3 (the plant grade); §16; I9 amended, I13's note, I20 and I21 new; §18.1 questions 1, 3, 6 amended, 9 and 10 new; §20 (planted work); Appendices A, B, C. Defaults: $\pi_0=0.10$, $\pi_{\max}=0.40$, $\beta_P=0.75$, $F^*=0.10$, $\gamma=1$, period 50 adjudicated plants or 200 ticks, $m_{\max}=4$, $n_{\min}=4$, $n_P=20$, $\theta_{\text{panel}}=0.20$, $\theta_{\text{duty}}=0.50$, $T_{\text{susp}}=100$, $p_{\text{start}}=p_{\text{cap}}=1.0$, $\Omega_0=300$.

**Found in review and adopted.** rc.1 named a plant actor in a view that carried the actor (§14 as it stood); rc.2 removed the actor from the view. rc.1's I9 and I21 could not both hold when eligible actors were fewer than $X_t$; rc.2 let the reserve run short and count it. rc.1's single plant reliability $\rho^P$ let a reviewer approving half the bad plants stay eligible for ever; rc.2 defined the record on the quantity the clock measures, in two tiers. rc.1 doubled the plant rate for newcomers, which round-robin cannot do without a signal; rc.2 dropped it. rc.1 misstated the solvency figure (29% of slots; it is 7% of slot-ticks), omitted $\Omega$'s working capital (45 per plant), the audit cap and the channels of its hypothesis; rc.2 stated them. rc.2 cleared the record on return from suspension, which would have readmitted stampers with clean slates; rc.3 made the record persist. rc.2 held two rules for a newcomer's audit odds; rc.3 kept §8.3's. rc.2's I21 read eligibility as a standing property while records move mid-review; rc.3 made it a property at assignment, with a suspended actor leaving duty whether or not a replacement exists. rc.3's period counts adjudications, not settlements.

**Found at implementation.** The revision's "at the end of the suspension it is readmitted only if its record passes, and otherwise remains suspended for a further $T_{\text{susp}}$", with the record persisting, makes every suspension permanent: the record is a trailing window of plant outcomes and a suspended actor judges no plants, so a record that failed at suspension cannot pass later. That reading leaves $T_{\text{susp}}$ inert and contradicts the revision's §2.4 ("an actor readmitted from suspension is a newcomer") and its §8 run 2 ("how long does it last once suspended stampers return as newcomers"). The engine implements the smallest reading that gives $T_{\text{susp}}$ its meaning: readmission on probation, to duty and not to panels, until the actor's next plant re-checks the record (§11.2). It was marked in the 1.2.0 text as the implementer's reading and **confirmed as the rule in force by patch 1.2.1** (October 17, 2026), in the sentence design revision 3 rc.1 gives in its Appendix A; design revision 3, if accepted, restates probation in full.

**Engine.** `create_plant` builds the case in the submitted state from two system wallets funded by $\Omega$, with the sealed marker in the History; adjudication at the initial verdict applies the §7.6 table and reveals the marker; every challenge operation refuses a plant; the actor carries the two plant windows, the two tiers, the suspension clock and the probation flag; eligibility is checked in every assignment and at rotation; rotation readmits, removes the ineligible when their review completes, fills to $X_t$ or shrinks to it, and counts the eligibility shortfall; the dial is read once per period on adjudications with its clamps, and sizes $\pi_t$, the applied audit odds, $X_t$ and $\omega_t$; the alarm re-seats by record; the view drops the actor; I20 and I21 are checked per tick; I9, I13 amended. `test_v12` carries the §20 figures and the mechanism's properties; the equivalence scenario plants work and its goldens were re-pinned once. Reviewer-authored tests were not edited: one (the third review's test 18, $\Omega$'s float pinned at 20) is superseded with the reason stated; every other reviewer test passes unchanged.

**After acceptance.** The runs of the revision's §8, in order: reproduce §20's planted-work figures (the test); controller on against off under a drifting stamper share, appeals truthful and no disputes, $X$ free to track; adaptive dispositions; the tipping point at 40/60, 30/70, 20/80 under 1.1.0 and 1.2.0; the red team (camouflaged stampers); $\Omega$'s plant spend and $\omega_t$ across $m$ and the reserve's size trajectory.

## Appendix H. Design revision 3 — inspection is membership (1.3.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr3_inspection_is_membership_1.3.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2 with the panel floor's availability clause); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S4].** Run 4 (October 17, 2026; 300 runs under 1.2.0, truthful appeals and no disputes throughout). Honesty held everywhere: false approvals four- to five-fold below 1.1.0 at every split from 50/50 to 80/20, and under a stamper share drifting to 90% they stopped growing once the dial saturated (83 against a control of 650). It was slow: a case waited for a reviewer 47 times longer than in the control, vesting ended 40% lower, and $\Omega$ could not fund a third of the plants the dial called for at $m_{\max}$. And suspension from duty was a reward: at 60/40 the eight careful actors were the only eligible reviewers, the dial sized the reserve to 7–8, they were on duty almost always — 500 to 800 reviews each at a fee of 2 — while the twelve suspended stampers, freed from duty, took the awards at 20 (delivery income 3,311 against 1,063 per actor; stampers richer in 19 of 20 seeds). Selection could not help, because it runs among free actors and the honest were not free. $\Omega$ banked the raised tax (+5,898 at 60/40) levied for the effort the honest were supplying; and a plant tied up 45 credits of which 30 was $\Omega$ paying itself.

**Mechanism.** The contest was no longer about detection but about who carries the reviewing load and what membership of the market requires. Suspension now suspends membership: no new award until the actor has inspected well, under full audit, for long enough to pass. Probation is the supervised way back; the members' duty cap keeps half the honest free to work while probationers take the initial seats; the dial's raised tax goes to the reviewers through the duty fund; plant capital counts only the pots.

**Classification.** A design revision motivated by a strategy finding, labelled and read on its own. It changes the meaning of an existing state (suspension) and adds a cap and a fund; it alters no settlement, verdict or challenge rule.

**What changed.** §2.1 (the fund); §4.1 (the split of $\omega_t$); §5.2 (membership and the award); §7.1 (probationers initial only); §7.6 (plant capital netted); §8.3 (the pin for probationers and undetermined newcomers, replacing 1.2.0's taper); §10.4 (the fund's flows); §11.2 (suspension from the market; probation restated in full; readmission on the trailing record with $n_{\min}$ new outcomes); §12.1 (two kinds of seat; the cap; the floor bounded by availability; the fill order; turns by kind); §12.2 (sizing on applied odds; member seats; working capital 15; the fund); §16; I21 amended, I22 and I23 new; §18.1 questions 1 and 9 amended, 11 new; §20 (membership); Appendices A, B, C. Defaults: readmission on $\theta_{\text{duty}}$ with $n_{\min}=4$ new outcomes; $T_{\text{susp}}=100$ unchanged; pinned odds; cap $\lceil E_t/2\rceil$; fund $(m_t-1)\omega_0 B$; plant capital 15; $m_{\max}$ 4 swept against 2; $\Omega_0$ 300 swept against 100.

**Found in review and adopted.** rc.1 stated readmission two ways and its worked example followed the wrong one (a reformed stamper panel-eligible at once at 0.17, when the one trailing record was 0.5); rc.2 kept one record, with the count of new outcomes a minimum only. rc.1's cap was a standing per-tick predicate that fails on a correct engine when $E_t$ falls mid-review, and its panel floor of two members contradicted a cap of one at $E_t=2$; rc.2 made the cap a seating rule, let a member above it finish its review, and let the floor yield over the cap. rc.1 sized the reserve on a population mean while probationers and newcomers are audited at 1; rc.2 sizes on applied odds. rc.1 left a turn's refill order unstated; rc.2 refills by kind. rc.1 netted plant capital without saying what became of the escrow and the guarantee lock; rc.2 states the I12/I15/vesting consequences. rc.2's panel floor was bounded by membership; the accepted text bounds it by availability, in the same form as I9. Smaller: probation pays a stamper a little (+1.2 a review) and the award is what deters; the newcomer pin's fresh-population worst case; a careful probationer's 19% restart, intended; the fund in §10.4; 1.2.1 applied the same day.

**Found at implementation — confirmed as patch 1.3.1 (October 17, 2026; design revision 4 rc.1, Appendix A).** Two readings, both marked in the text. (1) *The cap and the reserve floor.* $\lceil E_t/2\rceil$ at six actors is 3, below the $X_0=4$ of Appendix A's initialization and §20's worked example (C, D, E, F on duty): read literally, the baseline's own worlds — and every reviewer test built on them — would violate the revision at tick 0. The engine lets the cap never fall below the reserve's floor $V_{\max}+1$, as the panel floor yields to the cap; the difference from the literal text is confined to memberships of four to seven, where the literal cap would leave two or three members free. One line of the engine. (2) *An outgoing probationer with no free probationer stays.* Replacing it with a member would seat a member in an initial seat that the next tick's "probationers first" hands back — churn, not a rule. One branch of the rotation.

**Engine.** `award` refuses a non-member before any effect and records membership at award; after $T_{\text{susp}}$ the actor is a probationer — initial reviews only, odds pinned, re-checked at every plant, readmitted on the trailing record with the $n_{\min}$ counter; `_rotate_membership` seats member seats first, lets probationers displace idle members from initial seats, refills turns by kind, keeps the cap at seating and the floor by availability, and counts the eligibility shortfall; the §8.3 update does not run while pinned; the period sizes audit demand on applied odds and the member seats; the duty fund is a wallet credited at award and paid by role weight in each batch; a plant funds its pots only, with no escrow and no guarantee lock. I22 and I23 per tick; I21 amended; I15 exempts a plant's guarantee. `test_v13` carries the §20 membership figures the six-actor world admits and the mechanism's properties; `test_v12` asserts the 1.2.1 engine through `Params(membership=False, duty_fund=False, net_plant_capital=False)`, which the stamping driver runs as the 1.2.0 control; the equivalence scenario's goldens were re-pinned once. Reviewer-authored tests were not edited and pass unchanged.

**After acceptance.** The runs of the revision's §8, in order: reproduce §6 (the test); the tipping point at 50/60/70/80 under 1.3.0 against run 4's table — the sign and the channel at each split, vesting against the control; adaptive dispositions, 1.3.0 against 1.2.0, fund on and off, 3,000 ticks; drift under 1.3.0 with $m_{\max}\in\{2,4\}$ and $\Omega_0\in\{100,300\}$; probation safety — false approvals attributable to probationers, the audit load on members; the red team — the camouflaged stamper under the gate, and a probationer that behaves until readmitted and then stamps.

## Appendix I. Design revision 4 — the orchestrator's troops (1.4.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr4_the_orchestrators_troops_1.4.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S5].** Run 5 (October 17, 2026; 360 runs under 1.3.0). The gate holds everywhere: stampers 2,100–2,500 per actor poorer at every split from 50/50 to 80/20, richer in 0 of 20 seeds; an adapting population reaches ≤ 10% stampers in all 20 seeds by tick ~800 and vesting then recovers above 1.2.0's. A fixed captured population is honest and short-staffed: vesting 20–42% below 1.2.0, awards unfilled for want of a free member in 816–899 of 1,000 ticks — at 60/40 three tasks a tick of two ticks each need six free actors and eight members with four on duty leave four. The fund is fair pay, not an accelerator (convergence 770 ticks without it, 865 with). Probation safety binds on member availability, not on the cap: at $m_{\max}=2$, 65 of 97 false approvals were probationers' whose pinned audits found no panel-eligible member free, and the approvals reached customers; the drivers had dropped such rolls since run 1 (647 audits assigned of 2,666 rolled).

**Mechanism.** Both costs — the audit nobody was free to take and the award nobody was free to accept — are one shortage: the market's only source of capacity is its own members. The orchestrator gets servers of its own, used last (a window the market has in full), used once (one seat, released at completion), paid from the tax the dial already levies (the fund as their wallet, with a first charge on it), never preferred to a member, never on the state's own work as auditor, and counted in public (troop-ticks, troop shortfall, cases held for the market's audit). A rolled audit becomes an obligation of the case, which alone closes most of the leak.

**Classification.** A design revision motivated by two strategy findings, labelled and read on its own; the first to add a participant. It alters no settlement, verdict or challenge rule — a troop's seat settles by the ordinary rules with the fund as its wallet. Minor, not major: the members' rules are untouched, and a troop is an instrument of a party that already acts as requester and deliverer in every plant and already holds the fund.

**What changed.** §1 (the troop assumption); §2.1, §2.4; §4.4 (a troop's cost is a purchase); §5.2 (an award to a troop by summons); §7.1; §7.7 (new); §8.3 (the obligation; the troop reviewer's odds default); §10.4; §12.3 (new: summons for a seat and for an award, the engine summons and the driver answers, panels, the fund's order, minimization, hypotheses); §16; I9, I22, I23 amended, I24 and I25 new; §18.1 question 11 answered, 12 and 13 new; §20 (troops); Appendices A, B, C. Defaults: $\varepsilon_T=0.05$, $w_R=w_A=2$, $c_T=3$, $c^D_T=10$, $\bar B$ trailing, $R_T$ the period's peak troop capital, $A_T=100$, $\tau_T=2$.

**Found in review and adopted.** rc.1 let a troop-reviewed troop delivery whose audit found no member vest as "unverified by the market", against its own §2.2 and I25; rc.2 made that audit an obligation like any other, a member's only, with the reading "held for the market's audit". rc.1 left open whether the engine or the driver summons; rc.2: the engine summons, the driver answers. rc.1 called a troop's cost a burn "as burned bonds do"; rc.2: a resource purchase under §4.4. rc.1 did not say what became of a case nobody could audit; rc.2 accepts the hold and retries the summons. rc.2 states why 1.4.0 and not 2.0.0; fixes $w_R$ to 2 ticks (two review durations in the drivers), names $\bar B=\beta$ in the drivers as a limit of the drivers, and says no troop audits the state's own work. Smaller: the obligation as a conformance correction of the drivers, with the 1.2.0 and 1.3.0 controls re-run with the roll held; panels with troops; $A_T$ named and swept; Hypothesis 4's size changes by the uplift; 1.3.1 applied. **At acceptance,** the external reviewer's note: a troop's acceptance of a verdict is recorded at the verdict, not the deadline — same rule, earlier tick; adopted.

**Chosen at implementation.** A troop reviewer's approval of a member's work needs audit odds and a troop has no $p_j$: the engine applies the adaptive floor scaled by the dial, $\min(1, m_t p_{\text{floor}})$ — the odds a reviewer whose record stood would carry — stated as a Default in §8.3. A seat's wait is counted in seating steps (required at $t$, waited 1 at $t$'s seating, $w_R$ at the seating of $t+w_R-1$). A resumed review is the same assignment, so a troop whose $\mathsf P$ or $\bot$ is answered by a resubmission resumes its seat. I25's pinned-approval clause is the driver's conformance, not an engine predicate: a world whose orchestrator never rolls has no obligation to hold, and the reviewer-authored tests are such worlds. The 1.4.0 engine read "the trailing period" of $R_T$ as the larger of this period's peak and the previous period's, so the reserve did not drop at a boundary to whatever happened to be carried at that instant — a reading not recorded here at the time, superseded by 1.5.0's one-period rule starting at the standing demand (Appendix J).

**Engine.** A troop is a single-use id whose ledger operations resolve to the duty fund's wallet through an alias, so I15 reconciles the fund's locks as the troops' bonds and guarantees; `roll_audit` records the obligation in the shape of the vacated-audit hold, the pending window cannot close while it stands, `assign_audit` after the window is admitted for it, a human ruling ends it; `_summon_troops` runs at step 5 after the seating, with the window, the free-eligible-reviewer and free-fundable-member tests, $\beta\ge\bar B$, the fund's carrying capacity with $\Omega$'s advance, and the V_max bound; a troop's cost is `ledger.purchase` by the fund; a troop's role weight is excluded from the batch's emission and uplift; the fund repays the advance before and after each batch and pays uplift only from above $R_T$; a troop's delivery is final $\mathsf N$ at any verdict but $\mathsf F$; the readings are in the period record and the History. I24, I25 per tick; I9, I22, I23 amended. `test_v14` (10) carries the §20 figures the six-actor world admits and the mechanism's properties; the stamping driver rolls on `audit_odds`, records the roll, never drops it, supplies troop verdicts and submissions from the declared disposition, and offers `--rules 1.3.0` (troops off) as the control; the equivalence scenario's goldens were re-pinned once. Reviewer-authored tests were not edited and pass unchanged.

**After acceptance.** The runs of the revision's §8, in order: reproduce §6 (the test); the 1.2.0 and 1.3.0 controls re-run with the roll held — every later comparison is against these; the tipping point under 1.4.0 against them; adaptive dispositions 1.4.0 against 1.3.0 at 3,000 ticks; drift at $m_{\max}\in\{2,4\}$; the windows $w_R\times w_A$ at 60/40 and $A_T$ at 80/20; $c^D_T$ and $\varepsilon_T$; the red team — camouflage, the reformer, the all-troop corner for cases held for the market's audit.

## Appendix J. Design revision 5 — the reserve is sized to the need (1.5.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr5_the_reserve_is_sized_to_the_need_1.5.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S6].** Run 6 (October 17, 2026; 780 runs under 1.4.0, plus a 160-run ruling sweep). Troops do what revision 4 said: vesting back at the 1.2.0 control at 50–70% stampers and above it at 80% (2,376 against 2,024), doubled at drift (3,621 against 1,856); the state's share reads the capture — 12 / 27 / 44 / 56% at 50–80%; false approvals below the controls at every split; under adaptive dispositions the stamper share falls to ≤ 10% at the same tick as under 1.3.0 (900 against 870) and troops are zero from tick 1,500 in every seed. The controls with the roll held barely moved. Where the text was wrong: **the reserve was self-limiting by the accepted rule** — $R_T$ as the peak capital troops *did* carry, drained to by every batch's uplift, could never rise, so the fund refused 4,800–8,800 award summonses a run while paying 6,400–12,700 in uplift (10,000–22,000 refused at drift), $A_T$ not binding (300 against 100 cut refusals and moved no delivery); the treasury, not the market's need, set the state's share. **Troops consume member capacity:** members' awards fell by a fifth to a half at 70–80% because every troop delivery is reviewed and most are audited; their wealth still rose, moved from awards to fees. **The ruling is not the residual leak:** ruling accuracy 0.80 → 0.95 cut wrong rulings 34 → 9 but probationers' false approvals only 62 → 52 and 22 → 16 at drift and not at all at the tipping cells; what remains is the audit itself — the auditor of a pinned approval is whichever member or troop is free, and under drift a member inside its detection lag approves what the probationer approved.

**Mechanism.** One rule: the reserve is sized to the attempted demand — capital held by live troops plus the cost of the summonses the fund refused for funds at that seating, each case once per seating at the amount the carry check refused, a case's demand its unfilled seats — as the peak over the current period, starting at the standing demand at the period's first seating. A refusal raises the reserve by what was refused; the fund keeps its inflows until it can carry the summonses that were made; since every troop seat is net-positive to the fund it grows into the need on its own; the peak holds until the period ends, and the money returns to the reviewers then. Hypothesis 4 of revision 4 is restated (the honest keep their income, its composition shifts), and a reading is added: how a probationer's false approval reached the customer — by route, then by the auditor's kind.

**Classification.** A design revision motivated by a strategy finding against one rule of revision 4. It changes what the reserve measures and alters no settlement, verdict, challenge or membership rule — the smallest since the freeze. Minor: behaviour, not wording, so not a patch; nothing a member may do, so not a change of approach.

**Recorded and rejected.** Uplift paid only when the period's troop shortfall was zero (the degenerate form: accumulates while short, pays out everything once served, sawtooths with the period); the raised tax split between reserve and uplift at a fixed ratio (arbitrary, blind to need); a reserve that tracks the stock — the maximum of the current demand and a decayed peak of refused demand (more responsive, one more definition and a decay rule to choose; kept for §18.1 question 13 if one period's retention costs something that matters); the ruling's default moved to $1-\varepsilon_T$ (the sweep did not earn it).

**What changed.** §12.3 (the fund's order: the reserve clause; the new rule; the reading; the restated hypothesis; the 1.5.0 hypotheses); I23 amended; §18.1 question 13 continued, 14 new; §20 (the fund under 1.4.0 and the 1.5.0 ramp); Appendices A, C, I. Defaults: $R_T$ redefined; everything else as 1.4.0.

**Found in review and adopted.** rc.1's consequence and worked example described a reserve that falls as the backlog is served, which a peak over the period does not do until the period ends; rc.2 keeps the peak and says so, and settles the period as one, starting at the standing demand (the engine's 1.4.0 two-period reading dropped). "Refused" means refused for funds, not a request priced below $\bar B$, a requester that cannot pay in, or a full path. A case's demand is its unfilled seats, two for an empty panel. The refused cost is the amount the carry check refused, at indexation. The §6 figures revised for a delivery's capital held to vesting — about thirteen ticks, a steady demand near 270 — with the 1.4.0 §6 figure of 48 acknowledged as the same understatement. The reading attributes by route (audit upheld; audit rejected and ruling overturned) and then by four auditor kinds, including the readmitted member. Smaller: I23 made checkable over explicit readings; the period record's two components; a test that a refused summons carried at the next seating raises the peak once; the goldens re-pinned once; the red team's `--decline-awards` and requester flood named.

**Chosen at implementation.** The refused demand of a pass is accumulated inside the summons pass and read with the capital held at its end, so $D_s$ is one number per seating; the panel loop, which stops at the first refusal, adds the seats it did not attempt only when the refusal was for funds. The period's peak is reset at the period's end — before the same tick's seating — to the capital still held, and the seating then takes the maximum with its $D_s$: that is "starting at the standing demand". The uplift check compares, as under 1.4.0, the fund's *unlocked* balance against a reserve that counts capital held, so the fund's total when uplift resumes is about $R_T$ plus the locks then held.

**Engine.** `Params.reserve_demand` (True; False is the 1.4.0 reading, the declared control `--rules 1.4.0`); `troop_demand_held`, `troop_demand_refused`, `troop_demand_now`, `troop_demand_peak` and its refused component as world readings; `R_T` the current period's peak demand; the period record carries `demand_peak`, `demand_peak_refused`, `demand_last`; I23's two inequalities. `test_v15` (7): a refusal raises the reserve by the refused cost once per seating and the 1.4.0 reading does not move; a request below the market or unfundable by its requester adds nothing; a panel short of both seats adds two; refused-then-carried raises the peak once; no uplift at or below the reserve, and the balance above it paid out; the peak holds within the period and restarts at the standing demand at the boundary — zero at rest, the troop's capital while one is in the field; I23 catching. The stamping driver reads the demand per tick, attributes each probationer's false approval by route and auditor kind after the run, and offers `--decline-awards` (a share of careful actors that offer for nothing) and `--rules 1.4.0` as the control; the equivalence scenario's goldens were re-pinned once. Reviewer-authored tests were not edited and pass unchanged.

**After acceptance.** The runs of the revision's §8, in order: reproduce §6 (the 60/40 ramp); the tipping point under 1.5.0 against run 6 — refused summonses, the state's share, vesting, members' awards, uplift paid and when in the period, $R_T$ and $D_s$; the windows $w_A\in\{1,2,5\}$ at 60/40; adaptive dispositions — convergence, troop-ticks and $R_T$ to zero; drift at $m_{\max}\in\{2,4\}$ with the route-and-kind reading; the red team — decliners, a requester flood, the reformer and camouflage once more.

---

## Appendix K. Design revision 6 — auto-scale (1.6.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr6_auto_scale_1.6.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S7].** Run 7 (October 17, 2026; 482 runs under 1.5.0). The reserve rule did what it said and did not help: refusals rose, deliveries did not, vesting and uplift were identical to 1.4.0. What bound was the **reviewing seat** — on duty 4.5–5 at every split under every rule since 1.3.0, reviews that found no reviewer 7,300–11,400 case-ticks a run, the open-case queue about 300 at 50–60%, vesting pinned near the posting-rate ceiling whoever delivered; twelve probationers sat idle while reviews waited because the reserve was sized on expected demand. The **demand stock counted the queue**, which never ends (requests do not expire), so $R_T$ ran to thousands and was unreachable. The **one-period reset saw-toothed** (3,135 and 3,146 paid out in one batch while refusing nine summonses a tick). The **award window was not a knob**. **Q14 answered:** of probationers' false approvals that reached a customer, 85–90% travelled the upheld route, and of those 55–75% at fixed splits and 73–80% under drift were audited by a member whose record had not yet failed — the lag is the record's, not the drift's. Minimization held; the red team bought nothing.

**Mechanism.** Five rules, each behind a flag. *The service clock* (§3.1, §6.1): every root request is timed from posting to its first verdict or its final, oldest first, and the orchestrator owes the requester $r_s$ a tick past $T_s$, capped at $B$, from $\Omega$ or as $\Omega$'s liability — the queue is bounded in liability instead of unbounded in count, and the orchestrator has a cost for not scaling. *Auto-scale* (§10.4, §12.3): $A_T$ removed, $\Omega$ backs the fund above a plant reserve so the honesty clock is never starved, in a stated order with liabilities older than a period first; review seats are summoned before deliveries; a delivery is summoned only when its own capital and two review seats can be carried. *Seat the queue* (§12.1): the reserve target is at least the reviews waiting, by kind, so idle probationers are seated while reviews wait. *A panel seat requires a determined record* (§7.1), dormant until the world's first plant is adjudicated, so the lagging auditor is gone at a fixed split. *The reserve over two periods* (§12.3). Baruchi's reading at the design discussion: a request on hold is a problem for the network's rating — re-queue with priority and have the orchestrator take some of the blame in money; and if ELEF controls the network and the servers, allow auto-scale. His third suggestion, that the network earn less when the state scales, was adopted where the network is ELEF (the rebate and the working capital) and rejected where it is the members.

**Classification.** A design revision motivated by run 7's capacity finding and Q14. It alters no settlement rule between members and requesters; it adds the orchestrator's liability for lateness and its authority to scale. Minor: what members may do changes in one place only (an undetermined member holds no panel seat, which the newcomer pin already implied).

**Recorded and rejected.** *Requests expire* (the design reviewer's run-7 proposal) — bounded the queue by turning customers away; replaced by the clock and the rebate. *Members earn less while the state scales* — the members standing at 60/40 are the honest ones at capacity, speeds are fixed in the model, and what a market does under shortage is raise the price, which is §18.1 question 2; the one "earn less" already in stands (while the state carries, the uplift stops). *A rebate paid before anything was bought* — a flood would farm it; the rebate is a discount at the stop. *Lateness measured to the award* — the customer waits for the verdict.

**What changed.** §3.1, §6.1, §7.1, §10.4, §12.1, §12.3 (new rules); I9, I10, I22, I23, I24 amended, I26 new; §18.1 question 11 restated, 14 continued, 15 and 16 new; §20; Appendices A, C. Defaults: $T_s=8$, $r_s=\omega_0 B$, $A_T$ removed, the plant reserve, the delivery check, the reserve target, panel eligibility, $R_T$'s window, the rule flags; the members' duty cap and $\Omega_0$ swept.

**Found in review and adopted.** rc.1's panel rule refused every panel assignment in a world that had not planted — every reviewer-authored test world, and the first period of every run; rc.2 made it dormant until the first plant is adjudicated and named the first period's panels as the state's, with the determination arithmetic in the text. rc.1's order of payment named a plant cap the engine lacks and would have spent the honesty clock under stress; rc.2 holds back a plant reserve, with "plants unfunded for want of $\Omega$" a must-be-zero reading, and bounds liabilities in age with the reason for the order stated. rc.1's Hypothesis 1 asked for vesting above the $3\times0.8=2.4$ ceiling; rc.2 restates it on the queue. The delivery check against the fund plus $\Omega$'s available advance, drawing only the delivery's own shortfall; the clock stopping at a final without a verdict; "withdrawn before award"; the cold start read from every run's first periods rather than an arrivals process (adding an actor to a running world is recorded as a later design question). Smaller: one flag per rule and one-factor-at-a-time cells first; the dead price exclusion named; the cap's bound on the liability; I26 as a bookkeeping identity; the period's rate for "expected"; members' income by source beside the cap sweep.

**Chosen at implementation.** *Reviews waiting are counted by kind* and I9's clause is checked per kind under the cap, else every tick in which audits wait with the member seats full and only probationers free would fail an invariant the rules make true. *$\Omega$'s order within the tick:* liabilities older than a period at the start of the summons pass, the pass, younger liabilities after it. *The plant reserve's horizon:* the accepted text says the ticks remaining in the period; a plant's pots return to $\Omega$ at its closure, so the capital the clock needs at once is a plant's life of plants, $W+T_{\text{review}}=15$ ticks — the implementer's reading, taken as the default, with the literal horizon kept as a declared cell; at $\Omega_0=300$ the literal reserve (about 360 at 60/40) exceeds $\Omega$'s whole balance and the state cannot advance at all. *The clock also stops at a vesting without an initial verdict* (a human's override), the case the text's two stops did not name. *A rebate liability serviced after vesting* is an obligation established before it and is admitted by I10 by its note. *A partial advance* drawn for a seat the check then refuses sits in the fund until the batch repays it, as under 1.4.0. *The control* `--rules 1.5.0` turns all five flags off with $A_T$ binding and the one-period reset.

**Engine.** `Params.service_clock, T_s, r_s, omega_backs_fund, seat_the_queue, panel_determined, reserve_periods, duty_cap_frac, plant_reserve_life`; `Case.clock_ticks, clock_stopped_at, first_verdict_at, late_ticks, rebate_owed, rebate_paid`; the clocks run in the summons pass, stop in `_initial_verdict`, `_finalize_N` and `_vest`; `_stop_clock` pays from $\Omega$ above `_plant_reserve()` or records a liability, `_service_omega_liabilities(min_age)` before and after the pass; `_omega_available()` replaces $A_T$'s room; `_summon_troops` in two passes with the priority check; `_summon_delivery`'s carry check; `_reserve_target()` and `_waiting_reviews()` at every rotation, `_rotate` and `_rotate_membership` filling to the target; `Actor.panel_eligible` with determination behind `first_plant_adjudicated_at`; `R_T` over two periods; the period record carries the rating and $\Omega$'s readings; I9, I22, I23, I24 amended, I26 new. `test_v16` (11). The stamping driver: `--rules 1.6.0` (default) with `--clock`, `--backing`, `--seat-queue`, `--panel-determined`, `--reserve-periods`, `--duty-cap`, `--t-s`, `--plant-reserve`, `--beta-low`/`--beta-low-share`; the rating, $\Omega$ and seat columns; the goldens re-pinned once. Reviewer-authored tests were not edited and pass unchanged; the implementer's own earlier test files are pinned to their revisions' mode.

**After acceptance.** The runs of the revision's §8, in order — every comparison against run 7 read from the second period on: one factor at a time at 60/40 against `--rules 1.5.0`; reproduce §6; the tipping point; the cap; float and target; the windows; drift with the route-and-kind reading and the $\theta_{\text{panel}}$ / window sweep; adaptive; the ramp (the first two periods, by period); the red team.

## Appendix L. Design revision 7 — the state's earnings (1.7.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr7_the_states_earnings_1.7.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S8].** Run 8 (October 17–18, 2026; 840 runs under 1.6.0). One factor at a time: the panel rule was the honesty gain (false approvals 47 → 14 at 60/40 alone; the lagging-auditor kind zero at every fixed split with all five rules — Hypothesis 4 of revision 6 in full); $\Omega$'s backing the capacity gain (the queue halved, refusals −64%); the clock alone bankrupted $\Omega$ (liability 13,810 against a balance of 57, plants unfunded 136); seat-the-queue alone hurt; the two-period reserve was a wash. Together: false approvals 12 / 13 / 16 / 17 at 50–80% against 42 / 47 / 66 / 96, the queue 208 / 94 / 44 / 40, stampers 45% poorer — and **Hypothesis 2 inverted**: on time at 80/20 (0.90), where the cap's floor put every member on duty and the state delivered everything; late and insolvent at 50–60% (0.40 / 0.60, mean lateness 48 / 38, $\Omega$'s liability 26,100 / 19,778, $\Omega$ ending at 87 / 94, **plants unfunded 151 / 81** — the must-be-zero reading failed). $\Omega_0=1{,}000$ did not help; $T_s$ moved nothing; $w_A$ failed again. The float was not the limit; $\Omega$'s income against plant costs and rebates was — about 2 a tick of base fee against a late request accruing 1 a tick up to 20. **The money was in the wrong pocket:** the troops' net at 60/40 was 6,200–6,800 a run (the fund's uplift paid plus its closing balance less the surcharge received), and 1.4.0 §2.4 sent it to the market's reviewers as uplift by the clause that nothing a troop earns reaches $\Omega$, while the same $\Omega$ carried the rebates for the lateness those troops were summoned to prevent. The two-thirds cap at 60/40 halved the queue (94 → 44), lifted on-time from 0.60 to 0.90, cut mean lateness 38 → 8 and the liability 19,778 → 3,512 with false approvals unchanged and careful wealth within 3% — and, a figure the run-8 notes had not reported, **tripled plants unfunded** (81 → 272), because the state delivered more from the same $\Omega$. The adaptive run minimized every flow and not the one stock: $\Omega$'s liability, 26,000 at the end, never cleared. The red team bought nothing; the late withdrawer (unawarded requests withdrawn after 12 ticks) cut the liability 19,778 → 830 at 11% unserved — evidence for a cannot-serve protocol, held.

**Mechanism.** *Two accounts again* (§4.1, §10.4, §12.2, §12.3): the duty fund returns to 1.3.0 §12.2 exactly — the raised tax $(m_t-1)\,\omega_0 B$ per award in and nothing else, its whole balance out as uplift at every settlement batch by role weight, carrying forward what no review claims, no reserve, no lock; the troops' wallet is a separate **troop account** — $\Omega$'s advances and every troop's earnings in; costs purchased, bonds and guarantees posted, a guarantee forfeited at a final $\mathsf N$, a bond burned on an overturn, repayments out; and at each batch **the unlocked balance above $R_T$ flows to $\Omega$**. *The clause withdrawn:* 1.4.0's "nothing a troop earns reaches $\Omega$ except by repayment of an advance" — a troop is an actor of the state and its earnings are the state's. *What guards the conflict of interest the clause was for*, all standing: members first at every seat; the windows $w_R$, $w_A$; cost at summons; no emission, uplift, $\rho$ or $\kappa$ to a troop; the state's share on the public record — to which the revision adds **$\Omega$'s income by source** (base fee, troop surplus) per period, beside $\Omega$'s own money's flows (advances made, repaid) and its outflows (rebates paid, liabilities serviced, plant spend), so that a state living off its own deliveries is visible as such. *The reserve is capital held* (§12.3): $R_T$ is the two-period peak of the capital troops held at once — costs purchased plus locks — and no longer counts refused summonses; 1.5.0 added that component so a fund $\Omega$ backed only to $A_T$ could grow into the need, 1.6.0 removed $A_T$, and the component became retention without a purpose (952–3,494 at 60/40 in run 8, most of it refused demand held back from an $\Omega$ that could not fund plants). What $R_T$ is for now: the troops' in-flight capacity shielded from the liability queue — $\Omega$ pays liabilities older than a period before it advances, so an account that kept nothing would see every summons compete with every old debt exactly when the debts are largest. *$\Omega$ pays its debts from its income* (§10.4): the order of 1.6.0 unchanged and now funded, the troop surplus landing in $\Omega$ before the order runs; the plant reserve's horizon ruled a plant's life, $W+T_{\text{review}}=15$ ticks. *The members' duty cap* (§12.1) moves to $\lceil 2E_t/3\rceil$ on run 8's cap table — a default, the first time it moves. *The take swept:* $\omega_0\in\{5\%,10\%,15\%\}$ with the rebate $r_s$ **pinned at 1 a tick**, since $\omega_0$ also sets the surcharge and, by 1.6.0's default, the rebate; the customer's compensation for waiting is not a take-rate question. *Three wording items from run 8* (the 1.6.1 patch folded in): the clock's third stop, a vesting without an initial verdict; reviews waiting counted by kind and I9 per kind under the cap; the plant reserve's horizon.

**Classification.** A design revision motivated by a strategy finding against one rule of revision 4 (where a troop's earnings go) and one default of revision 3 (the cap). It alters no settlement rule between members and requesters. Minor: nothing a member may do changes; what changes is which of the orchestrator's accounts a troop's money lands in, and how many members may inspect at once.

**Recorded and rejected.** *The fund pays the state's lateness before uplift* (the design reviewer's first proposal) — the right money in the wrong pocket still; Baruchi's rule is cleaner: *why should an inspector profit from something a troop did? from their perspective it's just like any other actor in the system.* *Members earn less while the state scales* — still rejected (Appendix K). *The troop account as a pass-through* ($R_T=0$, every summons $\Omega$'s advance) — consistent and paying $\Omega$ sooner, but it hands the troops' capacity to the liability queue. *Expiry or admission control now* — held: **the cannot-serve protocol** ("the line is busy; expected wait $T$ — hold, or come back?", with the share of requests so answered as the other half of the network rating) is recorded as 1.8.0's candidate if 50/50's liability does not clear under this revision.

**What changed.** §4.1, §10.4, §12.1 (the cap's default), §12.2 (the uplift fund as 1.3.0), §12.3 (the troop account, $R_T$ as capital held, the clause struck, the guards and the reading); I9, I23, I24, I26 amended; §18.1 question 13 and 16 continued, 17 new; §20; Appendices A, C. Defaults: the cap $\lceil 2E_t/3\rceil$; the troop account and its flag; $R_T$ as capital held; the plant reserve's horizon 15 ticks; $\omega_0$ swept with $r_s$ pinned.

**Found in review and adopted.** The rc.1 review (`dr7_1.7.0-rc.1-review.md`) found that the $\omega_0$ sweep moved the rebate and the surcharge with the fee (rc.2 pins $r_s$); that run 8's cap table carried a cost the run-8 notes had not reported — plants unfunded tripled at 60/40 under the two-thirds cap — so Hypothesis 1 now carries the arithmetic (about 7,800 of need against 6,200 of surplus) with the plants as its sharp edge; that $R_T$ was not "about 270" but 952–3,494 and its job had changed (rc.2 redefines it as capital held and states the purpose); and that the surcharge received was about 7 a tick, not 3, so uplift falls by two fifths, not to a quarter. Smaller: repayments are flows of $\Omega$'s own money, not income; the floor's bind on "a third free"; forfeits and burns in I23's list; I24 never carried the struck clause; `--omega-rate`. The rc.2 review (`dr7_1.7.0-rc.2-review.md`): implementable as it stands, four readings — $R_T$ as capital held is the engine's 1.4.0 reading over two periods; Hypothesis 3 is read with refusals beside the state's share, since a richer $\Omega$ refuses fewer summonses under the same rules; the implementer's test files pinned to their revisions' flags; `--r-s` added and `--duty-cap` following the rules.

**Found at implementation — a figure in the text, not a rule.** rc.2 §1.5, §2.1 and §6 say a troop *review seat* "nets nothing — fee 2 or 3 against a cost of 3", adopting the rc.1 review's point 4. That point was the implementer's error: the engine has reimbursed a troop reviewer's cost from the requester's expense allowance like any reviewer's since 1.4.0 (`f0779ac`; 1.4.0 §6 and `test_v14` say so — "−3 cost, +3 reimbursed, +3 fee"), so a troop review seat nets its fee — +3 for an audit, +2 for an ordinary review — and under this revision that too is the state's. The surplus is therefore larger than the text says: at 60/40 in run 8 about 1,950 troop review seats a run earned their fees beside the deliveries' net. No rule moves; the consolidated text above states the seat's earnings correctly, the accepted text is kept verbatim with this note, and `test_v17` pins the figure. The equivalence scenario's dial no longer reaches $m_{\max}$ at one seed under the two-thirds cap; the pin reads the dial risen, not at its cap.

**Ruled after run 9 — patch 1.7.1 (October 18, 2026): the award summons is per request, not per tick.** Run 9's addendum traced the 50/50 collapse by period against 60/40 (`agentic_sim/results/studies/1.7.0-earnings/trace/`): the two splits are identical through the second period and part at the third, when the stampers are suspended — ten members remain at 50/50 against eight at 60/40, about five sit on duty at both, so five deliver at 50/50 against three, and one or two of them are free at the seating step on most ticks. The engine read 1.4.0 §2.3 per tick: one free fundable member blocked every award summons that tick. At 50/50, 79% of ticks had a queue past $w_A$ — 289 requests on average — with two members free and no summons; the summonses came in bursts when none happened to be free (the first at tick 288 on average, 38 at once; p90 23 a burst), between bursts the queue grew about a request a tick, and the queue's age was the lateness and the liability. The 1.6.0 control stalled the same way (74% of ticks, larger bursts, p90 43); the paths diverged at tick 504 on average, once old liabilities ranked before advances from tick 396 — the two accounts decided how the bursts were financed; the stall was the reading's. The same per-tick test explained run 8's inverted Hypothesis 2 (at 80/20 the four members are never free, so the state was never blocked). $T_{\text{susp}}\in\{0,50,100\}$ moved who is free and repaired neither split; honesty paid for lowering it at both. **The design reviewer's ruling:** the rule's meaning is per request — a root request that no eligible member has been awarded within $w_A$ ticks is awarded to a troop; a free member takes the oldest request it can; the others, unawarded past their window with nobody offering for them, are the state's. So at the seating step, requests that have waited $w_A$ and exceed the free fundable members are summoned for, oldest first, after the members have taken theirs; "members first" is unchanged — a troop never takes an award a member offered for. **Classification:** a conformance correction, with the wording made unambiguous — 1.7.1. The text admitted both readings; the per-tick one contradicts the rule's stated purpose (the state fills what the market leaves). Not a revision; no other rule moves. $T_{\text{susp}}$ stays at 100. **Engine:** `_summon_troops`'s award pass gathers the root requests past $w_A$ in creation order, counts the free fundable members at the market price (`_free_fundable_members`), leaves that many of the oldest to the market and summons for the rest, oldest first, each through the unchanged delivery check; the readings `awards_left_to_members` (summed over seatings; in the period record), `awards_waiting_now`, `members_free_now`. `test_v171` (4) pins it; the equivalence scenario's goldens re-pinned once at the correcting commit, since the scenario summons where one free member used to block; reviewer tests untouched. The 1.1.0–1.6.0 controls (`--rules`) carry the corrected reading too, as the 1.4.0 audit obligation did: a conformance correction holds in every mode, so earlier controls are not reproduced to the unit from here on. **Asked before 1.8.0:** the tipping cells 50–80% under the corrected reading against run 9 — queue, on-time, liability, plants unfunded, troop deliveries beside refusals for funds, $R_T$ (expected no longer set by burst peaks), $\Omega$'s income by source, false approvals; steady summons expected at 50–60%, 80/20 unchanged. If 50/50 clears, 1.8.0 is the cannot-serve protocol alone and the growth buffer is dropped.

**Chosen at implementation.** *"Unlocked above $R_T$"* is computed as the engine computed the uplift's room: the troop account's unlocked balance less $R_T$, where $R_T$ already counts the locks held — so under stress the account keeps more than $R_T$ in total and nothing flows until it has refilled; 1.5.0's arithmetic unchanged, and the reason $\Omega$'s income is the base fee alone during a ramp. *The surplus flows at each settlement batch* (step 3), after the advance is repaid and before step 5's order runs, as the text has it. *The troop account's flag* also selects the reserve's reading: with it off the merged fund, the demand reserve and `--rules 1.6.0`; the 1.5.0 demand readings ($D_s$, its refused part, the period's demand peak) stay in the record for questions 13 and 14. *The driver's cap* defaults to the rules' fraction and is its own knob, so §8.1's three cells separate.

**Engine.** `Params.troop_account` (on), `duty_cap_frac` $=2/3$; the wallet `TROOP_ACCT` beside `DUTY_FUND`, `World.troop_wallet`, troop ids aliased to it; `_fund_can_carry`, `_repay_advance`, the delivery check, the lock reconciliation and the troops' capital on the troop account; the batch pays the duty fund's whole unlocked balance as uplift and, after the advance is repaid, `_pay_troop_surplus` — the troop account's unlocked balance above `R_T` to $\Omega$; `R_T` the two-period peak of `_troop_capital_now()` under the flag; the period record's `uplift_fund`, `omega_income`, `omega_advances`, `omega_out`, with `omega_fee_income`, `troop_surplus_paid`, `advances_made`, `advances_repaid` on the world; I23 amended (the uplift fund equals the raised tax received less the uplift paid and holds no lock; the troop account reconciles against its recorded flows; $R_T\ge$ capital held; $R_T\ge D_s$ retired with the component). `test_v17` (8). The stamping driver: `--rules 1.7.0` (default) with `--accounts`, `--duty-cap` by the rules, `--omega-rate`, `--r-s`; the account, surplus and income columns; `--rules 1.6.0` as the control (the merged fund, the half cap). Goldens re-pinned once. Reviewer-authored tests were not edited and pass unchanged; `test_v13`–`test_v16` are pinned to their revisions' mode.

**After acceptance.** The runs of the revision's §8, in order — every comparison against run 8 read from the second period on, the state's share read beside the refusals: one factor at a time at 60/40 against `--rules 1.6.0` (the accounts alone, the cap alone, both — plants unfunded first); reproduce §6; the tipping point against run 8's two-thirds cells; the take ($\omega_0$ with $r_s$ pinned, at 50/50 and 60/40); adaptive; drift; the red team.

## Appendix M. Design revision 8 — the price of work (2.0.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr8_the_price_of_work_2.0.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.3); the three implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S1–S11, C-2.0].** Every run was priced by fiat at $\beta=20$ from a bottomless wallet; the selection rule's price term never moved, §4.6's execution-cost operation was never called, and nobody delegated. So the credit was a score: the driver's backing stood at $10^7$ and $v_t$ moved from 1.0000 to 0.9995 in a thousand ticks. "The state is broke" (runs 8–9) was never the loop breaking but a cash rule imposed on $\Omega$ as a stand-in for servers; the two accounts, $R_T$, the plant reserve and the order of payment existed to ration that cash. Scarcity had no signal but a queue (run 11's hard spike: 20–40 ticks in line, unspoken to). Baruchi's frame: decouple the servers from the economy; let printing make money worth less; give the money something to balance against.

**What changed.** Demand: requesters with wallets and a declared budget policy, funded by issuance; the pay-in locked at posting, a root's first lock; the ceiling the requester's, raised when flagged below market (§2.1, §4.1, §5.1). Supply: members bid at or above their reservation; the state at $\bar B$ from members' awards only, and at cost (§5.1, §7.7). Money: the price level $P_t$ indexes every nominal the rules fix, frozen per case; effort costs nominal as the anchor; no backing — sources issuance, emission, creation, sinks the burns, retirement, departure (§4.4–4.6). The state: never broke — creation for a required payment, retirement from every inflow, the deficit and $\Omega$'s net position public; $A_T$, $R_T$, the plant reserve, the order, the troop account and the liability stock retired (§10.4). Servers: $N_T$ outside the ledger, the capacity case recorded, two places per delivery (§12.3). Delegation deferred to 2.1.0 with heterogeneous costs. I1, I3, I23, I24, I26 amended; I27–I29 new; Q2 opened, Q5 kept, Q7 restated, Q11 and Q15 in real terms, Q16 the deficit, Q18 and Q19 new.

**The three reviews.** rc.1 → rc.2 (seven points, all adopted): the demand defaults did not clear at $P_0$ — the deposit's net cost of about 8 a task left about 10 for the price of 20, and the ceiling policy against the deposit's float starved the posting rate — resolved with initial wallets, issuance at the all-in, a capped ceiling policy and $M$ at genesis stated; delegation cannot pay with one cost — deferred; the pay-in locked at posting (§9.1's model); indexing frozen at the first lock (§4.5 kept) — with the design reviewer's addition that effort costs stay nominal as the anchor; the state's margin on a riot — at cost, $0.5P_t$ the control, $N_T$ finite so the capacity case is run; the deficit's size (thousands, the in-flight capital); retirement defined. rc.2 → rc.3 (three points, all adopted): the riot defined as a crowd of requesters, since six requesters at one post a tick cannot post fifteen; $N_T$ 40, clear of run 10's quiet field (30 bound at rest at 80/20); issuance 10 the default and 15 the sweep, so the worked example and the default agree. rc.3: implementable as it stands.

**Ruled at acceptance (October 18, 2026) — two rulings, no rc.4.** *(1) "Two of $N_T$" — reserve, release on seating.* A troop delivery holds two places from its summons until its initial review is assigned — to a market reviewer, after which it holds one; to a troop, after which the seat holds its own. The capacity reading counts places held. That is the clause's meaning (the field cannot fill with deliveries whose inspections are then refused), and the worked example's twenty-six is a plausible middle, not a bound. *(2) The crowd's leaving — a named sink, `departure`.* A requester that leaves takes its wallet with it: the unspent credits leave $M$ as a `departure` kind, the mirror of `issuance` bringing them in — not burned in the economy's sense, not hoards of agents who no longer exist; gone, and I1 sees the kind. Their open requests are served out first.

**Implementer's readings, recorded here.** *The market price before the first award.* 1.4.0's $\bar B$ fell back to the request's ceiling when no member had been awarded; under 2.0.0 the ceiling is the requester's choice (up to 40), so the fallback is the price level, $P_0$ before the first award — the first troop prices at 20, not at a ceiling of 40 — and nothing is flagged below market until a member has been awarded. *Retirement within the tick.* "At once" is the tick's granularity: $\Omega$ retires before it creates and at the tick's end, so I28's predicate (zero unlocked while the deficit stands) holds at every tick boundary; an inflow received mid-tick may pay a required payment in the same tick instead of being retired and recreated — the same net. *The cost API is in credits.* `spend` and `complete_review` burn exactly the credits given under 2.0.0; the troops' indexed $c_T$ is indexed by the engine before its burn. *Issuance defaults to 0 in the engine* and the driver declares it; the genesis balances are minted once as issuance and paid out as before, so the record's shape is the 1.x one. *A leaving requester* is one with no request of its still open — not final, or final with its pots still to return. *The wait recorded at a refusal for capacity* is in seating steps, as $w_A$ counts. *The margin's decay in the drivers* is continuous — $0.25/3$ a tick idle — since a threshold loses the fraction and ratchets the margin up; the award frequency per free member against the idle time is what sets whether bids drift to cost or to the ceiling, and the first smoke at 60/40 (two free members, two awards a tick) drifted to the ceiling (§20).

**Engine.** `Params.floating` (on), `P0`, `issuance` (0), `N_T` (40), `troop_cost_at_price` (on); the ledger's `index()` returns $P_t/P_0$ and `v` $P_0/P_t$, `purchase` a plain burn, the kinds `issuance`, `creation`, `retirement`, `departure`; `post_root` locks the all-in and freezes the nominals and $m$, `award` and `_summon_delivery` release the lock and draw the pay-in, `withdraw_unawarded` releases it; `raise_ceiling`, `add_requester`, `remove_requester`, `scenario_create`; `_omega_ensure` / `_omega_pay` / `_omega_retire` with the deficit counters; `price_level`, `market_price_known`; the troops' wallet is $\Omega$, `_fund_can_carry` creates, $R_T$ 0, the plant reserve 0, the liability list never fed; $N_T$ with places per troop, `_seat_delivery_inspection`; the capacity counters and the told flag; the period record's `P_level`, `created`, `retired`, `deficit`, `deficit_peak`, `omega_net`, `issued`, `departed`, `below_market`, `refused_capacity`, `places_held`, `M`. I27–I29; I15 knows the posting lock; I23, I24 amended. `test_v20` (16). **Five reviewer tests become expected failures**, each with its reason in `run_tests.py` and `conftest.py`: one pinned the backing's $v_t$ halving under emission (anticipated); one pinned the award-time refusal of an unfundable pay-in, now refused whole at posting (I29); three pinned 1.x figures through the index — the reviewer tests award at 10 and 40, not at $P_0$, so the price level moves after their first award and the nested nominals, the court fee and the cap move with it, as the rule says they must. *A reading for the reviewer:* $P_t$ on $\bar B$'s window moves within a period at every award; defined at the period boundary instead, the index would be constant within a period and those three tests would pass — one line in the engine, the text's call. The goldens were re-pinned once (`test_perf_equivalence`); the 1.x behaviour tests (`test_spec`, `test_v05`–`test_v06`, `test_v11`–`test_v171`) pin `floating=False`, as earlier revisions' tests pinned their flags. Suite: 259 passed, 31 expected failures, in all three modes.

**Drivers.** `--rules 2.0.0` (the default; `1.7.1` the fixed-price control): six requesters with wallets, issuance, the ceiling policy and $K_{\text{open}}$, reading the flag; bids by disposition with the margin dynamics and the at-the-ceiling control, the adaptive-reservation variant; the effort burned at submission; real-wealth columns; `--n-t`, `--c-d`, `--c-d-t-mode`, `--lam`, `--issuance`, `--wallet`, `--beta-max`, `--k-open`, `--bid`, `--c-d-adaptive`, `--create-at/--create-amount`, `--spike-requesters`; the price, deficit, capacity and demand columns.

**After acceptance.** The runs of the revision's §8, in order, every 1.x comparison in real terms from the second period on, against runs 10–11 as the fixed-price controls: reproduce §6; price discovery at 50–80% against the at-the-ceiling control; the deficit shock at 60/40 (1,000 / 2,000 / 5,000 at tick 600, $N_T$ 40 and unbounded); the riot under prices (the crowd of thirty, the flood of twelve, $N_T$ 40 and unbounded); delegation held for 2.1.0; the knobs ($\lambda$, $c^D_T$ at cost against the margin, issuance 10 / 15, the adaptive reservation); adaptive and drift in real terms; the red team (collusion at the ceiling, a member bidding below cost, a requester that underbids to wait for a troop, troop awards counted in $\bar B$ as the control that shows why they are not).

---

## Appendix N. Design revision 9 — the bank (2.1.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr9_the_bank_2.1.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S12, S13, C-2.1].** *The state at cost was below cost* — $c^D_T=\bar B$ burned at the summons, $\bar B$ recovered at vesting, and a fifth of the state's deliveries end in a final $\mathsf N$, recovering nothing and forfeiting $G$: about $0.3\,\bar B$ a delivery, created to cover it, the deficit growing 10–20 a tick for the rest of every run (run 12; 2.0.0's Hypothesis 7 failed). *The rebate was a pump:* under the flood a refusal for capacity meant a rebate created, unspendable, hoarded — deficit 59,700, $M$ 99,400. *The index moving within a period* broke three reviewer tests that pin a case's amounts. *The deficit could not be read:* capital in flight, losses and rebates owed were one figure. *Run 13 settled the price question* so that this revision does not touch it: the high price is the members' strategy restricting quantity under scarce supply (one award per 3.6 free member-ticks, the margin rule's own constant); adding workers takes the price to cost; the state at $\bar B$ never lowers it; the cap is the price level only when requesters bid above the market. The lever is entry — 2.2.0's. Baruchi's frame: the state as a living organism regulating the market, and a central bank as a separate entity — agreed as a second account with rules, not a second actor.

**What changed.** A second account, the bank, that alone creates and destroys: it mints by rule (issuance, emission, a plant's pots, the declared shock), publishes $P_t$ once a period, and lends the state the shortfall of a summons tied to it, repaid from its own return; the state burns its expected cost and delivers at $\bar B$; what a settled summons did not repay is the state's debt, repaid by every inflow the moment it arrives, before rebate liabilities and before $\Omega$ keeps; the bank lends for a delivery only within the wall — one period of $\Omega$'s fee income — and for a seat always; a rebate $\Omega$ cannot pay is a liability, never created; every refusal marks the request and is told (§2.4, §3.1, §4.1–4.5, §7.6, §7.7, §10.4, §12.2, §12.3, §16). Creation, retirement and the deficit retired as rules — kept as the control. I1, I3, I24, I26 amended; I28 rewritten; I30, I31 new; Q4, Q15, Q16, Q18, Q19 restated; Q20 (entry) kept for 2.2.0 with Q5.

**Recorded and rejected.** *The state pricing below $\bar B$* (its expected cost as its price): run 13 — the price-setter wherever members are scarce, the members crowded out. *Issuance as a dial*: money moves the price tag, not the work. *The bank as an actor*: nothing to optimise. *The oligopoly as a rule*: a strategy under scarce supply; the lever is entry. *The state refusing for cash*: "never broke" stands for capital — a summons is always lent for while the debt is within a period of income; only a state that has lost more than it earns retracts. **Recorded and held:** *the state's window $w_A$ as the knob* — held until entry exists (2.2.0), so that the knob is tested against the lever and not in its place.

**The two reviews.** rc.1 → rc.2: one correction adopted — a plant has been netted since 1.3.0 §2.5 (award and guarantee notional, only the pots funded), so the bank mints the pots, about 23 at $P_t=31$ and not 45, and *the whole sweep* of the plant's wallets at closure returns to the bank, since if any of it reached $\Omega$ the state would be earning minted money; one design choice left open, ruled — a binding wall refuses deliveries and never review seats (the seat serves a member's delivery; 1.6.0 found the seat caps the market; its loss is a bond); seven readings taken as written — the burn floored at 0 with the two-thirds limit named; $b_T$'s window extended back until ten have closed, else the prior; a seat's loan repaid at its review's settlement, each loan from its own summons's return; the first period at base by construction, every comparison from the second period on; neutrality by construction where the prior equals the driver's rate, so the §8 knobs and red team are the test and read first; `test_review_14` stays an expected failure (28); the *told* mark was the engine's already and is widened; §6 at 67 state deliveries a period, not 180. rc.2: implementable as it stands.

**Ruled at acceptance (October 19, 2026) — one ruling, no rc.3.** *Every inflow to $\Omega$ is ordered at once — debt → liabilities → $\Omega$ — the moment it arrives, inside the summons pass included.* The implementer noted that a base fee paid inside the pass could otherwise fund part of the next summons before the end-of-tick ordering repaid the debt, and would implement the two checkpoints as written unless every inflow was to be ordered. Ruled: yes, every inflow at once — the whole point of the bank is that while the state owes it, no income of the state funds a troop, not a whole one, not part of one; a mid-pass fee slipping through would be small money and a large hole in "the state never printed for itself". The pass-start and tick-end points stay as I30's checks, not as the rule.

**Implementer's readings, recorded here.** *The order on every inflow* is applied by the ledger's event hook: an event that credits $\Omega$'s unlocked balance — a transfer, a pool payout, a forfeit, a released lock — first repays the loan of the troop whose return it is (the event names the troop), then runs debt → liabilities; a loan's own mint is not an inflow. *A seat's loan closes at its review's settlement* — vested, overturned or withdrawn — not at the troop's release at the verdict, so that the seat's capital in flight is not read as debt for the review's pendency. *The wall in the goldens:* the seeded random drive's state deliveries fail far above a fifth (random verdicts, missed deadlines), so the wall binds there and the state retracts — 393 refusals for credit and 24 requests waiting at the end of seed 1 — the rule as written on a drive that is not an economy; the goldens pin it. *$\Omega$ funds its own summonses first:* in the honest market the state's retained fees fund most summonses and the loans line is small; losses on $\Omega$-funded deliveries are $\Omega$'s own income lost, not debt — debt arises only where a loan went unrepaid, as the text says. *The state-rejecter red team* cannot be a conforming reviewer: the reviewer's view carries no actor (§14), so the driver's rejecter reads the case's actor through the public view's case — declared in the manifest as a reading of what the rule would do if a reviewer could tell.

**Engine.** `Params.bank` (on, with `floating`), `c_d_t_mode` (`expected` / `at_cost` / `half`), `b_t_prior` 0.2, `b_t_min_n` 10, `debt_bound_periods` 1 (0 unbounded); the ledger's kinds `mint_plant`, `loan`, `mint_scenario` in and `repayment`, `retire_plant` out; the World's `bank_minted` / `bank_destroyed` by kind, `loans` by troop id, `debt` with its peak and age, `_omega_income` (the order), `_bank_repay`, `_close_loan`, `_debt_bound`, `_state_fail_rate`, `_mark_refused`; `_fund_can_carry(need, cid, kind)` lends or refuses; `_summon_delivery` burns the expected cost and marks; `_stop_clock` to the liability stock; `create_plant` mints and the tree closure destroys; `scenario_create` mints to the requesters; `price_level()` as published, set in `_maybe_end_period`; the period record's `bank` block and `omega_fee_total`; the Case's `refused`, `refused_at`, `refused_last`, `refused_credit`, `c_d_t`, `b_t_at_summons`; `view.bank()` and the marks in `view.market()`. I28 rewritten, I30, I31; I27–I31 in the full audit too. `test_v21` (13); `test_v20` pins `bank=False`; the goldens re-pinned once; three reviewer tests out of `EXPECTED_FAIL` (28 remain). Suite: 275 passed, 28 expected failures, in all three modes.

**Drivers.** `--rules 2.1.0` (the default; `2.0.0` the control with $\Omega$'s creation; `1.7.1` the fixed price): `--c-d-t-mode expected|at_cost|half`, `--b-t-prior`, `--b-t-min-n`, `--debt-bound-periods`, `--requester-on-refusal hold|withdraw` (the marks read through the public view, withdrawn and reposted), the red team `--troop-fail-rate` (the state's truth re-drawn at the troop's submission) and `--state-rejecters`; the bank's books per tick — minted and destroyed by kind, loans outstanding and peak, debt with peak and age, liabilities with the oldest age, the wall and its slack, $\Omega$'s retained income and net position, the cost of truth, $b_T$ and the burn, the refusals and marks by kind, withdrawn and reposted at the mark.

**After acceptance.** The runs of the revision's §8, in order, every comparison in real terms from the second period on, 2.0.0's cells (runs 12–13) the controls, every cell naming its requester policy and $K_{\text{open}}$: reproduce §6; the state neutral at 50–80% against `at_cost` on the identical path, read with the knobs and the red team where $b_T$ and the driver's rate differ; the pump — the flood and the crowd at $N_T=40$, 60/40 with $K_{\text{open}}$ lifted under both ceiling policies; the index per period against run 12; the inflation test — issuance 5 / 10 / 20 at 20 honest; told — the withdrawing requester; the books in every cell; the knobs; the red team; delegation and entry held for 2.2.0.
