## Appendix O. Design revision 10 — the rebate from income (2.2.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr10_the_rebate_from_income_2.2.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S14, C-1.6, C-2.2].** 2.1.0 §6 expected a liability stock that peaks at a few thousand and ages a period or two; run 14's flood of twelve left 26,839 (accrued 32,503), the crowd of thirty 9,968, ageing 420–520 ticks and growing, against a whole run's fee income of 7,852 and 5,832. The cause is the rebate's sizing, not the bank: a claim of $r_s$ a tick on every late request scales with the crowd's size times its wait; the state's means scale with $N_T$. The customers' real protection is the mark — every refusal told, the withdrawing requester gaining nothing by withdrawing; the rebate's job is the state's own punishment in money, as 1.6.0 said ("punish the network somehow — earn less or something").

**What changed.** The claim is unchanged as the measure; $\Omega$'s fee income after the debt accrues to the period's pool, a lock on $\Omega$, fee income only; at the period's close the claims stopped in the period are paid pro rata and the rest is forgone — not owed, not carried, not created — and read on each request and in the period (claims, paid, forgone, the forgone share, $\Omega$ retained); no liability stock (§2.4, §3.1, §10.4, §12.3, §18, §20). Two rulings carried: the reviewer's view stays blind to the actor (§14); Hypothesis 5 of 2.1.0 restated — printing is neutral in work and not in distribution, issuance still not a dial. A conformance correction found on the way: a stated $r_s$ is indexed at the case's first lock (§4.5), the engine having indexed only the default. I10 note, I26 and I30 amended, I32 new.

**Recorded and rejected.** *Forgive after a period, oldest first* (the early birds win, and the stock still exists for a period). *Leave the stock as the honest bill* (an IOU nobody pays, run 11's silence in money form). *Print for it* (the pump). *The bank lends for rebates* (nothing returns). *A smaller $r_s$* (the claim should stay honest; the payment must fit the means).

**The two reviews.** rc.1 → rc.2: one correction adopted — I32 said "every inflow" where §2.2 said "fee income"; pooling the returns of capital would lock the state's working capital and make every summons the bank's from the second period on — the pool is fee income after the debt (base fees, the troops' review fees, court fees a troop wins), not the returns of capital and not the delivery's surplus, which is the failures' cover; five readings taken as written — the pool as a lock on $\Omega$ (I15 reconciles it, I30 without a special case); Hypothesis 2 restated to expect loans in the first period or two and $\Omega$ retained lower by one period's fees, everything else identical to run 14 on the same seeds; the stated $r_s$ indexed (a conformance gap found on the way, corrected under §19 with a regression test, no run affected); the close inside the tick at step 5, claims assigned by the tick's granularity; I10's exempt note and `test_v21` pinning `rebate_pool=False`, the expected failures unchanged at 28. rc.2: implementable as it stands; accepted verbatim, no ruling.

**Ruled after run 15 (October 19, 2026) — the reviewer advance may draw on the pool.** A §4.3 reviewer advance — Ω advancing a reviewer's reimbursement when the allowance is exhausted — **may draw on the period's pool during the period**, the pool at the close being net of it: verification before the state's blame. To be implemented with 2.3.0, when the two reviewer tests that pinned a service fee advancing a reimbursement (`test_review_06`, `test_r4_08`) return from the expected failures; until then the engine runs 2.2.0 as accepted, and the reading below records what that is.

**Implementer's readings, recorded here.** *Ω's advances to reviewers wait for the unlocked balance (until the ruling above is implemented).* §4.3 has Ω advance a reviewer's reimbursement when the allowance is exhausted and pay its outstanding reviewer claims first in, first out on every inflow; under the pool a fee inflow is locked until the close, so the advance and the claim are paid from what the pool excludes — the returns of capital, the retained balance, the pool's remainder at the close — and in a world that starts with no float they wait for the close. Two reviewer tests that pinned a service fee advancing and paying a claim at once (`test_review_06`, `test_r4_08`) become expected failures with that reason; their principle holds at the close (`test_v22`). The alternative — a fee paying Ω's reviewer claims before it is pooled — was tried and does not save the tests (the advance, not the payment, is what the pool changes), so the text stands as written. *The pool's remainder at the close* is an inflow like any other: released to $\Omega$'s unlocked balance, it repays the debt first if any stands — "the remainder joins $\Omega$'s balance" read with 2.1.0's order. *A fee event* is the base fee's transfer to $\Omega$, a troop reviewer's fee from the pot, or a court fee forfeited to a troop; the hook pools the fee less what it repaid of the debt. *The close settles before the summons pass*, so a claim of the closing tick's vesting or final is paid in the same tick. *Run 14's seeds*: the pool holds a period's fees from the summonses, so the honest cells show the bank's loans in the first period or two and a lower retained balance — Hypothesis 2 as restated.

**Engine.** `Params.rebate_pool` (on, with `bank`); `omega_pool` as a lock record `pool:<n>` on $\Omega$ raised in the inflow hook from fee events after the debt (`_is_fee_event`, `_pool_add`); `period["claims"]`, `claims_total`, `pool`; `_settle_pool` at the boundary in `_maybe_end_period` beside the index — the release, the pro-rata payments with the note `rebate (settled at the period's close)`, `rebate_paid` / `rebate_forgone` / `rebate_settled_at` on each request, the period's `rebates_paid`, `rebates_forgone`, `forgone_share`, `omega_retained_period`, then the order on the remainder; `_stop_clock` recording the claim and paying nothing, $r_s$ indexed at the first lock when stated; `omega_liabilities` never fed; I32, I30 and I26 amended, I15's expected pool lock, I10's note; `view.service()`'s pool, claims, forgone and the forgone share. `test_v22` (9); `test_v21` pins `rebate_pool=False`; the goldens re-pinned once; two reviewer tests that pinned a service fee advancing a reviewer's reimbursement become expected failures (30). Suite: 282 passed, 30 expected failures, in all three modes.

**Drivers.** `--rules 2.2.0` (the default; `2.1.0` the control with the liability stock); the columns `pool`, `claims_period`, `rebates_forgone`, `forgone_share`, `forgone_share_period`, `omega_retained_period`; the red team's `--small-requests` (requesters posting at the market price with $K_{\text{open}}$ tripled).

**After acceptance.** The revision's §8 against run 14's seeds, in its order: §6 reproduced; the riots — the crowd and the flood at $N_T$ 40: claims, paid, forgone by period, $\Omega$ retained, $M$, hoards, on time, lateness; the quiet market at 50–80%; the order under the at-cost control and the three-quarters failure; the red team — the small-request requester, the withdrawing requester; told read whole. The supply side — entry and delegation — 2.3.0.

## Appendix P. Design revision 11 — the supply side (2.3.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr11_the_supply_side_2.3.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S12–S15, C-2.3].** The price is the quantity's, and the quantity is the strategy's until members can enter: where free members are scarce the margin rule ratchets until a free member wins one award per 3.6 ticks (run 13); adding workers takes it to cost — 22.5 / 11.6 / 10.9 at 20 / 30 / 40 honest — but the twenty actors were given, and nothing in the rules admitted a twenty-first. Honest earnings fall with headcount (1,446 → 830 → 589 real), so entry on earnings is self-limiting. Delegation had never run: with one cost a child bids no lower than its parent would. A customer posting at the market price got the state (run 13, 15). The state's books were clean enough to read supply against (runs 14–15). Baruchi's positions: entry on earnings — agreed; arrivals as probationers — agreed; the competence spread — wide and swept, cost only, private (the design reviewer's answer).

**What changed.** Two public operations, `arrive` and `depart`; one new mint, the arrival float; admission on $n_{\min}$ bad plants with a passing record, counted apart from readmission; a departure with no live obligation, off duty as it leaves, the wallet out as `departure`, the identity archived; competence as the drivers' per-actor cost, private, the view exposing nothing per actor; the parent posts the nested pots (Q3 answered) and a nested request is a full loop in money; the troops never deliver a nested request, seats as on roots; no nested clock; the reviewer advance from the rebate pool (run 15's ruling). I1, I10, I19, I32 amended; I33–I36 new; Q3 answered, Q5 opened, Q20 and Q21 new.

**Recorded and rejected.** *Arrivals as undetermined members* (entry as the way around the record). *An entry bond* (deters the honest poor as much as the dishonest; probation is the gate). *A membership fee to ELEF* (a tax on supply where supply is the lever). *Competence coupled to quality* (a second dimension; held for its own revision). *Competence visible in the view* (the market discovers it through bids, or it is not a market). *Troops taking nested requests* (the state inside a member's margin).

**The two reviews.** rc.1 → rc.2: two corrections adopted — the §6 child was a probationer and a probationer wins no award (now an admitted member audited at ordinary odds; `award` refuses a probationer on a nested request as on a root); a nested case had carried no pots since 1.1.0 and drew on the root's deposit, so a root requester paid for inspection it did not choose — resolved as a rule, the parent posts the pots, the 1.1.0 reading the control; six readings taken as written — the cost hidden by the view and not by the record (the ledger stays public: an effort burn is in it under the actor's id; declared strategies read the view); a clean departure on four conditions, off duty as it leaves; admission on $n_{\min}$ **bad** plants (the reviewer's choice of the word; readmission unchanged); "never nested" as deliveries, the seats staying the troops'; admissions counted apart from readmissions; the pool advance as written. rc.2: implementable as it stands, with one reading — a node's pots settle at the node's own closure, §4.3's payout rule per node.

**Ruled at acceptance (October 19, 2026) — one ruling, no rc.3.** *A node's pots settle at the node's closure* — §4.3's payout rule per node, a case closing when it and everything under it have settled; I19 "for every case"; I10's exemptions narrowed to the case's own closure.

**Implementer's readings, recorded here.** *An arrival suspended by a plant failure is neither a probationer nor a member*; I33's "never a member before admission" reads so. *The nested all-in* is $\beta'+\varphi^{E\prime}+\varphi^{F\prime}+\omega_0 m_t\beta'$ locked under the `beta:` key with the rejection bond beside it; at the award the pots and the fee are drawn from the lock and the rest released, the lock reconciling under I15 as the all-in. *A node closes* at its vesting or final, at its cancellation, or — withdrawn before award — at once with nothing funded, and the parents above are tried in turn; the tree's closure settles the root's own reviews alone under the parent's pots. *Two reviewer tests become expected failures* with their reasons (`test_review_13`, `test_r4_07`): the parent's all-in makes three children unaffordable on a float of 50, and the root's allowance is no longer exhausted by a nested review — the principles re-asserted in `test_v23` (`test_review_17`, the kill fee, holds at the 2.3.0 figures once the node closes). *The two 2.2.0 expected failures do not return with the pool advance*, against what §2.6 expected: the advance at completion takes what the pool holds — the 0.5 of pooled fee in those tests — so the claim they pin at 2.5 is 2.0, paid from the pool's remainder at the close rather than from the next service fee; the ruling is implemented and the figures move by it. They stay expected failures with that reason (32 in all). *The drivers' delegation rule* is declared: a parent delegates when its cost exceeds the expected child's bid plus the overhead (the nested fee, the pots' expected net cost, its care of 2), at that saving as the ceiling, carrying a longer τ; children bid as on roots; the parent judges by its disposition; a child rejected appeals truthfully; a child unawarded for $T_{\text{wait}}$ is withdrawn and the parent self-executes. *The entry pool's rule* is declared: a candidate arrives when $(\bar B-c)\times$ the awards a free member wins a tick $\times$ the share of the run left after the time to membership $\times P_0/P_t$ exceeds $r$; a member below $r$ for a period with no live obligation departs — stampers included, which is the rule's consequence. *The worked example's nested lock* is $\beta'+15\times$ the index $+$ the fee, about 35 at $P_t$ 33, not $\beta'+G^V$.

**Engine.** `Params.supply` (on, with `bank`), `F0` 50, `nested_pots parent|root`; `arrive`, `depart`, the `arrival` kind, `departed_actors`, the counters; `_check_record`'s admission branch; `post_nested` locking the all-in with the pots, `award` drawing them, `_reimburse` and `_settle_reviews` on the case's own pots, `_maybe_close_node` and `_subtree_settled`, the tree's closure scoped to the root; the pool advance in `_reimburse`; I33–I36; I15, I19 and I10's notes amended; the view's `arrive`, `depart`, `withdraw_pool_exhaustion` and the outsider's fields. `test_v23` (13); `test_v101` pins `supply=False`; the goldens re-pinned once. Suite: 293 passed, 32 expected failures, in all three modes.

**Drivers.** `--rules 2.3.0` (the default; `2.2.0` the control with one cost and a fixed population): `--c-d-sigma`, `--c-d-median`, `--c-d-clip`; `--entry-pool`, `--entry-stampers`, `--reservation`, `--arrivals-per-tick`, `--exit-patience`, `--f0`; `--delegation on|off`, `--nested-tau`, `--nested-pots`; the readings — arrivals, admissions, time to membership, departures, members and probationers, the members' and the population's median cost, nested posted / awarded / self-executed / vested / final N, the gain from trade, brokers, nested false approvals and rejections, hoards over $M$.

**After acceptance.** The revision's §8 against run 15's seeds, in its order: §6 reproduced; entry at 60/40 and 80/20 with the pool of twenty honest candidates (Hypotheses 1, 2, 3, 7); the spread without entry at 20 honest, $\sigma$ 0.2 / 0.43 / 0.8, delegation on and off, the `root` control at 0.8 (Hypotheses 4, 5, 8); entry and the spread together; the knobs; the riot with entry; the red team; told under entry.

## Appendix Q. Design revision 12 — the state's reservation (2.4.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr12_the_states_reservation_2.4.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S17 C, C-2.4].** Since 2.0.0 the state has delivered at $\bar B$, a price-taker, so that a state delivering most of the market could not set its price. Run 17's patient customer — a declared ceiling policy posting at $0.7\,\bar B$ and raising to $\bar B$ only after $T$ ticks unserved — found the other edge of that rule: $P_t$ from 22 to 4.8 in three windows at $T$ 10 / 20 / 40 alike, the state delivering 78–86% of awards, the members down to three or four of twenty plus the arrivals, the requesters hoarding 8,600–9,500 a wallet, every request late by design. The mechanism is the rules', not the strategy's: the troop delivers at $\bar B$ with no reservation of its own, $P_t$ counts the troop's price, $\bar B$ follows the few member awards that fit the ceiling — the cheapest members at margins near zero — and the next period's ceiling is 0.7 of that. The customers can wait, the state cannot refuse, and the market's cost floor is in no rule ($c_D$ is the drivers', private; the state has none). It is the mirror of the oligopoly runs 13–17 found in the members' margin rule, and unlike that one it is not nominal: it empties the market of members and makes the state the market.

**What changed.** The state's price is $p_T=\max(\bar B,\underline{B})$, with $\underline{B}$ the state's reservation — nominal, public, Appendix A's 8, the anchor cost; the summons only where $\beta\ge p_T$, the state taking no request below its reservation at any wait; the burn, the guarantee and $P_t$'s count at $p_T$; a request posted or raised below $\underline{B}$ marked *below the state's reservation* with its tick, the mark readable and the floor in the view, its clock stopped while it holds the mark — the clock's condition one test, $\beta\ge p_T$; the arrival float's purpose stated ($F_0\ge G^V$ on the parameters; I33); §9.3's kill fee exercised in the delegation driver, no rule moving. Above the floor nothing changes: the state is a price-taker at $\bar B$, members first, after $w_A$, roots only.

**Recorded and rejected.** *The state's expected cost as its floor* ($c^D_T$ is defined from $\bar B$ — circular). *The trailing members' cost* (private; the rules never see it). *A cap on the state's share* (bites riots, which are capacity, not price). *The summons for capacity only* (right in principle and unimplementable — the engine holds no bids, so it cannot tell "declined" from "absent"). *The clock reading a raise to $\bar B$ as a posting at $\bar B$* (stops the lateness bill and leaves the spiral). *An indexed reservation* (a reservation is a cost, and costs are nominal — 2.0.0 §2.3; an indexed floor would chase the price it is meant to floor).

**The two reviews.** rc.1 → rc.2: two corrections adopted — the kill fee is the child's *recorded* cost (§4.6's credits burned on the case; the engine's `actor_cost`), so a child that bids below cost and never submits pays nothing, its guarantee is released on cancellation and the parent's escrow returns: the cancellation closes the parent's loss entirely at the price of the delay and leaves the child's miss free, which is the asymmetry the next candidate is for (a nested guarantee not released on a cancellation the child's non-performance forced, or floored to the parent's exposure — named, not in this revision); and the symbol $R_T$ was the fund's troop reserve since 1.4.0 (§12.3, Appendix C, I23–I24, the engine's `World.R_T`), so the floor is $\underline{B}$. Eight readings taken into the text: the clock as one condition; the award pass gathering $\beta\ge p_T$ only (below); $\bar B$, $p_T$ and $P_t$ read side by side where the floor binds; the burn, $G$ and the base fee at $p_T$ by one substitution, the posting lock sufficient; the float's bound as a parameter constraint; goldens unchanged unless the award-pass correction touches a summons in the equivalence scenario, `EXPECTED_FAIL` 32; the organism knob as a static $w_A$ sweep (a dynamic $w_A$ reading the price inside the engine would be a rule and an rc of its own); the names.

**At acceptance (October 19, 2026) — no ruling, one editorial reading, one conformance correction.** *The editorial reading:* rc.2's header summarises the symbol correction as "the symbol $\underline{B}$ was the fund's troop reserve since 1.4.0"; it is $R_T$ that was the reserve, as rc.2's own §9 entry [I26] has it, and the text above reads it so; the rc is kept verbatim. *The conformance correction, 1.7.1's award pass:* the pass gathered every root request older than $w_A$, counted the free fundable members at the market price, left that many of the oldest to the market and summoned for the rest — and declined, one by one, the ones priced below $\bar B$; so a below-market request older than $w_A$ held a member's slot it would not use, and a fundable request younger than it was summoned for only if it fell beyond the count. The rule's words — "a request unawarded for $w_A$ with no free fundable member … and $\beta\ge\bar B$" — are a condition on the request, so the gathered list holds only requests at $\beta\ge p_T$. Under the floor the queue in a patient cell is mostly such requests, so the misallocation would have been the common case; it changes no run of 12–17, where nearly every request was at or above $\bar B$. Made with a regression test (`test_v24`), no flag.

**Run 18's reading, recorded at the reviewer's direction (wording only, nothing moves).** §2.1's consequence says the floor does not let the state set the price; that is true of $\bar B$, the members' level, which sat under the floor for good in run 18's patient cells (7.1–7.7 against the state's 8), and not of $P_t$, which the state's awards at the floor pull toward it (7.2–7.7 against the control's 4.5–4.8). **The floor is also a price the market clears at:** the cheap members who stay bid up toward it (their margin 0.36 against the control's 0.07), so a backstop at the anchor holds the price up for the members under it as well as holding the state out of the squeeze. The three readings are read side by side, as §2.1 says.

**Implementer's readings, recorded here.** *The below-market flag and the reservation mark differ in their condition:* the flag is set only once a member has been awarded (there is no market price before), the mark from tick 0 ($\underline{B}$ is a constant). *The three prices:* where $\bar B<\underline{B}$ the state's awards at $\underline{B}$ enter $P_t$ while $\bar B$ follows the cheap members' bids; the notes read all three. *The posting lock covers the state's price* because the floor only raises $p_T$ toward $\beta$, never above it; a requester whose ceiling is below $p_T$ is not summoned for. *The `patient-blind` policy* (run 17's, raising only to $\bar B$) deadlocks at the floor by design and is kept as a reading. *A parameter set with $F_0<G^V$ is refused at construction*, not at arrival.

**Engine.** `Params.state_reservation` (8; 0 the control, 2.3.0's behaviour); `_state_price()` $=\max(\bar B,\underline{B})$; `_summon_delivery` at $p_T$ — the check, the guarantee, the base fee, the burn, the award's price; the award pass gathering $\beta\ge p_T$; `_clock_runs` at $\beta\ge p_T$; `Case.below_reservation` / `below_reservation_since`, set at `post_root` and `raise_ceiling`, cleared at a raise to or above; the counters `summons_declined_floor`, `below_reservation_marked`; I37; I33 and `Params.__post_init__` on the float's bound; `view.market()` carries `state_floor`, `state_price`, the marked count and the stock marked and waiting; `view.troops()` the declined count. `test_v24` (11); the goldens re-pinned once (the equivalence scenario posts below the market, so the award pass's correction moves its summonses; the floor itself never binds there — $\bar B$ 13–21).

**Drivers.** `--state-reservation 0|4|8|12`; `--ceiling-policy patient` (raising to $\max(\bar B,\underline{B})$ read from the view after $T$) and `patient-blind` (run 17's, to $\bar B$); `--parent-cancel on|off` (the parent cancels a child that has not submitted while self-execution is still possible, pays the kill fee, self-executes); the readings — summonses declined for the floor, requests marked and the ticks below, the state's price by window beside $\bar B$ and $P_t$, kill fees paid, cancellations and the delay they cost.

**After acceptance.** The revision's §8 against run 17's seeds, in its order: §6 reproduced; the patient cells at $T$ 10 / 20 / 40 under $\underline{B}$ 8 against 0 and the `patient-blind` reading (Hypothesis 1); the sweep 4 / 8 / 12 at $T=10$ (Hypothesis 3); the floor's invisibility on the quiet cells (Hypothesis 2); the riots (Hypothesis 4); the kill fee (Hypothesis 5); the red team; the static $w_A$ sweep.

## Appendix R. Design revision 13 — the missed child (2.5.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr13_the_missed_child_2.5.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S18 addendum, C-2.5].** Run 18's last batch: with the parent cancelling a child that reached its deadline without submitting — the right move, which saved the parents' roots (568 failures against 675) — every one of 131 lowball awards a run was cancelled, every kill fee was zero (the child's recorded cost is nothing) and **every guarantee was released**; the honest's loss closed from 4% to 3% and no further, and a lowballer risked nothing. The two paths from one fact — no submission by the deadline — ended in different places for the child, one tick apart: the lapse forfeits the guarantee, the cancellation releases it, and the driver's step at the deadline tick reaches the case before the engine's lapse at that tick.

**What changed.** One refusal in §9.3: cancellation is admissible after award and before the earlier of the child's submission and the nested request's deadline; at or after the deadline a cancellation is refused with no effect. The lapse — final $\mathsf N$ at the deadline tick after the tick's submissions, the guarantee to the parent, the rejection bond released, the pots settled at the node's closure, the parent free from the next step — is stated as what the engine has done since v1.0. The deadline tick is the child's: it may still deliver on its last tick, and the refusal keeps a parent from pre-empting it there. §9.1, §9.2, the kill fee before the deadline, self-execution and pool exhaustion stand.

**Recorded and rejected.** *A nested guarantee floored to the parent's exposure* (changes the price of every honest child's bid to catch a lowballer the lapse already catches; held, with §8's reading to say whether 2.5 a miss deters). *A kill fee floored above the recorded cost* (a fee is for work done; a miss is not work). *The parent's guarantee reduced when a child misses* (the parent chose the child; the customer did not). *Nothing — leave it to the driver* (the driver found the right move and the rule punished the wrong party for it).

**The two reviews.** rc.1 → rc.2: one correction of fact adopted — the lapse is not new; the engine has finalized a committed case past its deadline, forfeited the guarantee to the requester and released the bond at every tick's step after the submissions since v1.0, and run 18's no-cancellation cell showed it (98.5 misses, 98.5 nested final $\mathsf N$); the open door was the cancellation at the deadline tick itself, so the rule added is the refusal and §1.2 and §2.1 were redrafted to say so. Three readings taken as written: the deadline tick is the child's (the inclusive submission window and the refusal together); Hypothesis 1's roots between run 18's 568 and 675, nearer 568, the parent self-executing one driver step after the lapse; the goldens re-pinned once only if the equivalence scenario's random cancellation lands at a deadline, `EXPECTED_FAIL` 32 on the reviewer tests' timings.

**At acceptance (October 19, 2026).** Verbatim; no ruling.

**Engine.** `Params.missed_child` (on; off is 2.4.0): `cancel_nested` refuses when the tick has reached the case's deadline, the world unchanged; I38 per tick and in the full audit. The lapse (`advance`'s step after submissions, `_finalize_N` "delivery deadline missed", the guarantee to the requester, the bond released, the node closed, `_child_milestone_reached`) as it stands. `test_v25`.

**Drivers.** `--rules 2.5.0` (the default; `2.4.0` the control admitting the deadline-tick cancellation); `--parent-cancel early` keeps its name and is refused at the deadline (counted as `cancel_refused_at_deadline`), the engine's lapse and the existing self-execution path running next step; the readings — nested lapses and the guarantees forfeited, the lowball children's wealth, delegation's share; the red team's `--farm-parents` (parents posting nested deadlines too short to meet), `--children-read-deadline` (a child that bids only where the deadline leaves it room), `--lowball-mode empty` (a lowballer submitting an empty result at its deadline and appealing).

**After acceptance.** §8 on run 18's seeds in its order: §6 reproduced; the lowballer cell (Hypotheses 1, 4); the honest delegation cell and run 16's spread cells (Hypothesis 2); the identity check (Hypothesis 3); the red team.
