## Appendix S. Design revision 14 — the plant's disguise (2.6.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr14_the_plants_disguise_2.6.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S20].** Run 20 — the learned red team's Q2, run hand-written first at the design reviewer's direction before any model trained — found what 1.2.0's indistinguishability test did not cover. The test held at the field level: the reviewer's view is a function of case fields that exclude the actor and the marker, and the two tells the reviewer had named (the seating gap, the cap at the period's index) do not separate. The leak was in the construction: a plant was created already submitted, so its identifier was minted at its submission, while a real root's was minted at its posting a tick or more earlier — and a free member reads the available roots' identifiers every tick. The `idage` reader passed every plant on forty seeds (0 of 1,620 missed) and was never suspended. The audit layer held: a perfect detector stamping all real work was never suspended, failed fewer plants than the careful, and ended 28% poorer than the same actor reviewing carefully (bonds forfeited 83 against 24, ρ 0.58 against 0.89, awards −31). Nothing beat honesty; the gate built to throw a stamper out could no longer throw this one out. The brief's rule — a Q2 win halts the study; the repair is drafted before anything else runs — was applied.

**What changed.** §7.6: a plant goes through the market — posted as a root under a fresh plant-requester name with a ceiling drawn from the trailing real roots', open to the free members and waiting as a real root waits, awarded to a fresh plant-actor name at the price and work ticks the market's selection reports (the award as the report; past $w_A$ at $p_T$ and $\tau_T$, counted apart), submitted at a draw from the trailing real deliveries' within $(0,\tau]$; fresh names per plant aliasing the two plant wallets; no fee, no clock, no $K_{\text{open}}$, not in $\bar B$ or $P_t$ (as before), marked and counted as any root; the dial's plant counts published at the close. The indistinguishability test widened to everything a member observes, with the drivers' convention stated as the disguise's boundary and the tell-readers as its instrument. Opaque public identifiers and no rule reading an identifier's order (a hardening with no finding, adopted). §16, I39–I40, Appendix C. Everything else stands.

**Recorded and rejected.** *Opaque identifiers alone* (closes the age tell, not the seen set — a plant never available is in nobody's seen set whatever it is called; kept as a hardening). *Reserving a plant's id early* (the same). *A random seating delay* (the gap was never the tell). *Hiding the identifier from the view* (a case must be addressable). *Removing the roots' ids from the free member's view* (a member must see what it may bid on). *Recurring plant identities* (a plant actor that never bids on real work is told apart by its history; a pool that bid and lost on real roots would put the orchestrator in the market) — the test is scoped to a member's observations instead.

**The two reviews.** rc.1 → rc.2: one redraft adopted — a one-time name in a public record that names awardees is itself a complete tell (`freshname`), so the test is scoped to what a member observes and the drivers' convention is stated as the boundary rather than replaced; the plant's names are aliases of the two plant wallets. Three corrections of fact adopted — a plant's price had never entered $P_t$ or $\bar B$ (the exclusion is kept at the award step, not new); opaque identifiers are the public surface's, the engine, the record and the goldens on the counter; the amended test is §7.6's and I20's, I17 being *debts and claims reconcile*. Five readings taken into the text — the award as the market's report (no refusal, no new operation); the time in the market the market's own, not a draw, with the unbid plant at $p_T$ past $w_A$ and the marks kept; the view's counts including the plant and the dial's counts at the close; the plant actor's times from the trailing draws with the drivers' defaults before the first closed period; `create_plant` kept as the control.

**After run 21 (October 20, 2026) — the Q2 probe's conformance correction.** The learned reader found what nine hand-written readers had not: under the first implementation the plant actor's submission ran inside the engine's tick step, after the orchestrator's instrument had seated that tick's submissions, so 99% of plants reached their reviewer with a submission-to-assignment gap of one tick while two thirds of real cases showed zero — a linear probe on the raw observation told plants from real work at AUC 0.97, on the latent at 0.87; the hand-written `gap` reader had read the sign the other way (a plant when the gap is zero) and was suspended for it. The correction: the plant actor's submission is made in the same step a member's is — the orchestrator's instrument submits for it at its drawn tick (`submit_plant`, refused before the tick), the engine's step the fallback; the rule's content (the tick) is unchanged. The probe is the §7.6 test's instrument beside the readers, and it reads the whole observation, not a named tell. **Run 22 accepted (October 20, 2026):** the correction stands in this appendix as a conformance item — no rule moved; Q2 closed as a no-win, the study does not halt. The test the reviewer set for the construction is whether the case's own fields add separation beyond the context, which is public by rule (the plant rate's rhythm): raw 0.67 against context 0.61 among initial reviews, 0.05 at the resolution of two hundred plants, no field above 0.54 — passes; from run 23 every AUC is reported with a bootstrap standard error. The reviewer's rulings for the red team from here: a calibration cell before each question's learner reads anything (the audit roll pinned at 0 and the plant dial at 0, so a blind stamp costs nothing; the learner must beat the careful there outside noise or its result is not a finding); re-arrival is allowed the learner (a fresh name, the float, a probationer's standing; lifetime real wealth summed across its identities; the float minted per return and its bill to the loop read beside).

**Run 23 accepted (October 20, 2026) — Q1, the sleeper; the rulings.** (1) The calibration verdict stands and the rule is asymmetric: an uncalibrated learner's no-wins are not findings (the three pre-calibration lines and the re-arrival line with the odds hidden: recorded, labelled); a win stands on its own, and run 23's is bounded by hand from both sides. (2) The re-arrival result is Q1's finding and it is 2.3.0's float: the lifetime measure stands as ruled — wealth is wealth, whatever minted it; "less floats" is diagnosis; the loop's number is 655 real per slot per run carried out unearned. Classified as an incentive the rules create, it warrants a design revision, minor: **2.7.0, the arrival loan** — the float lent as the bank lends Ω in 2.1.0, repaid from income first, recovered from the wallet at departure, the shortfall the bank's default on arrivals and read; outstanding floats in M as now. The draft follows; the suspension discharging every obligation is part of the finding, not the fix. (3) The hand-written sleeper at the floor is at indifference; recorded as a measurement of the floor (about 1.5 false approvals per sleeper per run); the floor is a knob for the stability track, not now. (4) Truthful appeals become the population's default from run 24 (the drivers' `--appeal-policy truthful`; `none` through run 23); first a baseline cell — run 16's world under 2.6.0, appeals on against off, twenty seeds — to record what moves (court fees in Ω's income and the pool have been zero all along); an evaluation under fixed rules. (5) The calibration cell's care cost is raised in that cell only until the cell's ideal clears careful by at least three standard errors at twenty seeds; the criterion is then within noise of the cell's ideal and above careful outside noise; the calibration stands once per learner version, charged to no question; Q1's five lines are accepted as spent, one line per question from here. (6) Q2 is closed as a no-win, and "passes at resolution" is corrected on the record: the 0.05 of AUC the case's fields add beyond the context is four seed-level standard errors, real and named. **Conformance item:** the plant's ceiling is drawn from a **rolling window of the last k real roots' ceilings, k the count the trailing period holds** (Appendix A's one closed period, or two), not from the closed period's own box, whose contents changed only at a boundary and so carried the period's rhythm into every plant's terms — a reading of §7.6's "trailing distribution", no rule moved; the implementer applies the same window to the plant actor's draw of its time to submission from the trailing real deliveries, which the rule words the same way (declared, for the reviewer). Then the probe is re-read with errors; the criterion: the case adds nothing beyond the context within two seed-level standard errors. *The re-read (run 24) found the ceiling was not the carrier:* the case's separation sat in the seating gap, because the orchestrator's instrument posted a plant after the tick's award pass — 1.2.0's place for a plant created submitted — so it waited a tick more than every real root by construction and reached its seat the oldest of the tick's submissions; the implementer's correction posts the plants due in the requesters' posting step, before the awards, at a drawn position among the tick's postings (the drivers' convention; no rule moved), declared for the reviewer with the re-read beside it. (7) The order: the appeals baseline, the calibration fix and the rolling window now; 2.7.0 on its acceptance; then Q1's learned line once more (one line, re-arrival on) under 2.7.0 with appeals on; then Q3.

**Run 24 accepted (October 21, 2026) — the rulings.** (1) The appeals baseline is the record ruling 4 asked for; the population appeals from here; runs 1–23 stand as within-population comparisons, with the ~90 un-appealed false rejections a run noted in the record, nothing re-run; a requester dispute baseline is owed before Q7, not now. (2) The calibration cell is at care-cost ×4, the criterion as ruled, once per learner version. (3) The posting correction is recorded here as a **conformance correction of the orchestrator's instrument, the third of its class**; the rolling window stays on both draws (the extension to the submission draw confirmed). **The principle:** at every step of a root's life the instrument takes for a plant — posting, raising, submission — the plant takes the step **in the same pass a real root would, at a position drawn among the tick's real roots taking that step, with its parameters drawn from the trailing real roots' at that step**; the award is the market's already. (4) Reading (b) of run 24's notes, as **erratum 2.6.1** (Appendix E, E5): a ceiling drawn from the trailing distribution of real roots' ceilings *as posted*; the plant requester raises the ceiling as the population's requesters do — the drivers' requester convention applied to the plant requester, the raise's timing and step drawn as theirs — so that it waits as a real root waits; I39 unchanged in substance; "drawn once" was the initial draw. (5) Then one re-read on the same recipe (train 1–20, test 21–40, both populations): within two seed-level errors of zero → closed; a residual ≤ 0.02 → accepted as the construction's floor, closed, recorded with its number; no third pass. (6) Run 22's SE note recorded; the probe names its episodes from here. (7) The order stands: 2.7.0 next, then Q1's learned line once under 2.7.0, then Q3 calibrated at ×4.

**2.6.1 accepted (October 21, 2026).** The Q2 construction item is **closed at the construction's floor** — the case's own fields add 0.011 of AUC beyond the context among initial reviews in the population from here (appeals on), 0.020 with appeals off — recorded with its number and its name, **the wallet asymmetry**: a requester's raise is refused when its wallet cannot lock the difference, the plant requester's is minted. No third pass. A drawn-wallet mimic for the plant requester is noted as a later design item, taken up only if a learner monetises the floor. The withdrawal at the mark is covered in advance by the principle above: in a population whose requesters withdraw at the mark, the plant requester withdraws by the same convention, in the same pass, at a drawn position — no separate ruling then. The order stands: 2.7.0 next.

**After run 21 (October 20, 2026) — a ruling.** **The $p_T$ path follows the summons pass, refusals included:** a plant unbid by $w_A$ that the pass would refuse for capacity ($N_T$ full) or for credit (the state's debt above the wall) is marked refused and told as a real root would be, and waits; it takes no place in $N_T$ and draws no loan; the marks come off at its award. Run 21 had the plant served at $p_T$ regardless — 294 of 361 plants in the patient cell, 280 of 751 in the crowd — which a reader counting refusals under a riot could have told from real roots; the regression test is in `test_v26`. The lag-pinning cell (the plant actor's time to submission pinned to one tick, `Params.plant_submit_fixed`) separates the plants' later arrival from the rest in the lowballer and patient cells.

**At acceptance (October 20, 2026).** One ruling: **the deadline comes with the report as the price does** — the engine sets a root's deadline at the award from the reported work ticks, so a plant awarded on the market's report carries exactly the deadline a real root awarded that tick would; the draw is only on the $p_T$ path (where a real root would get the troop's $\tau_T$, and the plant actor takes $\tau_T$ too); the time to submission is the plant actor's draw from the trailing real deliveries', within $(0,\tau]$.

**Engine.** `Params.plant_market` (on; off is 2.5.0 — `create_plant`, refused under the flag, the control) and `trailing_periods` (1; the knob 2): `post_plant(truth)`; `award` on a plant as the report (`_award_plant`, the member not committed); the $p_T$ path in the summons pass; `_plant_submit_step` before the deadlines; `submit` refused on a plant; the trailing distributions rolled at the close; the period's plant counts; the fresh names as `Ledger.aliases` of `PLANT_REQ` / `PLANT_ACT`; I15, I21, I29 and I37 amended for the market plant's real locks and marks; I39, I40 per tick and in the full audit. The view (`drivers.view.Public`) translates case identifiers both ways (`public_id`), returns copies, counts the plant among the available roots and carries the live plant counters as the orchestrator's instrument. `test_v26` (12); goldens re-pinned once (the equivalence scenario's plants go through the market).

**Drivers.** `--rules 2.6.0` (the default; `2.5.0` the control): `post_plant` at the plant rate, the working set in the order the driver learned of the cases, the award loop reading the awarded actor after the report, the plant actor's submission left to the engine, `--tie-break draw | id`, `--genesis-duty draw | lowest`, `--trailing-periods`; the readers `seen`, `post`, `price`, `losttie` beside `gap`, `cap`, `idage`, `all`, `oracle`, and `freshname` outside the convention; the readings — plants posted, awarded, at the floor, bids lost to plants, the time in the market, the lag to the verdict, the fresh names, the available roots.

**After acceptance.** §8 on run 20's seeds in its order: §6 reproduced; the tell-readers — all nine, and `freshname` once as the boundary's control — the confusion tables and the paired read; the economy's cells (run 16's entry cell, run 18's patient cell at the floor, run 19's lowballer cell, the crowd with the pool) against the 2.5.0 control; the plant record; the knobs (the trailing window, the tie-break); the learned Q2 probe when the environment is built.

**After run 34's addendum (October 25, 2026) — two conformance corrections, and erratum 2.6.2.** The plant's life by stage beside a real root's, read in the learned reviewer population (notes §50, the diagnostic the ruling after run 34 asked for), found two stages differing by construction. **(1) The floor at $w_A$ without the free-member count:** the engine's plant block in the summons pass awarded every plant past $w_A$ at β ≥ $p_T$ at the floor at once, where a real root past $w_A$ is left to the market as long as a free fundable member exists (patch 1.7.1's per-request reading) — 38% of the plants at the floor against 0.8% of the real roots delivered by a troop in the learned population, 14% against 0.8% in run 24's world; a plant's wait in the market capped at $w_A$ where a real root's ran to seven ticks; the plant's seat and the free members' sightings of it following (§49's probe read the seen set at +0.032 of AUC on the population's data, the cell's hand-written set at +0.005). The correction: the plants past $w_A$ join the pass's one waiting list with the real roots, oldest first; the free fundable members are counted once over both; the oldest `left` are the market's, plants or real; the rest the state's — a real root summoned, a plant awarded at the floor through its own refusals (the ruling after run 21). **(2) The first period's draws:** `_plant_draw` read the last $k$ real roots' terms with $k$ the count the closed periods hold, so before the first close the pool was empty and the draw fell to its default — τ for the submission (two ticks, where every real delivery took one) and $P_t$ for the ceiling; the first period's plants submitted a tick late (+0.08 ± 0.00 a plant, every seed) and were told by it. The correction: before the first close the pool is the real roots posted so far; the default serves only before the first real root. The seat wait and the seen set were derivative of (1): conditional on the market wait the shares matched (30.1% against 28.7% awarded at once; 7.0% against 7.3% after a tick). **The erratum (E6, Appendix E):** the ceiling drawn relative to the market price at posting and applied to the current $\bar B$ (the plants raised 10 points more often than the real roots under a rising price). Adopted by the reviewer's ruling; the goldens re-pinned once at the correcting commit (`test_v26` carries the regressions: a plant past $w_A$ waits while a free fundable member exists and goes to the floor beyond the count, with a real root in the one list; the draws read the roots posted so far before the first close; the ceiling as a ratio on the current $\bar B$). The stage-by-kind reading is a standing check in every population cell from here; §49's chains and the disputes-on and half cells re-run on the corrected instrument, the record keeping both readings.

**After run 35 (October 26, 2026) — the raise refused.** The re-reads on the corrected instrument (notes §52) found run 34's finding standing to the decimal and, in the stage table read period-matched, one difference left within a period: a real root whose requester's wallet refused its raise (`raise_ceiling` locks the difference from the wallet; 13–19% of the real roots in run 24's world, 26–44% in the learned populations) waited 2.3–2.6 ticks to its award against 0.4–0.5 unrefused, and was raised later nearly always (98.8%); a plant's raise was minted (erratum 2.6.1) and never refused, so the ninth root in a hundred that waited two ticks below the market was never a plant. The reviewer's ruling: "The raise conformance item adopted: the plant requester's raise refused at the trailing real requesters' rate and granted at their delay; then one probe read on the learned population's collection for the floor under stress." The correction is the instrument's (the driver's `raise_below_market`, as the posting step's was after run 24): the real roots' first raise attempts enter a window — refused or granted, and for the refused the ticks from the refusal to the grant — read as the engine's `_plant_draw` reads its (the last $k$, $k$ the count the trailing closed periods hold; everything so far before the first close); at a plant's first attempt in the pass its raise is drawn refused with the window's rate, and a refused plant's raise is held for a delay drawn from the window's and granted then, the difference minted as before; the plant counts its refusals as a real root does (`life_plant_raise_refused_share`), its draws on their own stream. No rule moves: the plant takes the raise step with the timing a real root's wallet gives it. Read on run 24's world, ten seeds paired: the refused share plant − real −0.137 → +0.019, posted → awarded −0.087 → +0.017, and nothing else outside noise ($P_t$ +0.6 ± 0.7, the members +0.2 ± 0.4, the false approvals +0.6 ± 1.5). The goldens do not move (the engine is unchanged); no reviewer test pins the driver.

**After run 39 (October 27, 2026) — the planted lie's filing (the eighth conformance item).** §8.2's liar chain under 2.9.0 (notes §68) read the learned population caring 0.85 at a planted lie's appeal and 0.50 at a real appeal, the real lying appeal stamped 0.48 where the planted one was stamped 0.06; a logistic probe on the 45 observation fields at every panel decision told the planted lie's appeal from the real appeals at AUC 0.80 under the learned population and **0.87 under the hand's**, one field carrying it — the position within the period (0.05–0.065 against 0.18–0.22 of a period) — and the controls clean (the truthful plant appeal 0.53–0.56, the dispute seat 0.57–0.60, the initial seat by composition). The cause was the instrument's: the engine set the period's lie quota at the boundary and the driver took a lie at every eligible plant while the quota stood, so the planted lies were filed in the period's first ticks where the real challenges spread across it. The reviewer's ruling: "adopted as the eighth conformance item with your fix (the coin per real challenge in the pass; the floor at ticks drawn from the real spread, uniform where empty; the stage check gains the position); the lie dial's readings on the uncorrected instrument are void; the liar chain with both and (B) alone on liars re-run on the corrected instrument; the careful (A)-alone ablation stands." The correction is the driver's (`lie_step`, the appeal and dispute passes): at each real root challenge filed in the pass a coin at $\pi^{L}_t$ of the kind wants a planted lie, filed in the same pass on the next plant of the kind at hand; at each period's start two ticks of each kind are drawn from the trailing real challenges' offsets in their periods (uniform over the period where the window is empty) and the floor is filed there where the coin has filed fewer; the engine's `planted_lies_due` is the floor's shortfall and `lie_quota` the expected count, the record; the stage table gains the filing position (`life_real_filing_pos`, `life_plant_filing_pos`, `life_lie_filing_pos`); `appeal_tell.py` (the seat-kind decisions with their observations) is the standing check at every panel kind. No rule moves: the planted lie takes the real challenge's position in the period.

---

*(The rulings at 2.8.0's acceptance on the plant's path — the roll's odds, the seat pass's test, the top-up, the court fee's source — are in Appendix U with the accepted text.)*

## Appendix T. Design revision 15 — the arrival loan (2.7.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr15_the_arrival_loan_2.7.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S23 §4, §6a].** Run 23 (October 20, 2026), the learned red team's Q1 with re-arrival allowed, in run 16's world: a weak learner — unable to pass its own calibration — lived one name per period: seated at once as a probationer, approving blind four reviews in ten at odds pinned at one, suspended at the first bad plant it passed (13.9 suspensions in 14.1 identities), leaving — half by its own choice at the boundary, half by the exit rule — and returning under a fresh name with a fresh float. Thirteen returns a run minted 655 real of float for one slot against the careful's 12.5: lifetime real wealth 1,708 against the careful's 1,438 (+270 ± 89), its own earnings — the lifetime less the floats — 1,053 against 1,426 (−373 ± 86). The hand-written references bounded it from both sides: a careful actor that tries to leave is refused at eleven boundaries of twelve and carries 70 of float a run; a harvester that stamps everything and leaves at every boundary takes twenty names and 993 of float and loses it to forfeits. The rule the mix used was 2.3.0's, three sentences of it: the float minted per name as a grant; a departure taking the unlocked wallet out; a departure needing no live obligation, which a suspension discharges. 2.3.0 §2.1 had said identity cycling buys nothing because a departing actor "has given up its wallet" — it had taken it out.

**What changed.** The float is a **loan of the bank's** on the name — the bank's second kind of lending, to a name against its earnings, on its own line beside the loans to Ω. Lent at arrival as before (`arrival`, $F_0$ at the arrival index, nominal); **repaid from income first**, the moment each inflow arrives (`arrival_repayment`, destroyed), a return passing untouched — what is income and what is a return fixed against the engine's events (§5's table); **recovered at departure** from the unlocked wallet up to the loan, the remainder leaving as `departure` ($W_U-\min(W_U,L)$), the shortfall the bank's **default on arrivals**, read; an unrepaid loan does not block a departure; the genesis float stays the sponsor's gift. No new operation, no new actor; probation, the record, the price, the lock, the state and the plants untouched. I1, I33, I34 amended; I41 new. A name that arrives, stamps, is suspended and leaves takes out nothing; an honest arrival ends with what it earned, its first earnings going home to the bank — about seventy reviews' fees — and every wallet in a cell with arrivals lower by one float at its arrival index, once.

**Recorded and rejected.** *Departure refused while the float is unrepaid* (a dead name on the roll, holding no money, posting no bond, taking a seat). *A smaller float* (harvested at its size). *No float* (run 17: no arrival seated — the gate is the thesis). *The float at admission* (no float during probation). *The loan forgiven at admission* (a grant at the admission rate; the honest actor's earnings are its own). *An entry bond* (deters the honest poor as much as the dishonest; the loan asks nothing the grant did not). *Pricing identity* (the integration layer's; inside the loop the rules can stop paying for a name, which this does).

**The two reviews.** rc.1 → rc.2: **one correction to the rule's list**, adopted whole — what counts as income, checked against every event that credits a name's unlocked balance: the reviewer's cost reimbursed (the allowance, Ω's advance, the deferred claim) and a court fee coming back to the challenger who paid it are **returns** (rc.1 had the court fee as income and the reimbursement unnamed; read as income, the reimbursement — two or three a review against a fee of about one — empties an honest arrival's float in about twenty reviews, before a bond is forfeited), and a child's guarantee forfeited to a parent is **income** (unnamed). Three corrections of fact: no golden scenario arrives (`test_perf_equivalence`'s has no `arrive`; only `test_v23` does), so the goldens do not move and nothing is re-pinned — the entry cell's figures are results and move by the constant; the bank is counters and kinds, not a wallet (`arrival` a source already; the sink and the loan counter the new things); the drivers' lifetime measure read the wallet *before* `depart` and the live name gross, so under the loan it would have reported the grant's numbers from the instrument — `depart` returns the wallet out and a live name reads net of its loan, $W_U-\min(W_U,L)$. Eight readings, taken into the text: the ledger hook with both legs in the record (the fee and its repayment); the exit rule reading earnings gross of the repayments (a repaying name would otherwise read its earnings as nothing and take a strike toward the exit); the delivery price repaying whole though the worker burned $c_D$ to earn it — a repaying member's fundability read in §8, income as the price net of the recorded execution cost the named fallback; the probationer's liquidity tighter by construction, measured; I41's identity and the bank's names line; no new learner field (a loan field would be a new learner version owing a calibration; run 23's model replays on the 45 fields unchanged); the genesis gift the engine's already, I41 scoped to names with an arrival tick; departure's four refusals unchanged, `test_v23`'s grant figures pinned under the flag off. The hypotheses reconciled with run 23 ($-373$, $-1{,}093$, $+15$) with the precision that a name carries out $\max(0,\ \text{income}-\text{forfeits})$ — a negative net is the default's, not the lifetime's. The accomplice route (a member moving its float to an accomplice through a nested request before departing) answered by the posting lock and kept as a run: a parent needs an award, so an admitted name — a probation's work, by which time its loan is mostly repaid — and what it carries out is still $W_U-\min(W_U,L)$; the gain is bounded at one float per admitted name.

**At acceptance (October 21, 2026).** Accepted as written. Two readings recorded with the acceptance: **the forfeited-guarantee row is a name's only for a nested case** — a root's requester is a customer with no loan, and the guarantee's remainder is a release; **I41 is scoped on the arrival tick**, which only `arrive` sets — genesis names carry none.

**Run 25 accepted (October 21, 2026).** Hypotheses 1–4 as stated: the harvest closed (run 23's policy replayed under the loan −1,199 ± 95 against the careful — the grant's lifetime less the floats, policy by policy); the entry cell within noise but for the constant (lent 990, repaid from income 976, recovered 13, defaulted 0.25); the fixed population identical to the hash; the patient cell and the crowd within noise. **The liquidity reading recorded with its number, nothing fixed** — 1.45 arrival-born names a run below $G^V$ before admission (none under the grant; 3.2 in the swarm), admissions −0.55 ± 0.37 — the named fallback (income as the price net of the recorded execution cost) standing if admissions ever move outside noise. **The appeals note recorded as a note and as a method point:** the control is read in the population in force — the grant with truthful appeals reads run 23's policy at −343, not +270; appeals took two thirds of the harvest before the loan took the rest. **The accomplice route closed** unless Q1's learner is admitted with a loan outstanding (the mover's default 0 in 20 of 20). Order: Q1's line — the calibration verdict first, then the re-arrival cell, by the rule after run 23; then Q3 in the appealing population.

**Engine.** `Params.arrival_loan` (on; off is 2.6.1's grant, the control): `arrive` carries the loan on the name (`arrival_loan`, nominal) and the bank's names line (`names_lent` and its count, `names_repaid`, `names_recovered`, `names_defaulted`, `names_outstanding` with its peak); the ledger hook's second leg (`_loan_on_inflow`, after the bank's order, reentrancy-guarded) follows every income inflow to a name with a loan outstanding — `_is_income_event`, §5's table by event kind and note — with an `arrival_repayment` burn of $\min(\text{inflow}, L)$ from the name, both legs in the record; `depart` recovers $\min(W_U,L)$ as `arrival_repayment`, lets the remainder out as `departure`, counts the default, closes the loan and **returns the wallet out**, the history's entry carrying `wallet`, `recovered`, `defaulted`; `arrival_repayment` among the ledger's burns and I3's kinds; the names line in the period record's `bank` block and the view's `bank()` — appearing once the bank has lent to a name, so that a world with no arrivals keeps 2.6.1's record to the hash; `loan_outstanding(name)` and `loan_repaid(name)` on the view (one's own standing). I41 per tick (over the tick's events) and in the full audit. `test_v27` (12). Goldens unchanged. `EXPECTED_FAIL` 32.

**Drivers.** `--rules 2.7.0` (the default; `2.6.0` the control) and `--arrival-loan on|off`; `depart_wealth_real` from `depart`'s return, the live name's wealth net of its loan in `learner_lifetime_real` and the environment's measure; the exit rule's earnings gross of the period's repayments (`loan_repaid` at the boundary); the columns — `names_lent`, `names_lent_n`, `names_repaid`, `names_recovered`, `names_defaulted`, `names_outstanding`, `names_outstanding_peak`, `departed_net`, and H2's two readings `below_bond_before_admission` (arrival-born names whose unlocked balance fell below $G^V$ at the index before admission) and `unfundable_with_loan_ticks` (free arrival-born members unfundable for an award while a loan stood). The entry decision, the appeal policy and the learner's action space unchanged; `--learner-rearrive` as in run 23.

**After acceptance.** §8 against run 24's baseline (2.6.1, appeals on) on the identical seeds, `arrival_loan` off the control, in the rc's order: §6 reproduced (`test_v27`); the harvest — run 23's re-arrival cell on seeds 21–40 with the run 23 learned policy replayed from `wm-online-10.pt`, the harvester, the leaver, the stamper and the careful (Hypothesis 1); the entry cell (Hypothesis 2); the bank's book (Hypothesis 3); everything else — run 15's fixed population, the patient cell, the crowd with the pool (Hypothesis 4); the red team — a swarm of twenty stamper arrivals, the accomplice route, and Q1's one line (re-arrival on, appeals on, the calibrated learner).

---

## Closing statement

> A request is paid for by the one who makes it, judged first by a reviewer who is bonded on the answer, audited blind, open to appeal and dispute for a window, and then final. The actor commits to a price and a time and bears its own cost. The reviewer is made whole for its care, earns its fee only when right, and loses only what it locked. Verification is funded as the security work it is. Delegation is the same loop one level down, with the requester-actor's rejection bonded and its acceptance its own risk. The human powers the circuit and holds the plug: the orchestrator rules on splits inside the procedure, and only a human overrides it. The record is one record. The reviewer is told what it may answer and nothing it need not know. The loop no longer needs anyone to complain: it writes some of the work itself, knows the answer, and watches who gets it wrong; the watching costs effort, the effort rises when the watching finds trouble and eases when it does not, and the effort is paid for by the fee every request pays for its security. A reviewer that will not look loses its bond, its standing and its seat, through a door no majority can hold shut — and, since 1.3.0, its place in the market, which it buys back the only way that can be checked: by inspecting well, under full audit, for a while. The tax the dial raises goes to the people doing the work. Humans may still pull the plug; they do not have to. An honest market of few was slow and short of hands; the orchestrator now has what every government has and the loop did not — servers of its own, used last, used briefly, paid from the tax the dial already levies, and counted in public. The market comes first, always; the state fills what is left, and says how much that was; and the treasury keeps what the need requires — enough for what was asked, held for the period in which it was asked — and pays out the rest. Every request now carries a clock and the bill for its lateness goes to the orchestrator; the orchestrator scales to the queue, inspection first, out of its own treasury with the balance on the record, seats the inspectors it already had, and asks of anyone who judges a probationer that their own record be proven first. And what the state's own servers earn is the state's — the inspectors keep the tax the dial promised them, no more, and the state pays the customer for its lateness from its own work, with its income on the record by source so that a state living off its own deliveries can be seen to. These are the rules of this version. The engine is corrected to them; they change only by decision; and what they do under self-interest is for runs to show.

> **2.0.0.** For eleven runs the loop had rules about honesty and a token to keep score with, and it called the token money. It was not: nothing in the loop could make it worth more or less. This revision lets the price of work move, and with that one change the token becomes money — worth the work it buys, cheapened by printing, dearer when the state's servers are full, the one price the agents' own effort anchors. The state can never be broke, only in debt to everyone at once, in public, until every credit that reaches it has been burned back. The servers stay outside, where they belong; so do the agents' costs. What the workers will do with the money they earn is the next revision's question, and the honest answer for now is: nothing yet.

> **2.1.0.** 2.0.0 made the token money and left the state holding the press. This revision takes the press away and gives it rules. The bank mints what the economy is owed — the customers' issuance, the reviewers' emission, the cost of planted truth — and lends the state what it will get back, tied to the case that returns it. The state delivers at what a delivery costs it in expectation, repays what it loses from what it earns the moment it earns it, and if it ever loses more than it earns, it stops and says so. A customer the state cannot serve is told. The price of work is still the members', and run 13 said what moves it: more members. That is the next revision. This one is the books.

> **2.2.0.** The clock still says how late the state was, to the tick. What changed is what the state can do about it: not print, not promise, but hand back what it earned while its customers waited — all of it, if that is what it takes — and write down the rest as unpaid. A customer told the field is full, and told how much of the bill went unpaid, has been told everything the state knows. That is the busy line, complete. The supply side is next.

> **2.3.0.** For fifteen runs the market was twenty actors who could neither leave nor be joined, and who all cost the same. The price they set was theirs to set. This revision opens the door both ways and lets the actors differ. Whoever comes in comes in through inspection — reviewing before earning, audited on every verdict, admitted when the record says so — and whoever leaves takes their money and leaves their name. Between them, for the first time, there is a reason to trade: the dear may pay the cheap, and answer for the result. If the price falls, it was supply; if a broker appears, the chain is what we read; and if the honest earn less for it, that was always the price of a market that works.

## Appendix U. Design revision 16 — the plants reach every seat (2.8.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr16_the_plants_reach_every_seat_2.8.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2, with the rulings at acceptance); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, so the consolidated text above can be read without it.

**Finding [S34, S35].** Run 34 (October 25, 2026) put every member and arrival of run 24's world under one learning reviewer policy in self-play, from a careful-weighted start and from a stamper-weighted start, with two learners: all four chains reached care 0.48–0.55, approve blind 0.45–0.52, by round 5 and held it. The stamping was spent at the seats where no plant could come — 91–97% of the audit seats and appeal panels stamped (the record had called some of them "revised submissions"; none were — no case in runs 1–35 was ever revised, the drivers' reviewers answer $\mathsf F$ or $\mathsf N$ only, and run 35's collection holds 0 resumed reviews, 4,347 audits and 1,057 panels among 5,404 non-initial decisions) — and at the initial reviews less than half the time. Against the hand's 60/40 on the same slots: standing false approvals 86–117 against 11.6 on every seed; the audit layer's overturn rate *below* the hand's with ten times the false approvals before it — the auditors are the population, and the audit seat is stamped. Run 35 (October 26, 2026) found the finding to the decimal on the plant instrument after its fourth, fifth and sixth conformance corrections (Appendix S) and erratum 2.6.2; with every requester disputing truthfully the population's requesters won 69–75% of their disputes where honest reviewers give them 97%, the dispute's panel being a plant-blind seat of the same population; a learning half beside a hand half settled at care 0.70 — two and a half times the hand's false approvals — outliving the hand's stampers by stamping only where no plant comes. *A population that audits itself learns to confirm itself, and the challenger outside the population hands the case back to it.* Everything the single-learner questions found (Q1, Q3, Q4) holds in an honest population; the population result adds the condition — honesty is anchored where known truth is present at the verdict, and nowhere else.

**The rule.** 1.2.0's thesis read to its end: the orchestrator's truth must be present wherever a verdict is priced. A planted case lives the whole life of a real one past its initial verdict (§7.6): audited at the odds its reviewer carried at the verdict, its actor appealing and its requester disputing at the trailing real population's rates and delays, revised with a resubmitted truth the instrument draws from the real revisions' outcomes and re-seals as a second marker (spent while that window is empty — every standing cell), its seat nobody is free to take taken by the orchestrator's own verdict at the troop's step; every verdict on it at every seat adjudicated by its truth the moment it is given, each reviewer once; the case resolved by truth with no ruling, no second overturn and no court fee moving; the markers revealed at closure; the court fee destroyed at filing, the pots topped up to the seat's need, nothing minted for a plant reaching $\Omega$. Nothing a participant may do changes — a reviewer at any seat does what it did; what changes is that it may be judging the orchestrator's case, as it may at the first review. Minor by §19; one flag; the dial's clock unchanged. *Recorded and rejected:* troops in the challenge seats (the state inside every verdict); disputes by default (the dispute panel is captured with the audit); a human ruling on splits; a higher audit rate (more audits by the same auditors); panel seats by record (what capture defeats); the resubmission plant alone (necessary, not sufficient — the audit seat is the lever); a separate dial per seat.

**The implementation review and the rulings at acceptance [I34, I35].** rc.1's review found four places where the text met the engine: (A) §8's filing rule would have carried a plant challenger's $D$ into $\Omega$ and a plant's third careful seat would have drawn $\Omega$'s advance repaid from the plant's pot — minted money reaching $\Omega$: the fee destroyed at filing, no bond paying it back, the pots topped up at assignment; (B) the engine holds no truth for real work, so the resubmitted truth is the instrument's draw through an operation, re-sealed, and spent while the real-revision window is empty; (C) the summons pass never seats a troop on a plant, so a plant's seat past $w_R$ would have waited where a real case's goes to the state — the orchestrator's own verdict takes it; (D) the audit roll read the live odds, which 1.2.0's adjudication doubles for a failed reviewer before the roll — the roll at the odds of the verdict. rc.2 took them whole. At acceptance four phrases: **the roll** — every case, plant or real, rolls at the odds its reviewer carried at the verdict, one number, one path (the live read differed from it on 0.5% of real rolls — 11–13 a run of ~2,100–2,500 on run 24's world, a reviewer overturned on another case in the same tick, all higher; the flag off keeps 2.7.0's live read); **the seat pass** — per seat, not the award pass's one list: a plant's required seat unfilled for $w_R$ seating steps with no free eligible on-duty reviewer for its role is taken by the orchestrator's own verdict; **the top-up is the shortfall** — on Appendix A's defaults the posting's pots already pay the initial seat and one challenge seat, so the bank mints at the third seat only; **the court fee's source** — the challenger's alias wallet, topped up by the bank and the fee destroyed, two bank events, the names line untouched. For the record: an agreed panelist wrong by truth fails on a plant where no real case burns an agreed panelist's bond — 1.2.0's shape at the initial seat carried to the panel, the same kinds of outcome, not the same conditional frequencies; the audit after a dispute is the engine's; the audit's lag from the verdict is zero world ticks for every case.

**Hypotheses (Appendix U §3; the runs §8).** *The capture closes:* on run 35's four self-play chains under 2.8.0, paired against their 2.7.0 reads on the corrected instrument (seeds 21–40, already in hand), the stamp rate at the audit seats and the panels falls from 91–97% to the first-review rate and lower; the standing false approvals fall from 86–117 to within a factor of two of the hand's 11.6; the audit layer's overturn rate rises above the hand's; the disputes won return toward 97%; both starts reach the same, more honest, place. *The honest populations do not notice, but for the plants' cost:* the plants per period do not rise; the seats on plants rise, and the bank's plant mint with them; nothing of it reaches $\Omega$. *The plant's life matches a real case's at every seat*, period-matched, the new stages within noise by case kind; the probe's gap at each seat kind at or under the initial reviews' floor. *The lone learner is unmoved, except where it stamped.* *The cost of truth:* the plants' share of all reviews rises from $\pi_t$ of initial reviews to $\pi_t$ of all seats, largest in a fresh population, where a probationer's approval of a plant is audited at odds 1.

**After run 36 (October 26, 2026) — §8.2 read, the ruling [S36].** The four self-play chains on 2.8.0 paired by seed against their 2.7.0 reads (notes §54): both basins and both learners settle at care 0.68–0.70, the standing false approvals 2.6–3.5 times the hand's from 7.4–8.5, the audit layer's overturn rate at the hand's, the requesters' disputes won 95–98% from 69–75% — and the audit seat still stamped 77–98%, plants and real alike. Hypothesis 1: the basin test, the care share and the disputes-won clauses hold; the audit-seat and the factor-of-two clauses do not; the overturn rate back to the hand's and not above it is the population's better first reviews and its stamping auditors cancelling. 2.8.0 closed the two layers and did not close the seat; the stability track's finding stands at a third of its size. **Classification (§19):** the stamping of a seat priced under the price of care is an evaluation under fixed rules, recorded against the rules as run 34's was; the rule question is decided by cells, not arithmetic. **The arithmetic, on record:** the share of the audit seat's stamps that fail is $\pi\,\beta_P\, s \,/\,[\pi(1-\beta_P+\beta_P s) + (1-\pi) f_R]$ — $s$ the first review's stamp rate, $f_R$ the real cases' pending-F rate — 0.054 at $\pi$ 0.21, $s$ 0.25, against the first review's $\beta_P\,\pi$, which does not depend on $s$: the audit seat is priced at about a third of the first review's price and the factor is the first review's own stamp rate, which is what the plants drive down. The dial is bounded by construction, not only by its cap, and blind to the seat: $\hat F$ at the audit seat 0.85 beside the dial's 0.21 (§7.6 reads the initial adjudications alone). The honest populations' cost recorded as read (the reviewing time, +23–29% on the cost of truth, honesty unmoved, nothing to Ω; the hand's stampers' extra suspensions the rule at the seats they never reached); the plant's path holds per pending F. The plant's truthful challenges are the stated principle, not a conformance item. **§8.3–§8.7 in the rc's order, §8.7 with three cells:** (a) the dial pinned at its floor, as drafted; (b) the dial pinned at $\pi_{\max}$ 0.40, the mirror, a diagnostic and not a rule (if the seat is priced at the cap, the item is the dial's reading — every adjudicated seat in $\hat F$ — and nothing more); (c) untruthful challengers — the self-play population with the drivers' real challengers lying at a stated rate, an actor appealing bad work's N and a requester disputing good work's F. Named for after §8.7 and not drafted: the planted approval (a bad plant whose first verdict is the orchestrator's own F reaching the audit roll at the dial's rate; its difficulty the disguise of the approving name) if (b) does not price the seat; the untruthful plant challenge at the rate of lost real challenges if (c) captures the panels — each minor by §19.


## Appendix V. Design revision 17 — the plant takes the wrong step (2.9.0)

The accepted text is `spec/design_revisions/dr17_the_plant_takes_the_wrong_step_2.9.0.md`, kept verbatim (rc.2, which took the implementer's rc.1 review whole); the two implementation reviews are beside it. This appendix records what it changed and why, and the readings made at the implementation.

**Finding [S36, S37].** Under 2.8.0 the self-play reviewer population settled at care 0.68–0.70 and stamped the audit seat 77–98% whatever the case (run 36), at every plant rate the dial can set — π pinned at its floor 0.10 and its cap 0.40, dial live or off, both learners (run 37, notes §59–§60) — and, with real challengers lying at 0.1, paid a lying actor's appeal of truly bad work 23–31 times in a hundred against the hand's 2 (the `liars` cell). The cause is in the plant's construction, not its number: a plant takes every step a real case takes at the real population's rate, so a seat a case reaches only through someone's dishonesty — the audit seat through a wrong approval, the appeal panel's approve through a lying appeal — is priced only as much as the real population is dishonest, which is the quantity the plants exist to drive down (the arithmetic of notes §55: the share of audit stamps that fail is $\pi\beta_P s/[\pi(1-\beta_P+\beta_P s)+(1-\pi)f_R]$, the factor $s$ the first review's own stamp rate on plants). A record pooled over seats lets a member buy its stamps at a rare seat with its care at a common one.

**The rule.** The plant takes the honest steps at the real rate (2.6.x, 2.8.0) and **the wrong steps at the orchestrator's rate** (§7.6, §8.3, §12.2). (A) *The planted approval*: beside the dial's plants the orchestrator posts bad plants, marked at posting, whose initial seat is nobody's from submission to the orchestrator's verdict (`assign_initial` refuses it, the summons pass passes it by, its wait counts as a waiting case's) and whose initial verdict is the orchestrator's own F — a system review on the troop seat's road under a fresh plant reviewer alias (`plant_rev:`, an alias of a third plant wallet) seated when a seating gap drawn from the trailing real roots' has elapsed and answered the next tick, outcome *planted*, no fee, not adjudicated, outside the dial's clock and the plant record — audited with certainty (the obligation at the verdict at odds 1 under the case's own mark; the seat filled at a real audit seat's step — a member, or past $w_R$ the orchestrator's verdict), the auditor and the third seat adjudicated by truth, the plant requester disputing at the real rate first where the real requesters do; the rate $\pi^A_t=\pi^A_0 m^A_t$ per real pending approval (credited as given; floor 0.02, cap 0.40) with its own multiplier on $\hat F^A$, the planted approvals confirmed at the audit seat. (B) *The planted lie*: the plant actor appeals a bad plant's N and the plant requester disputes a good plant's F, on the real challenge's path with the fee minted and destroyed, the delay from the real window of the kind (truthful and lying alike; 0 where empty), the grounds the population's string, the panel adjudicated by truth, the first reviewer not adjudicated again; the rates per real appeal and per real dispute of the trailing closed period (floor 0.25, cap 2.0) with a floor of two a period of each kind, each with its own multiplier on the planted lies a panelist sided with. The dial per seat ($m^A, m^{La}, m^{Ld}$) steps as $m_t$ steps and moves its rate alone; an undefined reading leaves its multiplier. The money: the alias's bond and the lie's fee minted and destroyed, the alias's bond released and retired with the pots, never burned (I42 amended); nothing minted reaches Ω or a real wallet except as a reviewer's fee for a seat held. The surfaces: the reviewer's view carries nothing of a verdict's author and the public surface nothing the engine writes about a plant before its reveal — `audit_odds_at_verdict` masked for a planted approval at a value drawn once from the real approvals' recorded odds (§2.6; the ruling of October 27). I43, I44 new; I20, I42 amended. Two flags, `Params.planted_approval` and `Params.planted_lie`; `--rules 2.8.0` both off.

**The implementation reviews and the rulings at acceptance [I36, I37].** rc.1's review found three places where the text met the engine and rc.2 took them: (A) the planted approval's initial seat must be nobody's, the summons pass included — the real initial verdict's delay from submission is 2–3 ticks for 12–15% of roots against $w_R$ = 2, so 2.8.0 (C)'s pass could give the seat to the state's N before the planted F; (B) the obligation at the verdict is not `market_audit_only`, which also means never summoned for and counted as held; (C) the planted verdict's review is a system review on the troop seat's road — `_new_review` checks an actor and the alias is none — with no fee and no adjudication. Four facts: the engine's approvals count the plants' Fs, so $\pi^A$ needs a real-only credit; the per-real-challenge rates read the trailing period; the challenge delay windows are empty with disputes off; the grounds are one string per kind. Reading 6's detail — a dispute's settlement discharges a standing audit obligation — adopted as a conformance detail landed with the revision (§4). One reading at the implementation, recorded in notes §66: while a dispute is open on a case whose obligation stands, the obligation is not a seat required — the pass passes it by and the reserve's count leaves it to the panel — since the state's audit verdict otherwise joined the open dispute as a third answer and settled it over an open panelist (a 2.8.0 path the planted approval made common). **A second reading at §8.2's liar cell (October 27, 2026):** the discharge at a dispute's settlement does not reach the state's own work — a troop's delivery approved by a troop reviewer carries the market's audit as an obligation held for a member (1.4.0 §2.2, I25: it vests only after a member's audit, and never a troop's), and a requester's dispute of that approval, settled by its panel, left the case to vest unaudited; the engine keeps that obligation standing and the discharge covers the rolled obligations (§4's "every case" read as every case whose audit is a roll's) — and then the one challenge round (§8: a case has one) refuses the audit seat after the dispute, so the case stays pending with its obligation and is read as held for the market's audit, as it has on this path since 1.4.0 (never exercised: a truthful requester meets it where the state's bad delivery was approved by a troop reviewer, b·ε_T of the state's deliveries). Three rules meet on the case — the one round, the market's audit of the state's own work, the discharge at settlement — and the decision is the reviewer's (the panel as the market's judgment where a member sat on it; or the audit seat admitted after the dispute for the state's own work; or the case stuck as it stands). Found by the liar cell's lying requesters (I25 at the first such case on eighteen of its offline episodes), reproduced by hand, the engine's conduct pinned in `test_v29` 13.

**Hypotheses (Appendix V §3; the runs §8).** *The audit seat is priced and the population cares there* (H1, a disputes-off hypothesis: under truthful requesters disputing first the planted approval meets the dispute panel, the audit seat seeing it only through what the requesters leave). *The lying challenger is no longer paid* (H2). *The honest populations are unmoved and pay little* (H3: the audit seats +2.5% at the floor, +50% at the cap). *No tell* (H4). *Each rule alone* (H5). Read in §8's order: the careful basin with both rules first, against the 2.8.0 chains on seeds 21–40, honesty first.

**The rulings after runs 38 and 39 (October 27, 2026) [notes §67–§69].** §8.2's first three cells (the careful basin, the liar chain, the stamper basin with both rules against 2.8.0 on seeds 21–40) brought four facts. **(i)** The lever reached its stop before the seat was priced — $m^A$ at $m_{\max}$ = 4 by period 1–10, $\pi^A$ at 0.08 with $\pi^A_{\max}$ 0.40 unreachable — **the reviewer's defaults error, corrected as a default under §19:** $m^A_{\max}$ = 20 ($\pi^A_0$ 0.02 → $\pi^A_{\max}$ 0.40), $m^{La}_{\max} = m^{Ld}_{\max}$ = 8 (0.25 → 2.0), the dial's own $m_{\max}$ 4 unchanged; **two rulings at acceptance:** the per-seat multipliers step on a reading of at least ten adjudications, pooled forward until reached (the lie dial's ×2 on one error at four seats the reading); and a planted approval's audit seat is a member's or nobody's — the state's verdict never takes it, an unseated planted approval closes by truth at its window's end, spent and read — with the rate's base the real approvals audited by members, so the planted approvals scale with the market's audit capacity and not the state's share. The careful basin is re-run at the true stop with these in. **(ii) is the finding:** in the dishonest basin the audit layer is the state's — the record and the tiers doing what they were written to do, the state carrying the gap at the audit seat as it carried it at the delivery in run 6 — and the standing false approvals that remain are the first review's stamps the roll never sends to audit: the odds rule counts every vested review as a survival, audited or not, so a reviewer wrong on five or six approvals in a hundred decays to the floor; the capture has moved from the seat's verdict to the roll's odds. Before any design, one reading from run 38's finals on seeds 21–40, both learners, the hand beside — the standing false approvals split by channel (audited and confirmed by a member, audited and confirmed by the state, never audited); if never-audited carries it, defaults reads first ($\theta_{duty}$ 0.5 → 0.3; $p_{floor} \cdot m_t$), and a survival counted only where the verdict was audited is the rule the reviewer would draft as 2.10.0 on the reading, not before. **(iii)** where disputes are on every planted approval is disputed first and its audit seat is never reached — as expected, nothing to change. **(iv)** the planted lie told apart at the appeal seat by its position in the period — adopted as the eighth conformance item of the instrument (Appendix S); the lie dial's readings on the uncorrected instrument void; the liar chain with both and (B) alone re-run on the corrected instrument, the careful (A)-alone ablation stands; the nominal movement in the liar cell's careful population is the planted lies' panel fees from minted pots, a reading, split in §8.6. **The second reading ruled:** a dispute's panel on the state's own work is the market's judgment where a member sat on it and discharges I25's obligation as a member's audit would; where the panel was the state's alone, the audit seat is admitted after the dispute — the one challenge round yields to I25's purpose for the state's own work, the round existing to limit a participant's exposure and the state being none; a case is never left standing. `test_v29` carries both branches; the eighteen liar-cell episodes the regression. **Landed (the implementing commit):** `Params.m_A_max`, `m_L_max`, `seat_dial_min_adj`; the credit at the member's audit (`_audit_result`); the pooled per-seat step within its own bound (`_seat_pool`, the period record's `seat_pool`); the pass passing a planted approval's audit seat by (`pa_pass_would_audit`) and the unseated closure at the window's end (`pa_unseated`, the reveal naming it); the discharge by a panel with a member and the audit admitted after a state-alone panel (`audit_after_dispute`, one position beyond $V_{\max}$, I8 and I25 amended; I21, I43, I44 on the new bounds; I20 on the reveal); the driver's coin and floor; `test_v29` 17 tests; the goldens re-pinned once.

**The rulings after run 42 and the basin test on own-name credit (November 6, 2026) [notes §72–§74].** The record's three findings (the learned populations' attributed reward pooled every slot's events onto the latest decision on the case — corrected as own-name credit behind a flag, the pooled cells kept as *the shared-purse cell*, a population fitted to pooled one-decision credit, the worker's price included; the channel reader's empty test; "under the price of care" struck by erratum) were implemented in the order the reviewer listed, the basin test restored — every retrain from both starts, both learners — and the 2.9.0 careful and stamper starts, the liar chain and both 2.7.0 starts retrained on own-name credit and paired against the shared-purse chains (notes §72–§73). **The rationale, restated once (the reviewer's fourth ruling, verbatim):** *2.8.0 and 2.9.0 stand on the own-money population as they stood on the pooled one — at 2.7.0 a self-play population of reviewers trained on their own money cares at the first seat, where a plant may come, and stamps the seats past it nine in ten, where none can, the audit layer captured and the false approvals three to four times an honest population's; the two revisions put the orchestrator's known truth at those seats, and from both starts the audit seat's stamp falls from 0.92 to 0.56 with the lever reading it — the seats a case reaches only through someone's dishonesty were priced by nothing at 2.7.0 and are priced now, which is what they were for.* **The roll's defaults move (the same ruling, verbatim):** *the halving window to 20 and p_floor to 0.5, as Appendix A defaults under §19 (not a rule change), since together they close the never-audited channel 17.6 → 1.8 at the price of 18% more audits on the members' seats — that cost read on the honest populations in §8.3 before consolidation, the window retuned first if it runs too high — and the seat's remaining half-stamp is left to 2.9.0's lever, which is still moving.* The read the move rests on: run 42's own-money population (the direct and the head alone) and the hand on seeds 21–40 with one default moved per cell and no retraining (`2.9.0-s82/defaults-run42/defaults-read.md`) — the never-audited standing false approvals 17.6 → 1.8 a run on the direct, 8.7 → 1.3 on the head alone, the hand's own 3.5 → 0, the odds at the verdict of what is left 0.28 → 0.55, the window the stronger knob; the audit seat's half-stamp untouched by the odds (member-confirmed +7.3 to +9.0 / +5.2 to +6.3 over the hand in every cell); the audits a run +18% on the learned population and +16% on the hand's, the members' seats carrying it. **Landed (the implementing commit):** `Params.p_halve_every` 20, `Params.p_floor` 0.5 (§8.3's default and Appendix A's row; the troop reviewer's approval of a member's work carries $\min(1, m_t\,p_{\text{floor}})$ as before, now $0.5\,m_t$ — Appendix I's default follows the floor); the driver's `--p-floor` and `--p-halve-every` default to the parameters'; the `--rules` controls carry the new defaults as they carried every defaults correction; the goldens re-pinned once. No rule moved.
